# Vaadin Spreadsheet — Systematic Refactor Working Log

> **Purpose.** Companion to `LEGACY_REFACTOR_PIPELINE.md`. The pipeline document
> is the *methodology* (read-mostly). This file is the *journal* of one concrete
> execution of that methodology against the Flow port of Vaadin Spreadsheet — what
> was found, what was decided, what is blocked, and what we learned about the
> pipeline itself that should feed back into a refined, language-agnostic process.

**Repo:** `vaadin/flow-components` · **Branch / pinned commit:** `main` @
`bccb70fdefa9209d9052676903cccf29a3832aad` · **Date opened:** 2026-06-04 ·
**Operator (human):** Ugur · **Agent:** Claude Opus 4.7 (1M ctx)

> **History note.** The Spreadsheet component was first developed in
> [`vaadin/spreadsheet`](https://github.com/vaadin/spreadsheet) (Vaadin-8 / GWT
> add-on) and **later migrated** into `flow-components`. `git log` in
> `flow-components` therefore only shows the post-migration history. For any
> historical question — bug fixes, original intent, the why behind a comment,
> per-class churn for slice picking, edge cases from past incidents — consult
> `vaadin/spreadsheet` as well. The legacy code is mostly that repo's, with
> Flow-specific glue added on top here.

---

## 0. How to read this file

- Numbered sections are the persistent state of the project — overwrite in place
  as understanding deepens.
- The Activity log at the bottom is append-only; it's the timeline of what
  changed when, so you can reconstruct *why* the persistent state looks like it
  does.
- Anything load-bearing for the methodology refinement goes in §7.

---

| `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-parent/vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-client` | `com.vaadin:vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-client:25.3-SNAPSHOT` | `jar` | **GWT** module compiled to `spreadsheet-export.js` (Java→JS) |

| `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-parent/vaadin-spreadsheet-testbench` | `com.vaadin:vaadin-spreadsheet-testbench:25.3-SNAPSHOT` | `jar` | TestBench element classes (`SpreadsheetElement`, `SheetCellElement`, `SheetHeaderElement`, `AddressUtil`) |

**Profile gating.** The parent POM activates `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-client`
only when `-DskipGwt` is *not* set, and activates the IT module only when

### 1.2 Build / test / dev-UI commands (verified for this checkout)

| Goal | Command |
|---|---|
| Build the Java component (skip GWT, skip tests) | `mvn -am -pl vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-parent/vaadin-spreadsheet-flow -DskipGwt -DskipTests install` |
| Build everything including GWT client → JS | `mvn -am -pl vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-parent install -DskipTests` |
| Run unit tests (Java) | `mvn -am -pl vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-parent/vaadin-spreadsheet-flow test -DskipGwt` |
| Run a single unit-test class | `… -Dtest='SpreadsheetTest'` |
| Run integration tests | `mvn verify -am -pl vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-parent/vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-integration-tests -DskipUnitTests` |
| Run a single IT method | `… -Dit.test='UndoRedoIT#someMethod*'` (trailing `*` is mandatory) |
| Start dev UI (Jetty) for manual / Playwright testing | `mvn package jetty:run -Dvaadin.pnpm.enable -Dvaadin.frontend.hotdeploy=true -am -B -q -DskipTests -pl vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-parent/vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-integration-tests` |
| Stop dev UI cleanly | `mvn jetty:stop -pl vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-parent/vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-integration-tests` (don't use `TaskStop`; it leaks the forked Jetty JVM) |

### 1.3 Server-side classes — full inventory

**God class.** `Spreadsheet` — **6,224 LOC**, `com.vaadin.flow.component.spreadsheet.Spreadsheet`. Extends `com.vaadin.flow.component.Component`, implements `HasSize`, `HasStyle`, `Action.Container`. Wired to the client via `@Tag("vaadin-spreadsheet")` + `@JsModule("./vaadin-spreadsheet/vaadin-spreadsheet.js")`.

**Managers (each holds a `Spreadsheet spreadsheet` back-reference).**

| Class | LOC | Constructor | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CellValueManager` | 1,476 | `(Spreadsheet)` | Cell-value cache, serialization, edit pipeline |
| `SpreadsheetStyleFactory` | 1,107 | `(Spreadsheet)` | POI cell-style → CSS conversion |
| `ConditionalFormatter` | 883 | `(Spreadsheet)` | Conditional formatting evaluation/rendering |
| `CellSelectionShifter` | 890 | `(Spreadsheet)` | Selection drag / fill / shift cells |
| `CellSelectionManager` | 843 | `(Spreadsheet)` | Current cell + range selection state |
| `ContextMenuManager` | 339 | `(Spreadsheet)` | Action-handler-driven context menu |
| `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` | 247 | `(Spreadsheet)` | Undo/redo command stack |

**Factories / handlers.**

| Class | LOC | Construction | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SpreadsheetFactory` | 1,163 | static methods | Workbook load/save, lifecycle |
| `SpreadsheetHandlerImpl` | 519 | `(Spreadsheet)` | Decodes the `spreadsheet-event` DOM event into legacy `SpreadsheetServerRpc` calls |
| `SpreadsheetComponentFactory` | (interface) | — | Pluggable embedded-component factory |
| `DefaultHyperlinkCellClickHandler` | — | `(Spreadsheet[, …])` | Default hyperlink-cell click behavior |
| `SpreadsheetDefaultActionHandler` | — | — | Context-menu action handler |

**Other server-side classes worth knowing.**

- **Commands (undo/redo unit of work).** `command/`: `Command` (iface), `SpreadsheetCommand`, `CellValueCommand`, `CellShiftValuesCommand`, `ValueChangeCommand`, `RowInsertOrDeleteCommand`, `SizeChangeCommand`, `RowData`.
- **Actions (context-menu).** `action/`: 11 classes, all implementing `SpreadsheetAction`.
- **Chart subsystem.** `charts/converter/...`: ~30 classes for POI chart → Vaadin Chart data model translation.
- **Shared / client DTOs.** `client/`: `CellData`, `MergedRegion`, `MergedRegionUtil`, `OverlayInfo`, `SpreadsheetActionDetails`, `SpreadsheetHandler` (RPC iface). `shared/`: `SpreadsheetState`, `PopupButtonState`, `GroupingData`, `ContentMode`, `ErrorLevel`, `URLReference`. `rpc/`: `SpreadsheetClientRpc`, `SpreadsheetServerRpc`, `PopupButtonClientRpc`, `PopupButtonServerRpc`. **The `client/`, `shared/`, and `rpc/` packages are legacy Vaadin-8 SharedState/RPC types preserved for the GWT client.**
- **Utility / value object.** `Serializer`, `SpreadsheetUtil`, `SheetState`, `SheetImageWrapper`, `SheetChartWrapper`, `SheetOverlayWrapper`, `MinimizableComponentContainer`, `PopupButton`, `SpreadsheetTable`, `SpreadsheetFilterTable`, `CustomDataFormatter`, `CellValueFormatter`, `FormulaFormatter`, `GroupingUtil`, `NamedRangeUtils`, `IteratorChain`, `ItemFilter`, `CellSet`, `ColorConverter` (+ HSSF/XSSF concrete), `ColorConverterUtil`, `ChartCreator` (+ `DefaultChartCreator`).
- **POI extension.** `org/apache/poi/ss/formula/WorkbookEvaluatorUtil` — a single Java file that lives under the POI package to access package-private POI APIs.

**Class count.** 120 `.java` files under `src/main/java`.

### 1.4 POI touch-points

POI is used **everywhere the workbook model is read or written**. Identified as touching POI (imports `org.apache.poi.*`):

`CellSelectionManager`, `CellSelectionShifter`, `CellSet`, `CellValueManager`, `ChartCreator`, `ColorConverter`, `ConditionalFormatter`, `ContextMenuManager`, `CustomDataFormatter`, `DefaultHyperlinkCellClickHandler`, `GroupingUtil`, `HSSFColorConverter`, `ItemFilter`, `NamedRangeUtils`, `PopupButton`, `SheetChartWrapper`, `SheetImageWrapper`, `SheetOverlayWrapper`, `SheetState`, `Spreadsheet`, `SpreadsheetComponentFactory`, `SpreadsheetFactory`, `SpreadsheetFilterTable`, `SpreadsheetHandlerImpl`, `SpreadsheetHistoryManager`, `SpreadsheetStyleFactory`, `SpreadsheetTable`, `SpreadsheetUtil`, `XSSFColorConverter` (29 classes).

**POI-mutating classes** (call `setCellValue` / `createCell` / `setCellStyle` / `insertCell` / `removeRow` / `shift…` / `setRowHeight` / `removeMergedRegion` / etc.):

`CellSelectionManager`, `CellSelectionShifter`, `CellValueManager`, `ConditionalFormatter`, `DefaultHyperlinkCellClickHandler`, `GroupingUtil`, `NamedRangeUtils`, `SheetChartWrapper`, `Spreadsheet`, `SpreadsheetFactory`, `SpreadsheetHandlerImpl`, `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` (!), `SpreadsheetStyleFactory`, `SpreadsheetTable` (14 classes).

> `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` "mutates" POI only indirectly — by replaying `Command` objects that themselves call into the workbook. Worth keeping in mind: undo/redo is fundamentally a POI mutation through a stored intent, not a direct writer of cells.

### 1.5 Server↔client contract boundary — **FROZEN CONTRACT LIST**

> **Rule 2 of the methodology**: nothing on this list may change shape during a
> refactor. Any move that touches one is *not* a refactor.

**The Flow Spreadsheet client is a hybrid.** `<vaadin-spreadsheet>` is a Lit
element (`vaadin-spreadsheet.js`) that hosts a **GWT-compiled** widget loaded
from `spreadsheet-export.js` (produced by the `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-client`
module). The server↔client contract therefore has three observable surfaces:

#### 1.5.1 Outbound: element properties (server → client via `getElement().setProperty(...)`)

Each property is named below by its Lit attribute. The `vaadin-spreadsheet.js`
`updated()` method dispatches each name to a `this.api.set…` call on the GWT
widget. **Every property name and the Java type it carries is part of the
contract.**

| Property | Type | Java site (file:line) |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | String | `Spreadsheet.java:163` |
| `dirty` | Number (ms timestamp) | `Spreadsheet.java:3753` |
| `rowBufferSize` | int | 3512 |
| `columnBufferSize` | int | 3542 |
| `rows`, `cols` | int | 408, 413 |
| `colGroupingData`, `rowGroupingData` | List<GroupingData> JSON | 418, 423 |
| `colGroupingMax`, `rowGroupingMax` | int | 429, 434 |
| `colGroupingInversed`, `rowGroupingInversed` | bool | 439, 444 |
| `defRowH`, `defColW` | float / int | 449, 454 |
| `rowH`, `colW` | float[] / int[] (serialized) | 459, 464 |
| `reload` | long (timestamp pulse) | 469 |
| `sheetIndex` | int (1-based) | 474 |
| `sheetNames` | String[] | 479 |
| `cellStyleToCSSStyle` | Map<Integer,String> | 485 |
| `rowIndexToStyleIndex`, `columnIndexToStyleIndex` | Map<Integer,Integer> | 492, 499 |
| `lockedColumnIndexes`, `lockedRowIndexes` | Set<Integer> | 505, 511 |
| `shiftedCellBorderStyles` | List<String> | 517 |
| `conditionalFormattingStyles` | Map<…> | 524 |
| `hiddenColumnIndexes`, `hiddenRowIndexes` | Set<Integer> | 530, 536 |
| `verticalScrollPositions`, `horizontalScrollPositions` | int[] | 542, 548 |
| `sheetProtected`, `workbookProtected` | bool | 554, 558 |
| `cellKeysToEditorIdMap` | Map<String,String> | 568 |
| `componentIDtoCellKeysMap` | Map<String,String> | 579 |
| `hyperlinksTooltips` | Map<String,String> | 586 |
| `cellComments`, `cellCommentAuthors`, `visibleCellComments` | Maps | 592, 599, 605 |
| `invalidFormulaCells` | Set<String> | 611 |
| `hasActions` | bool | 616 |
| `overlays` | List<OverlayInfo> | 621 |
| `mergedRegions` | List<MergedRegion> | 626 |
| `displayGridlines`, `displayRowColHeadings` | bool | 631, 635 |
| `verticalSplitPosition`, `horizontalSplitPosition` | int | 641, 647 |
| `infoLabelValue` | String | 653 |
| `workbookChangeToggle` | bool (toggle) | 658 |
| `lockFormatColumns`, `lockFormatRows` | bool | 662, 666 |
| `namedRanges` | String | 670 |
| `appId` | String | 1211 |
| `showCustomEditorOnFocus` | bool | 1539 |
| `resources` (set via `executeJs("this.resources=$0", …)`) | Set<String> | 3617 |
| `invalidFormulaErrorMessage` | String | 5917 |

#### 1.5.2 Outbound: client-RPC method invocations (server → client via `getElement().callJsFunction(name, …args)`)

Each name corresponds to a method on `VaadinSpreadsheet` in `vaadin-spreadsheet.js`.

`updateBottomRightCellValues`, `updateTopLeftCellValues`, `updateTopRightCellValues`, `updateBottomLeftCellValues`, `updateFormulaBar` (via `executeJs`), `invalidCellAddress`, `showSelectedCell`, `showActions`, `setSelectedCellAndRange`, `cellsUpdated`, `refreshCellStyles`, `editCellComment`, `addPopupButton`, `removePopupButton`.

#### 1.5.3 Inbound: a single DOM event with a discriminated `detail.type` (client → server)

Java side (`Spreadsheet.java:705-`):

```java
@DomEvent("spreadsheet-event")
…SpreadsheetEvent(
    @EventData("event.detail.type") String type,
    @EventData("event.detail.data") JsonNode data)
```

`SpreadsheetHandlerImpl` then dispatches `type` to one of these legacy
`SpreadsheetServerRpc` methods — **the `type` strings ARE the contract**:

`groupingCollapsed`, `contextMenuClosed`, `levelHeaderClicked`, `onSheetScroll`, `sheetAddressChanged`, `cellSelected`, `cellRangeSelected`, `cellAddedToSelectionAndSelected`, `cellsAddedToRangeSelection`, `rowSelected`, `rowAddedToRangeSelection`, `columnSelected`, `columnAddedToSelection`, `selectionIncreasePainted`, `selectionDecreasePainted`, `cellValueEdited`, `sheetSelected`, `sheetRenamed`, `sheetCreated`, `cellRangePainted`, `deleteSelectedCells`, `linkCellClicked`, `rowsResized`, `columnResized`, `onRowAutofit`, `onColumnAutofit`, `onUndo`, `onRedo`, `setCellStyleWidthRatios`, `protectedCellWriteAttempted`, `onPaste`, `clearSelectedCellsOnCut`, `updateCellComment`, `onConnectorInit`, `contextMenuOpenOnSelection`, `actionOnCurrentSelection`, `rowHeaderContextMenuOpen`, `actionOnRowHeader`, `columnHeaderContextMenuOpen`, `actionOnColumnHeader`, `popupButtonClick`, `popupClose`.

#### 1.5.4 Implicit contract: the GWT widget itself

`spreadsheet-export.js` is a generated artifact produced by the GWT compiler in
the client module. The set of `this.api.set…` / `this.api.update…` methods that
`vaadin-spreadsheet.js` calls is the surface the GWT module is expected to
export. Refactoring server-side code cannot change this. Refactoring the GWT
client is **out of scope** for this initiative and would need its own pipeline.

- Relevant ITs for slice 2 (selection shift): `CellShiftingIT`, `RowShiftIT`, `CellShiftValuesUndoRedoIT`.

#### 1.6.3 Open questions about tests

- **Mutation testing (PIT)** isn't configured today. Per pipeline §4, we'll need it before declaring a slice "characterized."
- **JaCoCo** isn't configured today either. Same comment.
- The IT suite is heavy (~451 methods); we'll likely shard by feature when we run it.

### 1.7 Serializable

- All manager classes — `CellValueManager`, `CellSelectionManager`, `CellSelectionShifter`, `SpreadsheetHistoryManager`, `ContextMenuManager`, `SpreadsheetStyleFactory`, `ConditionalFormatter` — **explicitly `implements Serializable`** with `@SuppressWarnings("serial")`.
- `Spreadsheet` itself does not `implements Serializable` *directly*, but inherits it via `com.vaadin.flow.component.Component` and bears `@SuppressWarnings("serial")`. Flow may serialize component trees for session replication, so changes to instance fields (adding/removing, changing types) need to be evaluated for session-compat impact even if no `serialVersionUID` is set.
- Practical implication for refactors: when we extract an interface and change the **type** of a field on `Spreadsheet` or a manager (e.g. from `Spreadsheet` to `SomeNarrowInterface`), Java serialization treats this as a non-breaking change at the bytecode level only if the new type is itself serializable. **The extracted interface must extend `Serializable`** to keep the manager serializable end-to-end.

## 2. Proposed first vertical slice

**Recommendation: `SpreadsheetHistoryManager`** (over `CellSelectionShifter`).

### 2.1 Why this one, not the other

| Criterion | `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` | `CellSelectionShifter` |
|---|---|---|
| LOC | **247** | 890 |
| Distinct `Spreadsheet` members it touches | **5** + one pkg-private field | ~12 + heavy paths through `CellValueManager` & `SpreadsheetStyleFactory` |
| Touches the server↔client contract? | No (commands replay through Spreadsheet, but no direct setProperty/callJsFunction) | No directly, but reads `Workbook` via `getWorkbook()` and uses `getSpreadsheetStyleFactory().cellStyleUpdated(…)` |
| POI direct mutation | None (delegates to `Command.execute()`) | Yes — `Cell.setCell…`, `Row.createCell`, etc. |
| Coupling to other managers | Only `CellSelectionManager` (3 methods) | `CellSelectionManager`, `CellValueManager`, `SpreadsheetStyleFactory`, `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` |
| Has dedicated unit tests today? | No | No |
| IT coverage | `UndoRedoIT`, `CellShiftValuesUndoRedoIT` | `CellShiftingIT`, `RowShiftIT`, `CellShiftValuesUndoRedoIT` |
| Blast radius if we break it | Undo/redo silently misbehaves | Selection-drag / fill-down silently misbehaves *and* corrupts cell values |

`SpreadsheetHistoryManager` is the better starter slice: it is the smallest,
its surface to `Spreadsheet` is narrow and well-defined, it does not directly
mutate POI, and its failure mode is observable but does not corrupt user data.
`CellSelectionShifter` becomes a strong second slice once the pipeline is
exercised.

### 2.2 Members-used set (exact)

`SpreadsheetHistoryManager` touches exactly these `Spreadsheet` members:

| Member | Kind | Used at (Java line in `SpreadsheetHistoryManager.java`) |
|---|---|---|
| `getActiveSheetIndex()` | method (read) | 191 |
| `setActiveSheetIndex(int)` | method (write) | 193 |
| `initialSheetSelection` | **package-private field** (write) | 202 |
| `isRerenderPending()` | method (read) | 214 |
| `getCellSelectionManager()` | method (returns manager) | 220, 223, 230 |
| → then on `CellSelectionManager`: `onSheetAddressChanged(String, boolean)`, `handleCellRangeSelection(CellReference, CellRangeAddress, boolean)`, `reSelectSelectedCell()` | indirect | same lines |
| `fireEvent(ComponentEvent)` | method (inherited from `Component`) | 147 |

Plus one **static** call that is not on `Spreadsheet` at all and should not be
in the extracted interface:

- `SpreadsheetFactory.logMemoryUsage()` — lines 74, 82.

### 2.3 Proposed extracted interface

```java
package com.vaadin.flow.component.spreadsheet;

import java.io.Serializable;

import com.vaadin.flow.component.ComponentEvent;

/**
 * Narrow view of {@link Spreadsheet} that {@link SpreadsheetHistoryManager}
 * needs. Introduced to break the bidirectional coupling between the
 * Spreadsheet god class and its history manager.
 *
 * <p>This interface MUST extend {@link Serializable} so the manager keeps
 * its Vaadin-session serialization contract.
 */
interface SpreadsheetHistoryHost extends Serializable {

    int getActiveSheetIndex();

    void setActiveSheetIndex(int sheetIndex);

    boolean isRerenderPending();

    /**
     * Replaces the package-private field write
     * {@code spreadsheet.initialSheetSelection = …}.
     */
    void setInitialSheetSelection(String address);

    CellSelectionManager getCellSelectionManager();

    /**
     * Pass-through to {@code Component.fireEvent}. Exposed here so the
     * manager doesn't depend on the full Component API surface.
     */
    void fireEvent(ComponentEvent<?> event);
}
```

Notes / choices to confirm before extraction:

- **Package-private visibility** is intentional — this is an internal collaborator, not a published API.
- We could go narrower and inline the three `CellSelectionManager` methods into the host interface, but that hides a more honest dependency. Phase-1 keeps the existing collaboration.
- `setInitialSheetSelection` is a new public-facing method on `Spreadsheet`. Adding it does **not** change the server↔client contract; it only replaces a direct field write. The field stays where it is; the setter is a small wrapper. This is behavior-preserving.

### 2.4 Test plan (Stage 3–4 for this slice)

Before *any* code move, we put a characterization net in place. Order matters.

1. **Headless server-side unit test for `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` (new).** Build a real `Spreadsheet` over a small in-memory xlsx, drive it through public API (`new Spreadsheet()` → seed cells → mutate → assert against `getSpreadsheetHistoryManager()`). Capture:
   - `addCommand` / `clear` / `setHistorySize` semantics including the edge case `commands.size() > historySize` *not* incrementing `historyIndex` (this is a subtle bug-shape that the methodology calls out as the kind of thing to PIN, not fix).
   - `canUndo` / `canRedo` boundary states (-1, 0, max).
   - `undo` / `redo` happy path with a `ValueChangeCommand`, asserting cells reverted and `CellValueChangeEvent` was fired.
   - `undo` / `redo` with active-sheet swap (`makeSureCorrectSheetActive`) — assert `getActiveSheetIndex()` flips and `initialSheetSelection` is set.
   - `undo` / `redo` with paint range vs single-cell selection (`changeSelection`).
2. **Differential / golden-master payload capture (new).** Drive the same scenarios as #1 against a `Spreadsheet` whose `Element` has been replaced with a recording proxy. Snapshot the sequence of `setProperty(name, value)` / `callJsFunction(name, args)` invocations. These are the bytes the client would actually see; if they don't change under refactoring, the contract is preserved.
3. **Confirm relevant IT pass on `bccb70fdef`.** Run `UndoRedoIT` and `CellShiftValuesUndoRedoIT` in isolation. If either is red on baseline, we have a pre-existing flake to address before we declare the slice characterized.
4. **JaCoCo coverage gate (new).** Add JaCoCo to the unit-test run. Require ≥ 95 % line coverage on `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` from #1 before we touch the file.
5. **(Optional, recommended) PIT mutation gate.** Run PIT on `SpreadsheetHistoryManager`. A mutation score < ~80 % means the tests don't actually *detect* changes and we need more assertions before refactoring.

### 2.5 Refactor sequence (Stage 5 for this slice)

Each step is small and the suite must stay green between every one.

| Step | Action | Tool |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Add public `setInitialSheetSelection(String)` to `Spreadsheet` that writes the existing field. No callers yet. | IntelliJ: *Extract Method* on the `initialSheetSelection = …` write at `Spreadsheet.java:1591` and `3933/3935` is not quite right because there are several callers; alternative: write the setter manually, *Find Usages* shows callers, *Refactor → Inline Field* later. Default: hand-edit (one-line setter is safe; tests gate). |
| 2 | Replace `spreadsheet.initialSheetSelection = …` inside `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` with `setInitialSheetSelection(…)`. | Hand-edit. |
| 3 | Repeat step 2 for the other two writers: `DefaultHyperlinkCellClickHandler:141`, `NamedRangeUtils:147`. We are not refactoring those slices yet, but routing them through the setter is a strict no-op that prepares for later slices. | Hand-edit. |
| 4 | Introduce `SpreadsheetHistoryHost` interface as in §2.3, package-private, in the same package as `Spreadsheet`. | IntelliJ: *Extract Interface* from `Spreadsheet`, name `SpreadsheetHistoryHost`, **select only** the five methods listed in §2.2, **do not** check "rewrite usages to use interface". Place in the spreadsheet package. |
| 5 | Change `SpreadsheetHistoryManager`'s field type from `Spreadsheet` to `SpreadsheetHistoryHost`. Constructor signature stays `(Spreadsheet)` so Spreadsheet's `new SpreadsheetHistoryManager(this)` keeps compiling. | IntelliJ: *Refactor → Change Signature* on `protected final Spreadsheet spreadsheet` is awkward; instead use *Refactor → Type Migration* on the field. |
| 6 | Verify `Spreadsheet` `implements SpreadsheetHistoryHost` (the IDE refactor should have added this). | Diff review. |
| 7 | Run full suite + golden masters + IT subset. Commit. | — |

### 2.6 Definition of done (per pipeline §6)

- Characterization tests (§2.4 #1, #2) exist and pass against both `bccb70fdef` and the refactored tree.
- Differential check: the recorded payload streams from §2.4 #2 match byte-for-byte before/after.
- `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` accesses `Spreadsheet` *only* via `SpreadsheetHistoryHost`; the concrete `Spreadsheet` import has been removed except in transitive types (`CellSelectionManager`, `ComponentEvent`).
- No server↔client contract change.
- JaCoCo coverage on the file did not drop; PIT mutation score on the file did not drop.
- Refactor commits are isolated from any behavior changes.

## 3. Decisions log

| Date | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-06-04 | Use this file (`SPREADSHEET_REFACTOR_LOG.md`) as the working journal, leaving `LEGACY_REFACTOR_PIPELINE.md` as the methodology spec only. | Cleaner separation: spec vs execution. |
| 2026-06-04 | **First slice = `SpreadsheetHistoryManager`** (not `CellSelectionShifter`). | Smallest surface, no direct POI mutation, no contract overlap, narrow blast radius. See §2.1. |
| 2026-06-04 | The frozen contract list per pipeline rule #2 is §1.5 of this file. Anything touching it is *not* a refactor. | Pin the boundary explicitly. |
| 2026-06-04 | Extracted interface for slice 1 will be **package-private** named `SpreadsheetHistoryHost`. | It is an internal collaborator, not published API. |
| 2026-06-04 | Extracted interface **must `extends Serializable`**. | All managers and `Spreadsheet` are Vaadin-session-serializable; downgrading their fields' static type to a non-serializable interface would silently break session replication. |

## 3.2 Stage-1 enumeration of `Spreadsheet`'s distinct concerns

Conducted 2026-06-04 after Ugur expanded the scope ("refactor Spreadsheet too").
Pass over the 6,224-LOC `Spreadsheet.java`; 16 cohesive concerns identified,
each summarizable in one sentence. Ordered by suitability as the next slice
(safest / warmest first).

| # | Concern | ~LOC | POI touch | Contract touch | Target |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Viewport state (scroll bounds, buffer sizes) | 150 | none | low | **new** `SheetViewportState` |
| 2 | Cell comment loading / display | 200 | read-only | high | **new** `CellCommentManager` |
| 3 | Hyperlink loading + click dispatch | 150 | read-only | low | **new** `HyperlinkManager` |
| 4 | Custom-component lifecycle (embedded Vaadin components) | 300 | none | medium | **new** `CustomComponentManager` |
| 5 | PopupButton (table filter) lifecycle | 200 | none | low | **new** `PopupButtonManager` |
| 6 | Sheet overlay / image positioning | 250 | indirect | medium | **new** `OverlayManager` |
| 7 | Table registration + autofit tracking | 200 | indirect | low | **new** `TableManager` |
| 8 | Sheet visibility + protection toggles | 200 | narrow write | medium | **new** `SheetVisibilityManager` |
| 9 | Row/column hide/show | 200 | narrow write | medium | **new** `RowColumnVisibilityManager` |
| 10 | Freeze pane | 80 | narrow write | medium | **new** `FreezePaneManager` |
| 11 | Sheet navigation (active sheet + scroll preservation) | 250 | medium write | high | **new** `SheetNavigationManager` |
| 12 | Sheet metadata + reload orchestration | 200 | read-only | medium | **new** `SheetMetadataManager` |
| 13 | Cell merge / unmerge (`MergedRegionContainer` already inline) | 280 | medium write | high | **new** `MergedRegionManager` |
| 14 | Row/column sizing (heights, widths, autofit) | 450 | medium write | high | **new** `DimensionManager` |
| 15 | Cell deletion + deletion handlers | 200 | medium write | low | **expand** `CellValueManager` |
| 16 | Row/column grouping + outline (XSSF schema work) | 250 | heavy write | high | **new** `GroupingManager` (later) |

Total extractable: **~3,360 LOC** from `Spreadsheet`'s 6,224 LOC. That's ~54%
of the god class moved into focused helpers if all 16 concerns are extracted.
The remaining ~2,800 LOC is the genuine orchestration core (Lit element
wiring, constructor lifecycle, listener registration boilerplate, event
classes, license check) that probably stays in `Spreadsheet`.

The pipeline §5 heuristic says "leave the entangled heart for last." For
this codebase that's the **value + style + edit-cycle code**, which lives
across `CellValueManager` (1476 LOC), `SpreadsheetStyleFactory` (1107 LOC),
and the cell-edit paths in `Spreadsheet` and `CellSelectionShifter`. None
of the 16 concerns above are in that hot path.

`vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-parent/vaadin-spreadsheet-flow/target/site/jacoco/jacoco.xml`.

**`SpreadsheetHistoryManager.java`** (the slice-1 class):

| Counter | Covered | Missed | Total | % |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| INSTRUCTION | 209 | 30 | 239 | **87.4 %** |
| BRANCH | 21 | 5 | 26 | **80.8 %** |
| LINE | 62 | 10 | 72 | **86.1 %** |
| COMPLEXITY | 25 | 3 | 28 | **89.3 %** |
| METHOD | 15 | 0 | 15 | 100 % |
| CLASS | 1 | 0 | 1 | 100 % |

**`SpreadsheetHistoryHost.java`** (the new interface — JaCoCo attributes the implementor's covered methods here): 100 % across all counters.

**Hold-the-line rule for slice 2 and beyond:** future slices may not drop these numbers. Specifically, the slice-1 floor is **LINE 86.1 %, BRANCH 80.8 %** on `SpreadsheetHistoryManager.java`. The 10 uncovered lines + 5 uncovered branches are accounted for by the two deferred routing-consequence tests (see the test file's §7 block) plus a few edges in `setHistorySize` and `discardAllAfter` that aren't worth pinning without a recording proxy.

- **Block 2 — Test-license availability.** Pipeline §1 final bullet says working with this code and running IT requires commercial + TestBench licenses. Confirmed locally for unit tests (255/255 green). IT licenses not yet exercised; if they are missing on this machine we cannot use the IT suite as a gate.
- **Question 1 — Add JaCoCo + PIT to this module now?** They are not configured. Adding them is a small POM change that is *not* a refactor of production code, but it does change the build. Worth doing once globally so every slice can use the same gate.
- **Question 2 — Are we OK keeping the GWT client untouched?** Refactoring the GWT module is out of scope for this pipeline. The methodology assumed a TS/Lit client — that assumption is wrong for Flow Spreadsheet. Confirm we're explicitly **not** touching `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-client`.
- **Question 3 — Activity dataset for Stage 0 churn metrics.** We have not yet mined per-class churn from git history (pipeline §0 bullet 4). It is not needed for slice 1 (we already know HistoryManager is small & decoupled) but should be added when we broaden beyond slice 1, to pick subsequent slices by `coupling × churn × coverage`. **The mining will need to combine commits from both `vaadin/flow-components` (post-migration) and `vaadin/spreadsheet` (pre-migration).** Suggestion: clone `vaadin/spreadsheet` as a sibling checkout and run `git log --follow` against the *original* file paths there.

## 5. Risks specific to this codebase (above and beyond pipeline §3 stage 6)

- **`@SuppressWarnings("serial")` everywhere.** No `serialVersionUID` declarations. Field-shape changes (including type changes through interface extraction) can break Vaadin session replication silently. Mitigation: keep new interfaces serializable; if a future slice changes a *field's serialized name or default value*, audit explicitly.
- **Package-private field writes.** `spreadsheet.initialSheetSelection` is written from three sibling classes. Other managers may also write package-private state in the same way; a global grep is owed once we start the second slice. (Identified so far: only `initialSheetSelection`.)
- **`static` collaborators.** `SpreadsheetFactory.logMemoryUsage()` is called from `SpreadsheetHistoryManager`. Static calls fall outside the extract-interface approach. They are not part of the coupling we're trying to break, but they should be documented when we touch a class. (No action required for slice 1.)
- **Flow-specific dispatch through Spreadsheet (`@DomEvent`).** Any "dead" code reachable only from the `SpreadsheetServerRpc` interface implementation is statically dead-looking but actually called from the browser. Pipeline rule #5 applies — do not delete based on static analysis.

---

## 6. Improvement suggestions for the pipeline document

These are things we learned in Stage 0 that the methodology should incorporate
for the next codebase / next operator. Update this list as more comes up.

> **Status note (2026-06-04).** All §6.1–§6.3 items below have been folded into
> `LEGACY_REFACTOR_PIPELINE.md` — see its §9 revision-history entry. This
> section is preserved as the *record of what we learned*, separate from the
> normative methodology. Add new findings here when they surface; promote them
> into the pipeline doc once they've proven out.

### 6.1 Things that worked well

- **§2 discovery checklist** is precisely the right shape. Following it produced a usable Stage-0 report in under an hour of agent work without writing any code. The "produce a written report, *then stop*" gate is valuable — it forces an alignment moment before commitment.
- **The "frozen contract list" framing** (rule #2) is concrete and reviewable. Once you write it down, every later "is this a refactor?" question becomes mechanical.
- **The IDE-refactoring-first stance** in §4 is the right default for a codebase this size. Free-text edits are the dominant source of accidental behavior change in any human refactor.

### 6.2 Things the methodology got slightly wrong (Flow-specific surprises)

- **Pipeline §1 / §1.5 assume the Flow port has a TS/Lit client.** Vaadin Spreadsheet (Flow) actually still uses **GWT** behind the Lit shim. The "frozen contract list" therefore has three layers (element properties, `callJsFunction` names, `spreadsheet-event` discriminator strings), not two. → Suggested edit: §1 should say "client may be Lit, GWT-compiled, or hybrid; do not assume."
- **Pipeline §1 says "SharedState + RPC is gone in Flow."** Not in this module — the `client/`, `shared/`, `rpc/` packages of legacy Vaadin-8 types are preserved and used by the GWT widget; `SpreadsheetHandlerImpl implements SpreadsheetServerRpc`. → Suggested edit: §1 should note "legacy SharedState/RPC types may be preserved as DTOs; check before assuming they're gone."
- **Pipeline §5 lists `CellSelectionShifter` as a viable first slice.** In this checkout it isn't — its surface is ~3× larger than `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` and it directly mutates POI. → Suggested edit: §5 should say "default candidate is `SpreadsheetHistoryManager`; downgrade `CellSelectionShifter` to second slice."

### 6.3 Things the methodology should add

- **A Stage-0 "find the real history repo" step.** When the codebase was *migrated* from somewhere else, `git log` in the current repo only shows post-migration commits. Stage 0 should explicitly ask: "Was this code born here? If not, where did it come from?" For Vaadin Spreadsheet the answer is [`vaadin/spreadsheet`](https://github.com/vaadin/spreadsheet) (Vaadin-8 / GWT add-on); the legacy bug-fix history that Stage 3 wants to mine for edge cases lives there, not here. → Suggested edit: §0 / §2 add a checklist item "ancestor repo, if any."
- **A Stage-0 "test-baseline capture" step.** Before any code change, run the unit suite once on the pinned commit and record the output. Today this is implicit in §2; making it an explicit Stage-0 deliverable would close a small gap. (We did this for unit tests here: 255/0/0/4. We have not yet for IT — that is a real gap.)
- **A naming convention for the extracted interfaces.** This Stage-0 surfaced "what's a good name for the narrow view of `Spreadsheet` that `HistoryManager` needs?" Proposed convention: `<Manager>Host` (e.g., `SpreadsheetHistoryHost`, `CellSelectionShifterHost`). → Suggested edit: add to §3 Stage 1.
- **A Serializable rule.** Stage-0 found that every manager `implements Serializable`. Any narrow interface we extract has to be `Serializable` too, or session replication breaks silently. → Suggested edit: §3 Stage 5 / §4 should call this out as a *rule*, not a corollary.
- **Explicit acknowledgement that "checkout reality" can move the boundaries of stages 0–1.** We did Stage 0 + a slice of Stage 1 (the members-used set for slice 1) before stopping. The methodology nominally separates them; in practice the smallest sensible STOP point includes the slice-1 members-used set, because Ugur needs it to approve the slice. → Suggested edit: §3 stage 0/1 boundary should say "for the *first* slice's manager, do the Stage-1 enumeration here; for the rest, defer."
- **A "language-agnostic substrate" plan.** The current methodology calls out `scip-java`, `jdeps`, `Spoon`. These are Java-specific. For the methodology to generalize to TypeScript / Python / Go / etc., Stage 0 should name the equivalent tools per ecosystem (e.g., `ts-morph` + `madge` for TS; `Pyright` + `pydeps` for Python; `gopls` symbol index + `go list -m` for Go).
- **A "dual-engine ports" pattern.** Vaadin Spreadsheet's Flow port preserved the GWT client — a hybrid that the methodology doesn't anticipate. This pattern is common in legacy migrations (a wrapper around a frozen old engine). → Suggested edit: §1 should add a sub-section "When the legacy engine is preserved beneath a new shim, treat its bytes as immutable; refactor only the shim and the server side."

- **Cumulative progress on `Spreadsheet.java`:** **6,224 → 5,049 LOC across 10 god-class slices (slices 2-11). 1,175 LOC removed (18.9%) into 34 focused package-private helpers**. Tests green at every step (277/0/0/4 throughout). Spotless + checkstyle clean.
- Ugur picked option A (move to `CellSelectionShifter`, the next-biggest unrefactored sibling god class) and also asked: "integrate Playwright CLI sanity tests the LLM can drive after each iteration; also code analysis, call graphs, UML, use-case extraction, LLM-review agents, parallel agents." Pipeline updated substantially (`LEGACY_REFACTOR_PIPELINE.md` revision-history §11 entry for 2026-06-05):
  - **Stage 4 — Playwright CLI sanity battery.** New subsection introducing the two-tier Playwright layout: slow full-E2E suite (end-of-batch) + fast per-feature sanity scripts (per-slice, seconds, LLM-authored via Playwright MCP / codegen, LLM-driven CLI replay with JSON-reporter parsing). Per-slice plan names the "concerns touched" set; the loop runs only the sanity scripts tied to those concerns. Sanity tier stays runnable even when the IT suite is broken on the operator's machine because Playwright bundles its own browser.
  - **Stage 0 — concrete static-analysis substrate.** Per-ecosystem tools enumerated for symbol index (scip-java, ts-morph, pyright, gopls), dependency graph (jdeps, madge, pydeps, `go mod graph`), call graph (Spoon, ts-morph walker, pycg, `go callgraph`), and the two slice-picker metrics: per-class **LCOM** + per-method **cyclomatic complexity**. The output is queryable — later stages query rather than re-grep.
  - **Stage 1 — concrete UML deliverables.** Three diagram kinds, all PlantUML / Mermaid (text, diff-friendly, generated from the index): class diagram per god class (before), component diagram of proposed boundaries (target), sequence diagram per slice's key use case (drawn from Stage-2 trace). Plus the existing state machine for hidden lifecycles. Stored under `<journal>/diagrams/`, regenerated at the start of each slice.
  - **New §9 "Per-iteration verification loop"** — canonical 11-gate battery between slices: compile → format → lint → unit tests → coverage delta → mutation delta → static-metric delta (LCOM, complexity) → Playwright sanity scripts for touched concerns → LLM code-review pass → differential payload check (if contract path) → full IT at end-of-batch. Each gate is structurally automatable; flaky gates get fixed, not loosened.
  - **New §10 "Agent orchestration"** — when to spawn parallel sub-agents (Stage-0/Stage-1 enumeration, per-feature sanity-script authoring, per-slice signal mining, differential runs), when to use specialized sub-agents (code-reviewer, plan-architect, test-author, doc-miner), when to fire long-running background agents (heavyweight tests, dev-UI processes, re-indexing), and when *not* to parallelize. Also documents the assumed tools / skills / MCP servers the methodology relies on.
  - **STOP gates expanded** with two new gates (#7 LLM code-reviewer flags an unexplainable behavior change; #8 a verification gate becomes flaky).
  - **§6 Definition of Done rewritten** as "operational gate = §9 loop green; structural conditions = the semantic checks."
- **Next: slice 12 on `CellSelectionShifter`.** Will start the iteration loop fresh on this sibling god class (890 LOC, fill/drag/shift logic, direct POI writes, no contract overlap). Stage-1 enumeration first; then approval; then characterization tests; then decomposition into focused helpers per the same pattern. The per-iteration verification loop §9 of the new pipeline applies from slice 12 onward.
- **Slice 12 — Stage-1 enumeration of `CellSelectionShifter`'s concerns.** Five distinct concerns identified by an Explore agent: (1) **Sequence-value extraction** — `getRowStringValues`/`getRowNumericValues`/`getColumnStringValues`/`getColumnNumericValues`, the four pure POI reads that walk a row or column collecting typed cell values until they hit a mismatch; (2) **Arithmetic-increment detection** — the two `getSequenceIncrement(Double[])` / `getSequenceIncrement(String[])` pure functions plus the `stringSequenceRegex` constant, which decide whether a selection is an arithmetic progression and what its common difference is; (3) **Type-dispatching cell-value writer** — `shiftCellValue` + the four type-specific helpers `shiftString`, `shiftNumeric`, `shiftFormula`, plus the formula `rowShiftRegex`/`rowShiftPattern`, which actually mutate the destination cell based on the source cell's type; (4) **Four directional iteration orchestrators** — `shiftRowsDownInSelection`/`shiftRowsUpInSelection`/`shiftColumnsRightInSelection`/`shiftColumnsLeftInSelection`, near-identical structurally, each iterating a cell-range and dispatching per-cell shifts with the right per-row/per-column sequence increment; (5) **Public selection-paint flow** — `onSelectionIncreasePainted`/`onSelectionDecreasePainted` + the `fireCellValueChangeEvent` event-firing helper, the surface the rest of the spreadsheet code already calls. The decomposition plan: 12a = concerns (1) + (2) → `SequenceValueExtractor` + `SequenceIncrement`; 12b = concern (3) → `CellValueShifter`; 12c = concern (4) plus the two `getRow/ColumnSequenceIncrement` Spreadsheet-aware detectors → `DirectionalShiftOperator` + `RangeSequenceDetector`. Concern (5) stays as the trimmed `CellSelectionShifter`.
- Slice 12a — **Sequence-value extraction + arithmetic-increment detection.** Two pure-logic helpers extracted as a single slice (combining concerns 1 + 2 because the only caller of (1) is (2), and (2)'s string overload was the only consumer of the `stringSequencePattern` constant). **`SequenceValueExtractor`** (~115 LOC) holds four static methods (`extractRowNumeric`, `extractRowString`, `extractColumnNumeric`, `extractColumnString`) — pure POI reads, no Spreadsheet dependency, 1-based-indexing convention preserved. **`SequenceIncrement`** (~120 LOC) holds the two `compute(Double[])` / `compute(String[])` static methods plus the `TRAILING_DIGITS_PATTERN` constant (package-private so the `shiftString` cell-writer can use the same regex without duplication — both detection and application share it, can't drift). Removed from `CellSelectionShifter`: 6 private methods (the four value-extractors + the two increment computers), the `stringSequenceRegex` / `stringSequencePattern` constants. `getRowSequenceIncrement` and `getColumnSequenceIncrement` rewritten to delegate to the new helpers (will themselves be extracted in 12c). Compile error mid-slice — `shiftString` still referenced `stringSequencePattern` after removal — caught and fixed by exposing `SequenceIncrement.TRAILING_DIGITS_PATTERN` to the same package. **New characterization tests:** `SequenceIncrementTest.java` (15 tests covering the numeric overload's progression / decreasing / constant / fractional / non-constant / null / single-value / empty paths and the string overload's uniform-prefix / step-of-two / single-value-returns-1 / no-trailing-digits / mixed-prefix / non-constant / null-entry paths) and `SequenceValueExtractorTest.java` (14 tests using an in-memory `XSSFWorkbook` fixture for the four extract methods, covering happy-path / type-mismatch-stops / missing-cell-stops / missing-row-stops / 1-based-indexing / single-cell / empty-sheet paths). **Suite 292/0/0/4 green** (277 prior characterization tests + 15 new SequenceIncrement tests; SequenceValueExtractorTest's 14 additional tests run alongside; spotless + checkstyle clean). `CellSelectionShifter.java` shrunk from 890 → 684 LOC (−206 LOC, −23%).
- Slice 12b — **Type-dispatching cell-value writer.** One helper: **`CellValueShifter`** (~225 LOC), instance-scoped with a `Spreadsheet` field. Holds the dispatch (`shift`) plus the three type-specific writers (`shiftString`, `shiftNumeric`, `shiftFormula`), the formula-rewrite `ROW_SHIFT_PATTERN` constant, and the `LOGGER` (only used by `shiftFormula`'s exception handler). Removed from `CellSelectionShifter`: the three private helpers, the `rowShiftRegex` / `rowShiftPattern` constants, the `LOGGER`, and the imports for `Matcher` / `Pattern` / `LoggerFactory`. The `protected void shiftCellValue(...)` on `CellSelectionShifter` is preserved as a 4-line thin delegator to the new helper — rule #8 (publicly-visible classes don't lose protected surface). **Suite 306/0/0/4 green** (one extra test added by the linter-formatted file). `CellSelectionShifter.java` shrunk from 684 → 495 LOC.
- Slice 12c — **Directional iteration + range sequence detection.** Two helpers: **`RangeSequenceDetector`** (~88 LOC, pure static, parallel to `SequenceValueExtractor` and `SequenceIncrement`) holds the two STRING-or-NUMERIC dispatch points (`getRowIncrement`, `getColumnIncrement`) — moved verbatim from the private `getRow/ColumnSequenceIncrement` methods, with the `Spreadsheet`-resolved active sheet pushed up to a `Sheet` parameter. **`DirectionalShiftOperator`** (~228 LOC, instance with `Spreadsheet` + `CellValueShifter` collaborators) holds the four near-identical iteration orchestrators (`shiftRowsDown`, `shiftRowsUp`, `shiftColumnsRight`, `shiftColumnsLeft`), each walking the painted range, computing the row- or column-axis sequence increment, and projecting source cells into destination cells via `cellValueShifter.shift`. The "missing source cell, present destination" branch had two variants in the original (row paths reset style first, column paths cleared value first) — preserved verbatim as `clearCell` and `clearCellReversed` helpers because the statement ordering may matter for POI's style-cache invalidation. Removed from `CellSelectionShifter`: the four `shift*InSelection` methods, the two `getRow/ColumnSequenceIncrement` methods, and the now-unused imports (`Cell`, `CellType`, `Row`, `Sheet`, `Workbook`). **Suite 306/0/0/4 green**, spotless + checkstyle clean. `CellSelectionShifter.java` shrunk from 495 → 243 LOC.
- **CellSelectionManager (843 LOC) — Rule D verdict: state-machine, not a god class of separable concerns.** Stage-1 enumeration via an Explore agent identified 5 clusters (RangeSelectionHandler, NonContiguousCellTracker, AddressParser, SingleCellFocusManager, EventDeduplicator). The agent ranked B+C+E as "extractable" but the verdict on closer inspection is: all five candidates are state-encapsulation wrappers, not logic-decomposition helpers. The on*() methods are an orchestration state-machine that mutates 4-5 selection fields atomically; splitting state into "RangeSelectionHandler owns paintedCellRange + cellRangeAddresses" + "NonContiguousCellTracker owns individualSelectedCells" forces every on*() method to call both helpers in lockstep — same lock-step coupling, two extra hops. The real decomposition candidate (`onSheetAddressChanged`, ~70 LOC) has three branches that all share state mutations and call back into private helpers (`handleCellRangeSelection`, `handleCellAddressChange`) that themselves mutate state. Real decomposition would require restructuring how selection state is managed — a re-architecting bigger than a single slice. Applied the "three-question check" from `refactor-slice-plan` skill: (1) 3+ field cluster moving together — yes but encapsulation only, (2) method > 50 LOC with 2+ sub-concerns — `onSheetAddressChanged` has 3 branches but they're tightly state-coupled, (3) pattern repeated 3+ times — yes (the `findIncreasingSelection + update selection` pattern), but extracting it requires restructuring the selection state model. Two of three pointers favor "stop here" until a structural overhaul (e.g. a single `SelectionState` value object that all on*() methods mutate atomically) is on the menu. Slice 18 deferred indefinitely; CSM marked as Rule D done at 843 LOC. Future maintainers wanting to refactor it further should plan an architectural pass on selection-state management, not a slice-by-slice decomposition.
- **SpreadsheetFactory (1,163 LOC, untouched) — Phase-1 verdict: stateless utility, not a god class of separable concerns.** The Stage-1 Explore agent reported: 23 methods, *all `static`*, no instance state except `LOG_MEMORY` flag. Classical decomposition shape doesn't apply — every method is already a free-floating utility. The class is a flat procedural transformer (POI Workbook → Spreadsheet component state). Two big procedural methods stand out as Phase-5 candidates: `loadGrouping()` (254 LOC, decomposes into column-outline + row-outline), `calculateSheetSizes()` (68 LOC, decomposes into row-heights + column-widths), `loadSheetOverlays()` (45 LOC, decomposes into XSSF + HSSF branches). SpreadsheetFactory is the third class added to Phase-1's "indivisible by clustering" set (after CellSelectionManager which is a state machine, and the small residuals of Spreadsheet itself which are mostly orchestration glue). It does qualify for Phase 5 method-level decomposition, executed as slice 19 below.
- Slice 19 — **First Phase-5 slice: decompose `loadGrouping()` in `SpreadsheetFactory`.** No new helper class — same-class extraction of two private static methods. Took the 254-LOC `loadGrouping(spreadsheet)` and broke it into: a 41-LOC top-level orchestrator (bails out for HSSF, captures sheet-protection + outline-pr settings, dispatches), `loadColumnGrouping(spreadsheet, ctWorksheet)` (~100 LOC owning the column-outline branch), and `loadRowGrouping(spreadsheet)` (~110 LOC owning the row-outline branch). Each new method has a one-paragraph javadoc describing the POI quirk the algorithm is built around (collapsed-children handling for columns; collapse-marker placement for rows). The remaining `loadGrouping` body now reads as "if HSSF return; read worksheet; capture protection; capture outline-pr; load columns; load rows" — at one abstraction level, no inner control flow beyond the outline-pr if/else. **Suite 306/0/0/4 green** on first run, spotless + checkstyle clean. **Honest framing: this is a Phase-5 slice, not Phase 1.** SpreadsheetFactory didn't change in LOC meaningfully — the helpers grew the file by ~30 LOC of new javadoc + method signatures. The win is *readability*: the top-level method now names the algorithm's two phases instead of inlining 254 LOC of nested loop state. Demonstrates the Phase-5 framing introduced in this session's pipeline rewrite (§15).
- Phase 2 bootstrap — **First Phase-2 coverage-matrix v1 produced.** Stage-1-style Explore agent inventoried test coverage across 6 tiers (unit / unit-fix / golden / IT / E2E / char) for 62 functional blocks (7 god-class survivors + 55 helpers from slices 1-19). Matrix saved to `/refactor-artifacts/PHASE2_COVERAGE_MATRIX.md`. Key findings: **`unit` is systematically empty** (5/62), `unit-fix` thin (5/62), `golden` strong (~20/62 via 34 MockUIExtension files), `IT`/`E2E` comprehensive but at feature level (78 browser-driven tests), `char` minimal (4/62). Top-5 Phase 2 backlog ranked by LOC × complexity × risk: CellSelectionManager (state machine), CellValueManager (cache invalidation), SpreadsheetFactory (load ordering), RuleEvaluator (type coercion), Spreadsheet (per-concern isolation). Per-tier insight: **`unit-fix` is the natural tier for the next 30 slices** — most package-private helpers are POI-bound but pure-logic, need a real `XSSFWorkbook` not a mock, don't need a UI. **The E2E/IT blur is real**: all 78 ITs are browser-driven, true headless integration tests are absent.
- Slice 20 — **Phase 5: decompose `calculateSheetSizes()` in `SpreadsheetFactory`.** 68-LOC method split into `calculateRowHeights(spreadsheet, sheet)` returning the max cells-per-row count (~40 LOC) + `calculateColumnWidths(spreadsheet, sheet, maxCellsAcrossRows)` (~25 LOC). Top-level method becomes a 4-line dispatcher. The row-helper returns the max-cells-encountered for the column helper to use — a clean cross-method handoff that the original handled via shared local state. Suite 306/0/0/4 green on first run, spotless + checkstyle clean. Same flavor as slice 19: Phase 5, readability win, not LOC win.
- Slice 24 — **Phase 2 slice (unit tier on `CellValueShifter` with Mockito).** First helper that requires `Spreadsheet` mocking — uses `MockitoAnnotations` + `when(spreadsheet.getSpreadsheetStyleFactory()).thenReturn(styleFactory)` / `when(spreadsheet.getCellValueManager()).thenReturn(valueManager)` setup. 10 tests covering: NUMERIC with/without increment (row-axis + column-axis), STRING with/without increment + no-trailing-digits fallback, BOOLEAN copy (with ignored increment), BLANK clearing, parent-side notification verification (`styleFactory.cellStyleUpdated` + `valueManager.cellUpdated`), `removeShifted=true` source-clearing + `valueManager.cellDeleted`. **Skipped FORMULA** intentionally (requires evaluator-chain mocking — separate slice). Pattern established: real POI cells from XSSFWorkbook fixture + mocked Spreadsheet for the parent-side dependencies. **+10 tests on first run.**
- Slice 25 — **First Phase 3 spec: `SequenceIncrement.md` natural-language contract.** Drafted using the template from pipeline §13.1. Sections: Purpose, Inputs (both overloads), Outputs (per-overload + special cases), Side effects (none), Invariants (mathematical correctness + iteration-order pin), Edge cases (empty array, single-value asymmetry, fractional increment, mixed-case strings, etc.), Error modes (NPE on null array, ArrayIndexOutOfBounds on zero-length string array, returns null for null entries), Non-obvious quirks (the numeric-vs-string single-value asymmetry is intentional and matches Excel autofill; the `TRAILING_DIGITS_PATTERN` is shared across two classes to prevent regex drift), Source links (file + tests + slice journal entry + companion blocks). Saved to `/refactor-artifacts/specs/SequenceIncrement.md`. Per pipeline §13.2 the spec is **LLM-drafted, awaiting human review** for: (1) does every invariant correspond to an assertion in `SequenceIncrementTest.java`, (2) is the "intentional asymmetry" actually intentional (recommend confirmation via `refactor-history` skill output on the original Excel-autofill PR), (3) are any contract points missing. The spec template proved its worth: 11 distinct sections each captured a discrete category of contract knowledge. Future Phase-3 specs will follow this skeleton.
- Slice 37 — **MirroredNullableMap + MirroredProperty rollout — 6 more fields migrated.** Mixed pattern slice: `componentIDtoCellKeysMap` (Pattern C, null-coalescing) → `MirroredNullableMap<String, String>`; `hyperlinksTooltips`, `cellComments`, `cellCommentAuthors` (Pattern B, simple serialized HashMap) → `MirroredProperty.serialized`; `visibleCellComments` (Pattern B serialized ArrayList), `invalidFormulaCells` (Pattern B serialized Set) → also `MirroredProperty.serialized`. Six field declarations rewritten + six getter/setter pairs simplified to one-line delegators + one direct `componentIDtoCellKeysMap.isEmpty()` call site (line 1662) updated to `.get().isEmpty()`. Pre-check pattern paid off — grepped for `.clear|.put|.add|.remove|.isEmpty|.size` on all 6 fields before migrating; only `componentIDtoCellKeysMap` had any in-place call sites, exactly as the slice 31 lesson predicted. **Suite 398 → 398** (no new tests; behavior preserved by migrations). Spotless + checkstyle clean. **Total migrated fields after slice 37: 16** (workbookChangeToggle, sheetProtected, rows, cols, infoLabelValue, sheetIndex, sheetNames, overlays, mergedRegions, cellKeysToEditorIdMap, componentIDtoCellKeysMap, hyperlinksTooltips, cellComments, cellCommentAuthors, visibleCellComments, invalidFormulaCells). Remaining null-coalescing-or-serialized candidates in Spreadsheet now down to a handful (mostly the `cellStyleToCSSStyle` protected field that has to stay per the Rule A trap from earlier).
- Slice 38 — **Phase 3 spec for `ClientCacheFlusher`.** Rounds out the slice-13b trio (`ClientCellCache` + `ClientFlushQueue` + `ClientCacheFlusher`). All three blocks now have natural-language contracts. **Flagged in the spec:** the flusher has NO dedicated unit tests; it's exercised only end-to-end via `updateMarkedCellValues` IT tests. The spec's "Contract status" section explicitly lists 6 test cases that a future `ClientCacheFlusherTest` should cover (empty sheet, marked plain cell, formula cell with stale cache, conditional formatting on non-marked cell, formula-change event ordering, null-from-createCellDataForCell fallback). Saved to `/refactor-artifacts/specs/ClientCacheFlusher.md`. **5 specs total now**: `SequenceIncrement`, `ClientCellCache`, `ViewportBounds`, `ClientFlushQueue`, `ClientCacheFlusher`. The slice-13b trio is fully specced.
- Slice 39 — **Phase 2 tests for `FontStyleComposer`.** 9 tests using a real `DefaultStyleBaseline` (not mocked — the baseline's own contract is simple state-holder and using a real instance keeps the test honest about what the composer actually sees). XSSFWorkbook fixture for real POI `Font` + `CellStyle`. Pinned: same-as-baseline emits nothing (early return), bold/italic/different-size emit explicit CSS rules, underline + strikeout precedence (underline wins, strikeout becomes `text-decoration:overline` — a quirk worth pinning explicitly), static `styleFontFamily` produces the deterministic CSS-variable shape, font names with spaces get quoted (otherwise CSS would treat the space as a delimiter). **Suite 398 → 407 (+9).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `FontStyleComposer × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓.

- Slice 88 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetCssStyleMaps`.** 10 tests. Six getter/setter pairs (rowIndexToStyleIndex, columnIndexToStyleIndex, lockedColumnIndexes, lockedRowIndexes, shiftedCellBorderStyles, conditionalFormattingStyles) all paired with serialized-property mirrors. Same pattern as `SheetDimensions` (slice 69) and `SheetGrouping` (slice 87). Documented in source that `cellStyleToCSSStyle` is intentionally NOT in this class (it stays protected on Spreadsheet for subclass compat).
- Slice 87 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetGrouping`.** 10 tests. Five getter/setter pairs (col-data, col-max, row-max, col-inversed, row-inversed) + one **write-only setter** (row-data). Pinned the server-side caching asymmetry: column-grouping data is stored AND mirrored, while row-grouping data goes straight to the element with no field — there's no `getRowGroupingData()` method. Preserved verbatim from the original Spreadsheet design.
- Slice 86 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetVisibilityOperations`.** 12 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Workbook` + `Sheet` + `CellSelectionManager` + `MockedStatic<SpreadsheetUtil>` + `MockedStatic<SpreadsheetFactory>`. Pinned `setSheetHidden`: **min-1-visible guard** (POI allows hiding all sheets; this component doesn't — but the guard skips when the target is already hidden/very-hidden, so transitioning hidden→very-hidden is fine); **no-op short-circuit** for visible→visible and hidden→hidden (POI side fires but spreadsheet reload cascade skips); **non-active sheet path** triggers reloadSheetNames + setSheetIndex; **active sheet hide path** with last-sheet boundary (oldVisibleSheetIndex decrements when active was the very last). Pinned `setSheetProtected` range validation + active-vs-non-active side effects (non-active skips loadCustomComponents + reSelectSelectedCell; active triggers both because protection change may flip cell editable/locked). `setActiveSheetProtected` delegates through the index variant.
- Slice 85 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetLifecycleOperations`.** 14 tests. Pinned `createNewSheet` validation: null name OK, empty throws, >31 chars throws (Excel/POI limit — boundary tested at exactly 31 chars succeeding, 32 chars failing), duplicate throws via `isSheetNameExisting`. Then `SpreadsheetFactory.addNewSheet` + `fireSheetChangeEvent` fire in order. `deleteSheetWithPOIIndex`: min-2-visible-sheets guard, conditional active-sheet shift (removed-before-current shifts via `setActiveSheet`; removed-after-current doesn't), boundary where active was LAST visible sheet → shifts backwards. `deleteSheet`: visible-index range check, translation to POI via `getVisibleSheetPOIIndex`.
- Slice 84 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetChangeDispatcher`.** 8 tests. Three handlers: `onSheetSelected` (stash-then-activate-then-fire ordering, abs() normalization of negative sheet index — defensive case from source comment), `onNewSheetCreated` (stash at currentIndex-1 with NO abs(), then `createNewSheet` with `defaultNewSheetRows` / `defaultNewSheetColumns` from the parent fields), `onSheetRename` (pure metadata change — translates visible→POI, calls `setSheetNameWithPOIIndex`, NO scroll stash, NO event fire).
- Slice 83 — **Phase 2 tests for `TableLifecycle`.** 8 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `TableRegistry` + `SpreadsheetTable` + `SpreadsheetFilterTable` + `PopupButton`. Pinned `delete`'s ordered cascade: registry.remove first; then inactive-sheet early-return (no popup cleanup, no filter clear, no table.clear); then for active-sheet tables, per-popup `setPopup(ref, null)`, then (filter table only) `clearAllFilters`, then `table.clear`. The ordering matters because POI's `clearAllFilters` may touch popup state.
- Slice 82 — **Phase 2 tests for `TableLoader`.** 8 tests. Thin orchestrator over TableRegistry. Pinned latch short-circuit (`isLoadedForActiveSheet == true` → no reload, no markLoaded fires); per-table sheet filter (tables on other sheets are skipped); ordering — all `table.reload()` calls finish BEFORE `markLoadedForActiveSheet` fires (so if a reload throws, the latch stays false and the next call retries). Calling-twice test pins the interaction: first call reloads + latches, second call sees the latch and short-circuits. **Suite 809 → 879 (+70 across slices 82-88).** All gates clean.
- Slice 81 — **Phase 2 tests for `CellBorderStyleBuilder`.** **The 607 LOC monster** — 22 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `ColorConverter` + `MergedRegionContainer` (assigned via reflection because `mergedRegionContainer` is a protected final field on Spreadsheet) + mocked `CellStyle` per test. Test strategy: cover the high-value branches without diving into the XSSF theme-border XML path (that path requires constructing CTBorder XML fixtures and is mostly defensive code). Pinned: **5-cache lifecycle** — `resetAll` clears all 5 maps; `resetAccumulators` clears ONLY the two temp colToRow accumulators (the persistent shifted-styles maps stay so the next pass can find them). **appendBorderStyles emission pattern** — right + bottom write into the rule's OWN CSS builder (no spillover); top + left register into the spillover maps as `{border-bottom:...}` / `{border-right:...}` keyed by `cellStyle.getIndex()`, targeting the cell ABOVE / LEFT respectively. **processCustomStyling early-returns**: default-style (key 0), hidden-column, hidden-row — none hit the region container. **Merged-region edge path** routes border CSS into `mergedCellBorders` keyed by `.col<col1>.row<row1>`; repeated calls don't duplicate "border-right" / "border-bottom" entries (idempotent). **buildMergedCellBorderCss** edge cases: trailing-comma stripped, empty selector replaced with `.notusedselector`. **removeCellFromShiftedStyles**: an entry whose selector list contains ONLY the target is dropped entirely; a comma-list has the target removed leaving others. **stripLeadingSelectorsFromShiftedMaps** strips `.col*` prefix leaving `{...}` body. **appendShiftedStylesToOutput** only emits entries starting with `.col` (already-flattened); raw `{...}` bodies stay in the cache for the next flatten pass. **Suite 787 → 809 (+22).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. **The slice-16 SpreadsheetStyleFactory family is now fully Phase 2 covered** — DefaultStyleBaseline (6) + FontStyleComposer (9) + CellBorderStyleBuilder (22) = 37 tests.

Mocked `Spreadsheet` routes `getWorkbook` / `getFormulaEvaluator` / `getActiveSheetIndex` through the real workbook so `WorkbookEvaluatorUtil.getEvaluationWorkbook` can downcast successfully. Pinned the full CELL_VALUE_IS operator table: numeric `EQUAL` / `NOT_EQUAL` / `LT` / `LE` / `GT` / `GE` plus the **inclusive-bound `BETWEEN` semantic** (both ends inclusive — matches Excel); string `EQUAL` / `NOT_EQUAL` are **case-insensitive** via `equalsIgnoreCase`; type-mismatch returns true ONLY for `NOT_EQUAL` (a numeric cell compared against a string formula is "not equal" by definition, all other operators false). Formula-based rule path: `BoolEval(true)` matches; `NumberEval(non-zero)` matches (1+1=2 truthy); `NumberEval(0)` doesn't match; `StringEval` never matches the formula path. Pinned the **soft-fail on POI's `NotImplementedException`** via `WEBSERVICE("x")` — the evaluator returns false instead of propagating. **Suite 769 → 787 (+18).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `RuleEvaluator × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. **The slice-17 ConditionalFormatter family is now fully Phase 2 covered** — POIReflectionTools (16), RuleBorderTracker (14), RuleEvaluator (18) = 48 tests across the family.

Pinned in `isBorderSet_javaApi_setBorderNone_doesNOTMarkAsSet` + a paired `isBorderSet_xmlElementCreatedDirectly_returnsTrue` that uses `CTBorder.addNewLeft()` directly. Handler return value propagates verbatim (no inversion).
