A 30-minute survey + roadmap shapes the next 5-10 implementation slices toward a defined endpoint instead of "we'll get there eventually". The user's "in the end" goal becomes a checklist with verified facts, not a north star that drifts. This is the same pattern from slice 116 (Tier 3 deep examination) — pause for planning when the trajectory matters more than the next port.
- Slice 126 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.2 cont.: complete SpreadsheetClientApi interface (close slice 120 gap).** Audit finding while planning slice 126: slice 120's `SpreadsheetClientApi` declared "50 methods" but a `comm -23` diff between the wrapper element's `this.api.X(...)` calls and the interface's declared methods revealed **54 missing methods**. The skeleton's "compile-time contract enforcement" claim from slice 121 — "if a future slice forgets to add a method, the TS compiler fails" — was broken: the WRAPPER calls 104 methods, the INTERFACE declares 50. Implementing the interface guarantees nothing about the wrapper-callable surface. The missing 54 break down as: **42 callback setters** (`setXCallback(callback)`) registering client→server event handlers — setOnSheetScroll, setCellSelected, setCellValueEdited, setSheetSelected, setOnUndo, setOnPaste, etc. (the GWT-era event-flow registration API); **11 property setters** that slice 120 missed (setVerticalSplitPosition, setHorizontalSplitPosition, setDisplayGridlines, setDisplayRowColHeadings, setLockFormatColumns, setLockFormatRows, setHasActions, setWorkbookChangeToggle, setWorkbookProtected, setInfoLabelValue, setHyperlinksTooltips, setId, setClass); **1 legacy property access** (`spreadsheetWidget.sheetWidget.definedRowHeights`) — the GWT wrapper's "wait for first render" timing trick. **Slice 120's gap diagnosis**: the original survey ran `grep -oE "this\.api\.X(...)" wrapper.js` but stopped reading too early — the callback registrations live in a SEPARATE section of wrapper.js (lines 440-604), an `initApi()` block that fires once after the api is created. The slice 120 survey only walked the property-changed-callback block (lines 174-305) and missed the registration block entirely. **Slice 126 shipped**: (1) `lit/types/rpc-server-to-client.ts` extended from 50 → 105 methods with proper section dividers (`// Freeze pane split positions`, `// Display flags`, `// Display content`, `// Event-callback registration` with a multi-line javadoc explaining the convention) plus a `// Internal-widget escape hatch (legacy)` section for `spreadsheetWidget?: { sheetWidget?: { definedRowHeights?: unknown; [key: string]: unknown } }` typed permissively because Phase 3.6 removes it. Callback parameter types use `(event: unknown) => void` uniformly — future slices can tighten per-callback as the implementations land + as the `SpreadsheetEvent` discriminated union data interfaces get exercised. (2) `lit/Spreadsheet.ts` skeleton extended with matching stubs: 11 new private fields (workbookProtected, hyperlinksTooltips, verticalSplitPosition, horizontalSplitPosition, displayGridlines, displayRowColHeadings, lockFormatColumns, lockFormatRows, hasActions, workbookChangeToggle, infoLabelValue, elementId, elementClass — kept `elementId` / `elementClass` to avoid shadowing the host element's id/class — and a `callbacks: Record<string, (event: unknown) => void>` registry), 12 property setters (each mirroring its private field), 42 callback setters (each writing into the callbacks map keyed by the event name, e.g. `this.callbacks.GroupingCollapsed = callback`). The callback registry pattern — single Map keyed by event name vs 42 separate fields — was chosen because the Phase 3.3+ dispatch code will iterate the registry by name when an event fires; per-event fields would force a 42-way switch statement at dispatch time. (3) `lit/Spreadsheet.ts` adds `readonly spreadsheetWidget = undefined` so the GWT-era wrapper's existence check (`if (!this.api.spreadsheetWidget.sheetWidget.definedRowHeights)`) sees a clean undefined and falls through to the GWT-blob path. **Tests**: extended `lit-spreadsheet-skeleton.test.ts` with 9 new "is callable + storable" smoke tests across 3 new describe blocks (freeze + display state, callback registry, legacy escape hatch). Per slice 121's pattern, these are smoke tests — they don't pin behavior beyond "doesn't throw + stored if observable". The real value is COMPILE-TIME: now the skeleton's `implements SpreadsheetClientApi` clause ACTUALLY enforces the full 105-method wire contract. If a future slice removes any of these methods (or changes its signature), the test file fails to type-check + the IT fails to compile. wtr reports **175 passed / 0 failed** (+9 vs slice 125's 166). **Lessons banked**: (1) When auditing an interface vs its implementation, a `comm` diff between two `grep | sort -u` lists is fast + complete; eyeballing function lists is slow + error-prone. The slice 120 review (which I conducted) missed 54 methods because I scanned visually. (2) When a wrapper has TWO sections that call the same API (one for property changes, one for callback registration), check both. The slice 120 audit checked one. (3) Type the callback registry as a single Map<string, callback> not per-event fields — saves ~40 LOC and a 40-way switch at dispatch time. (4) "Compile-time enforcement" is a useful claim only if the interface IS the full contract. An interface that's a subset is worse than no interface — it gives false confidence. Slice 121's "load-bearing safety net" claim was load-bearing on a NON-COMPLETE foundation; slice 126 makes the foundation complete. **Total slice volume**: ~140 LOC added to rpc-server-to-client.ts, ~280 LOC added to Spreadsheet.ts, ~120 LOC added to lit-spreadsheet-skeleton.test.ts (9 new tests across 3 describe blocks).
- Slice 125 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.2 cont.: CellKeys codec port + skill updates banking lessons from 122-124.** Two payloads in one slice: (1) small additional port to keep Phase 3.2 momentum, (2) bank lessons from prior slices into the skill files so future-Claude doesn't repeat the same mistakes. **CellKeys port**: `lit/algorithms/CellKeys.ts` (~150 LOC including javadoc) — 5 functions from the GWT client's wire-format codec. `toKey(col, row)` and `toCssKey(col, row)` from `SheetWidget.java` lines 4578-4584 ("col1 row1" + ".col1.row1"). `parseColRow(str)` from `SheetJsniUtil.parseColRow` lines 20-47 — character-code scanner that accumulates digits before/after the first space, stops after the second. `headerType(str)` from `SheetJsniUtil.isHeader` lines 58-76 — returns numeric `HEADER_NONE=0` / `HEADER_ROW=1` / `HEADER_COL=2` based on the first two characters of the string ("rh..." vs "ch..." vs anything else). Note the GWT quirk: cell keys like "col1 row1" start with 'c' but the SECOND char is 'o' (not 'h'), so `headerType("col1 row1")` returns 0 — same goes for "row5" (second char 'o', not 'h'). The classifier is deliberately narrow; cell keys are NOT headers per this contract. Preserved verbatim. `parseHeaderIndex(str)` from JsniUtil lines 79-93 — character-code scanner that accumulates ALL digits wherever they appear. The multi-run case ("rh1ch2" → 12) is a quirk the upstream presumably never invokes; ported verbatim. **23 tests** in `lit-cell-keys.test.ts` covering toKey + toCssKey formatting, parseColRow round-trip across coordinate ranges + the second-space-stop quirk + zero defaults for missing digits, headerType for all three branches + the "row5 is NOT a row header" quirk + edge cases (empty, single-char, unrelated strings), parseHeaderIndex including the multi-digit-run quirk. **Skill updates banking lessons from 122-124**: (a) `code-modernization` skill — added a new section "Porting between languages (a Tier 3 sub-pattern)" with four subsections: "Local source over JAR/online docs" (cites the sibling-repos pattern: read `../flow`, `../docs`, `../web-components` directly, not artifact extraction); "Byte-equivalence is the default; document deviations with QUIRK callouts" (formalizes the `// QUIRK:` javadoc pattern + regression tests that pin the quirk by name); "Tests must ACTUALLY RUN — `tsc --noEmit` is not a runtime check" (the slice 124 lesson — a `>` vs `>=` off-by-one passed strict TS compilation but failed runtime tests); "Use the same in-ecosystem reference as for code modernization" (the vaadin-grid-flow gridConnector.ts pattern cite). (b) `refactor-verify` skill Gate 4 — added "Verify the count matches expectation, not just the exit code" guidance. The example: `wtr` reporting `8 test files | 0 passed, 0 failed` exits 0 but ran nothing — the slice 124 discovery that slices 118-121's 107 tests had never executed despite four green slices. The gate now mandates counting `it(`/`@Test` declarations manually and checking the runner's total matches. **WTR validates the slice**: 166/166 tests pass (143 from slice 124's run + 23 new). **Pattern**: paired infrastructure-investment slices with a small port keeps momentum while paying down the "we should have done this two slices ago" debt. Slice 124 fixed the wtr config; slice 125 banks the lesson so the FIX is durable across personnel turnover. **Total slice volume**: 1 new TS source (~150 LOC), 1 new test file (~160 LOC, 23 tests), 2 skill updates (~80 LOC added across code-modernization + refactor-verify), README + log updates. **Lesson banked here**: skills are not just guidance for the operator — they ARE part of the project's deliverable. Updating a skill is more leverageable than fixing the same bug in three future slices. Pair skill updates with the next slice they prevent regressions in.
- Slice 124 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.2 cont.: ViewportMath pure-algorithm port + WTR config fix.** Second Phase 3.2 slice — first after slice 121's skeleton. Targeted the GWT client's coordinate / viewport / virtual-scrolling math because (a) it's pure-algorithm, no DOM, (b) it's the innermost loop of cell recycling — porting early unblocks downstream subsystems, and (c) the GWT side has it tightly bounded between `getRowHeight`, `convertPointsToPixel`, and `resetScrollView` in `SheetWidget.java` lines 2300-2910. **Ported**: 7 functions into a single `lit/algorithms/ViewportMath.ts` (~250 LOC including type defs + javadoc): (1) `convertPointsToPixel(points, ppi)` — Excel 1/72-inch-per-point → integer pixels, using `Math.trunc` to mirror Java's `BigDecimal.intValue()` truncation (NOT round). (2) `convertPixelsToPoint(pixels, ppi)` — float inverse. (3) `getDefaultRowHeightPx(defaultRowHeightPoints, ppi)` — with 96-ppi fallback when caller passes 0 or negative, matching SheetWidget's lazy-DOM-measurement default. (4) `getRowHeightPx(row1Based, definedRowHeightsPx, hiddenRows, defaultRowHeightPx)` — handles hidden (return 0), beyond-defined (return default), in-range (return array entry). **Behavior preservation note**: the GWT version uses `row >= definedRowHeights.length` (NOT `>`), meaning a length-N array returns array entries for rows 1..N-1 and falls to default for row N. This is unusual — looks like the upstream array is sized N+1 — but ported verbatim for byte-equivalence with a documented javadoc "QUIRK" callout. (5) `calculateLeftValueOfScrolledColumns(horizontalSplitPosition, firstColumnIndex, getColWidth)` — sums column widths between freeze split and first visible. (6) `calculateTopValueOfScrolledRows(verticalSplitPosition, firstRowIndex, getRowHeightForIndex)` — symmetric. (7) `computeViewportBounds(ViewportInput) → ViewportBounds` — the centerpiece, ported from `resetScrollView` lines 2851-2910. Accepts a structured input bundle (scrollLeft, scrollTop, viewportW/H, freeze-pane indices, buffer sizes, max rows/columns, definedRows, defaultRowHeightPx, getColWidthActual + getRowHeight callbacks) and returns an 8-field output (first/last index + position for both axes). The 4 nested loops (advance first to scroll-buffer, extend last to scroll+viewport+buffer, per axis) mirror the Java exactly. Row branch preserves the `firstRowIndex >= definedRows` special-case where it bypasses `getRowHeight` and uses `defaultRowHeightPx` directly. **Tests**: `lit-viewport-math.test.ts` — 27 tests across 9 describe blocks covering unit conversion (truncation vs rounding, non-default PPI, round-trip), default row height (fallback when PPI = 0 / negative, supplied PPI used when positive), getRowHeightPx (in-range, hidden, beyond-defined, the `>= length` quirk, hidden-beyond-defined), `calculateLeftValueOfScrolledColumns` (no freeze, freeze boundary, sum across, callback invocation tracking), and the four computeViewportBounds scenarios (no-freeze with scroll, buffer math, horizontal + vertical freeze, rows beyond definedRows). Each test traces the algorithm explicitly in comments so future readers can debug regressions against the trace. **Big incidental fix**: while running the new tests I discovered that NONE of the slices 118-121 tests (107 tests across 7 files) had actually been running in CI. The 404 error: `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow/src/main/resources/META-INF/resources/frontend/vaadin-spreadsheet/lit/algorithms/ViewportMath.ts` — the test runner couldn't serve files outside the IT module's directory because rootDir defaulted to `.` (the IT module). The lit/ source lives in the sibling production module. Fix: override `rootDir: '..'` in `web-test-runner.config.mjs`, plus `files: ['test/lit-*.test.ts']` and `nodeResolve: true`. Verification: `wtr` (no args) now reports `8 test files | 143 passed, 0 failed` in 1.3s. This single-line config fix retroactively validates slices 118-121's 107 tests AND today's 27 — they all pass. **Verification chain extended**: slice 124's port wouldn't have caught the `getRowHeightPx` off-by-one bug I almost shipped (`>` vs `>=`) without running the actual tests — `tsc --noEmit` would have green-lit my wrong code. The wtr config fix is the load-bearing safety net for all future Phase 3.x TS work. **Other infrastructure noticed**: top-level `node_modules` wasn't installed (the `@web/test-runner` etc. devDependencies in `flow-components/package.json` weren't installed); I ran `npm install` at the top once and now it's there. The IT module's own `package.json` is regenerated by Maven on every build (per the maven-clean-plugin fileset at `pom.xml` lines 158-178), so adding `scripts` there wouldn't stick. The wtr config (.mjs file) IS persistent because it's not in the clean fileset. **Total slice volume**: 1 new TS source (~250 LOC), 1 new test file (~330 LOC), 1 config update (4 LOC), 2 README updates (lit/README.md). **Lessons banked**: (1) when porting math from a different language, ALWAYS run the tests — `tsc` passes for syntactically valid code with semantic bugs. Almost shipped a `>` vs `>=` off-by-one that the human-eye review of the Java sources missed. (2) When a multi-slice feature has an aspirational test runner that "doesn't actually run", the cost of fixing the runner is borne by whichever slice notices it. Pay the cost — the retroactive validation of 107 prior tests is worth the 5 minutes of config debugging. (3) The "behavior preservation vs intentional improvement" decision recurred for the row-height array-length quirk. Per the pattern from slice 119 (`[A-z]` regex character class preserved verbatim, but regex-metachar escape was the new-behavior improvement): preserve quirks verbatim, document with "QUIRK" callout, flag for human review. The TS port matches the Java byte-for-byte until someone audits and chooses to change it. (4) Local repo lookups won big again — the TestBench-API survey in slice 123 read from `../flow/`'s `vaadin-testbench-shared` sources; the GWT-side `SheetWidget.java` survey in slice 124 read directly from `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-client` in this repo. Both were faster than online docs would have been.
- Slice 123 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.0 execution: capture run + diff IT + first 9 baselines.** Pays off slice 122's deferred work. **Shipped**: (1) `Phase30BaselineDiffIT.java` (~85 LOC) — loops the same 9 fixtures, reads each baseline via `ImageIO`, calls `getCommandExecutor().compareScreen(BufferedImage, errorName)`, aggregates failures + missing-baseline reports into a single assertion. Runs by default on every CI build (no system-property gate). (2) `pom.xml` testbench config — `screenshotComparisonTolerance=0.01`, `screenshotComparisonCursorDetection=true`, `maxScreenshotRetries=2`, `screenshotRetryDelay=500`, `screenshotReferenceDirectory=${project.basedir}/src/reference-screenshots`, `screenshotErrorDirectory=${project.build.directory}/screenshot-diffs`. Used `combine.children="append"` so the parent pom's existing testbench params (testsInParallel, SharedBrowser.reuseBrowser) are preserved. (3) **9 baseline PNGs** under `src/reference-screenshots/<slug>/1280x720-dpr1-chrome.png`. Each visually inspected: 8 show rich GWT-rendered content (Tagetik 5 production dashboard with line chart, merged cells with row labels A1:B2 + tooltips + 9-color cells, frozen panes with frozen markers, ConditionalFormatter heatmap with Top-20%/Above-Average/Bottom-10 callouts, spreadsheet_styles with bold/italic/colored cells, FormulaValues with embedded bar chart, data_filters with €-prefixed amounts and filter dropdown chevrons, scrolled_frozen with hidden-row markers); 2 show legitimately-sparse content (100_000_rows.xlsx with "File opened" in A1 + empty grid below as the perf-baseline fixture's true initial state, Groupingtest.xlsx with row+column grouping outlines visible at the edges + empty content as the grouping-fixture's true initial state). PNG sizes range 36KB-148KB. **Fixture list correction**: discovered mid-slice that the original top-10 fixture list contains 3 entries (`Simple Invoice.xlsx`, `Grouping.xlsx`, `Formulas.xlsx`) which live in `src/main/resources/testsheets/` but `SpreadsheetPage` loads only from `test_sheets/` (via `ClassLoader.getResource("test_sheets/")` at line 71-82). The `testsheets/` directory was added in vaadin/spreadsheet#770 (commit d6424aac5c) and never wired to any UI element. First capture-run attempt failed on fixture #1 with "Value 'Simple Invoice.xlsx' not found in the combobox". Resolution: substituted `Formulas.xlsx` → `FormulaValues.xlsx` and `Grouping.xlsx` → `Groupingtest.xlsx` (both extant in `test_sheets/`, same theme coverage per FIXTURE_INVENTORY); dropped `Simple Invoice.xlsx` outright (no clean substitute). The 10-fixture list became 9. The mismatch between FIXTURE_INVENTORY's recommendation and what SpreadsheetPage actually exposes is a slice 117 gap; CAPTURE_WORKFLOW.md documents the resolution + flags FIXTURE_INVENTORY.md for revision. **End-to-end loop validated**: re-ran capture (9 baselines wrote successfully, ~38s wall-clock); then ran the diff IT against the just-captured baselines → `Tests run: 1, Failures: 0, Errors: 0`. No screenshot-diffs/ directory was created (no mismatches). The 0.01 per-pixel tolerance + cursor detection handled the natural inter-run variation. **API surprise**: TestBench's `compareScreen` methods are on the `CanCompareScreenshots` interface implemented by `TestBenchCommandExecutor`, NOT directly on `TestBenchTestCase`. The diff IT calls `getCommandExecutor().compareScreen(BufferedImage, String)`. Also: the only available overloads are `compareScreen(String referenceId)` (uses ReferenceNameGenerator to append `_<platform>_<browser>_<version>`), `compareScreen(File reference)` (explicit file, no error-naming hint), and `compareScreen(BufferedImage reference, String referenceName)` (image + error name). I used the BufferedImage variant — it lets me deterministically resolve the reference path AND provides a clean error-name hint for `target/screenshot-diffs/<errorName>_*.png`. Confirmed via `../flow/`'s `vaadin-testbench-shared` sources (user-flagged pattern: prefer local sibling repos over m2-jar extraction for Vaadin API research). **Memory update**: renamed `media-graph-vaadin-repo-refs` memory to `vaadin-local-repos` (general, not project-specific) — applies to any Vaadin work I do, not just media-graph. **Total slice volume**: 1 new IT (~85 LOC), 1 pom config block (10 lines), 1 updated capture-IT (fixture list trim), 9 binary PNGs (~600 KB combined), CAPTURE_WORKFLOW.md updates (status, capture-vs-diff table, fixture caveat section). **Lesson banked**: when introducing a new test category to a repo (here, visual regression — never used in flow-components before), the FIRST run is going to surface infrastructure gaps that survey-style audits miss. Slice 117 catalogued 162 fixtures across `testsheets/` + `test_sheets/` but didn't notice that only one of those two directories is reachable via the demo. The audit framing was "are these files on disk?" — the right framing for Phase 3.0 was "are these files reachable through the capture path I'm about to wire up?" A pre-run grep against `SpreadsheetPage.java` for which directories it consumes would have caught this in slice 117 instead of slice 123. Pattern: when bootstrapping a NEW test category, the audit must trace the full input pipeline, not just enumerate the file set.
- Slice 122 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.0 execution: golden-screenshot capture infrastructure.** Original framing was "team-driven", then pivoted to manual Playwright MCP capture against running Jetty, then pivoted again to the TestBench-IT capture path after surveying the actual production pipeline. **Why the second pivot**: Playwright MCP works for one-off capture but is not the production diff pipeline — `INFRA_PROPOSAL.md` § 1.1 already locked in TestBench `compareScreen`. Bootstrapping baselines via a tool that won't match the diff capture would compare apples to oranges. So slice 122 ships the TestBench-based capture path. **Investigation findings**: (1) `AbstractSpreadsheetIT.loadFile(String)` already exists at line 329 — selects a fixture in the `#testSheetSelect` Vaadin combo + clicks `#update`, drives the existing demo page exactly the way human-operated tests do. The capture IT inherits this for free. (2) **No `compareScreen` usage anywhere in flow-components** (verified by `grep -r "compareScreen" --include="*.java"` returning empty) — Phase 3.0 introduces visual regression to this repo. The 6 IT classes that DO exist test interaction behavior via TestBench DOM queries, not pixel comparison. (3) **All 10 top-priority fixtures verified to exist on disk** (`Simple Invoice.xlsx`, `Tagetik 5.xlsx`, `Grouping.xlsx`, `100_000_rows.xlsx`, `merged_cells.xlsx`, `scrolled_frozen.xlsx`, `ConditionalFormatterSamples.xlsx`, `spreadsheet_styles.xlsx`, `Formulas.xlsx`, `data_filters.xlsx`) — split between `testsheets/` (5 fixtures) and `test_sheets/` (157 fixtures), no duplicates. (4) **TestBench `compareScreen` does NOT auto-write missing references** — it expects baselines to exist and fails when they don't. So the bootstrap path can't use `compareScreen` for first-time capture. **Shipped**: (1) `Phase30BaselineCaptureIT.java` (~95 LOC, extends `AbstractSpreadsheetIT`, gated behind `-Dphase3.baseline.capture=true` so it never runs in normal CI). Single `@Test captureTopPriorityFixtures()` method loops over the 10 priority fixtures, calls `loadFile(fixture)`, waits 2s for render, then writes the screenshot via `((TakesScreenshot) getDriver()).getScreenshotAs(OutputType.BYTES)` to `src/reference-screenshots/<slug>/1280x720-dpr1-chrome.png`. The slug helper drops the extension, lowercases, replaces non-alphanumerics with `-` ("Simple Invoice.xlsx" → "simple-invoice", "100_000_rows.xlsx" → "100-000-rows"). Overrides the inherited 1920x1080 window with 1280x720 per `INFRA_PROPOSAL.md` § 1.1's capture viewport decision. (2) `refactor-artifacts/tier3-phase-3.0/CAPTURE_WORKFLOW.md` runbook documenting the capture-vs-diff split (the capture IT bootstraps baselines once; a future diff IT runs `compareScreen` on every CI build), the bootstrap command (`mvn ... -Dphase3.baseline.capture=true verify`), how to regenerate single-fixture baselines (PR author proposes, reviewer counter-signs per § 1.1), the scaling path to the remaining 152 fixtures (~80 min wall-clock), the cross-axis filename convention `<slug>/<viewport>-<dpr>-<browser>.png` that reserves space for Firefox + retina add-ons, and the known limitations (font hinting OS-dependence, scrollbar visibility, animation jitter). (3) Updated INFRA_PROPOSAL.md to cross-reference the new IT + workflow doc (deferred — added to slice 123). **Did NOT ship** (intentionally): (a) The actual bulk run of the capture — that's a separate "verify the IT works against a real Jetty + commit the resulting PNGs" task best done as its own slice so the PNGs land in a focused diff-reviewable commit. (b) The companion diff IT that calls `compareScreen` against the baselines — that's slice 123. (c) testbench properties config in pom.xml for tolerance / reference dir — INFRA_PROPOSAL.md § 6 has the snippet, applying it is slice 123 along with the diff IT. **Why this scope split**: writing the capture infrastructure WITHOUT running it lets future review focus on "does this approach make sense?" without also reviewing 10 binary PNG additions. The run + commit-baselines flow becomes its own focused slice (Ugur or a future Claude session triggers it via the documented `-Dphase3.baseline.capture=true` command). **Lesson banked**: when a slice has both an "infrastructure" deliverable and an "execution result" deliverable, ship them as separate slices unless they're truly inseparable. The infra is review-by-code, the result is review-by-PNG-diff — different reviewer headspaces. Also banked the pivot pattern: when the user's "X is enough" guidance unblocks me from a stuck framing (here, "team-driven" was the stuck framing), USE X for unblocking but reassess whether X is the right LONG-term tool. Playwright MCP was right for "prove the capture path renders correctly" (which I did via 2 PoC screenshots earlier in the session, both deleted as workflow-verification artifacts); it was wrong for "be the production capture pipeline" because it doesn't compose with TestBench's diff path.
- Slice 121 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.2 kickoff: Spreadsheet skeleton class + SelectionState pure module.** First Phase 3.2 slice. The Phase 3.2 plan was 6 weeks of "cell grid + virtual scrolling" team-driven work — but the SCAFFOLDING for that work (the class skeleton, the selection-state shape) can be done without a running dev environment. **Skeleton class** at `lit/Spreadsheet.ts` — implements `SpreadsheetClientApi` (the 50-method interface from slice 120) with every method stubbed. Property setters write to private fields (the state model the Phase 3.2+ renderer will read from); the 12 explicit RPC methods + lifecycle hooks are TODO comments naming the phase that fills each one in. Read accessors (`getRows`, `getCols`, `getSheetIndex`, `getSheetNames`, `getSelection`, `getMergedRegions`, `isSheetProtected`) provide the test surface the team can exercise against. **The compile-time invariant**: because the class declares `implements SpreadsheetClientApi`, the TS compiler enforces that every one of the 50 interface methods exists. If a future slice forgets to add a method, the type-check fails. This is the load-bearing safety net the rewrite turns on — when Phase 3.6 flips the Lit wrapper's import from the 1.3 MB GWT blob to this class, the wrapper's existing call sites must keep compiling. **Selection state extraction** at `lit/state/SelectionState.ts` — pulled from the GWT side's `SelectionHandler.java` (1,093 LOC) plus the state-bearing fields on `SheetWidget.java`. Architecturally cleaner than the GWT version which scattered selection state across handler + widget. **TS interface**: `SelectionState { activeCell: CellPosition, activeRange: CellRange, auxRanges: CellRange[] }` — three layered concepts: the single active cell with keyboard focus, the most-recently-extended range containing it, and any auxiliary ranges added via Ctrl+Click. **Helper functions**: `selectSingleCell`, `selectRange`, `normalizedRange` (enforces `c1≤c2` and `r1≤r2`), `isCoherentSelection` (false iff aux ranges exist — the clipboard codec checks this), `addAuxRange` (Ctrl+Click: moves the current active range to aux, makes the new range active), `isCellSelected` (across active + all auxes), `isActiveCell`, `containsCell`, `iterateRange` (generator, row-major), `selectionBoundingBox`. All pure functions returning new state — no mutation. **Coordinates are 1-based throughout**, matching the rest of the API. **Tests**: 2 new test files. `lit-selection-state.test.ts` — 19 tests covering single-cell construction, range construction with corner normalization, anchor preservation on shift-extend, aux-range chaining + coherence-breakage, cell-membership checks across active + auxes, generator row-major order, bounding-box calculation including the "aux ranges extend in both directions" edge. `lit-spreadsheet-skeleton.test.ts` — 7 tests covering construction with a host element, setWidth/setHeight applying to the host's style, property-mirror round-trip (rows/cols, sheet index/names, merged regions, sheet-protected flag), all 12 RPC stubs callable without throwing, lifecycle stubs callable, popup-button stubs callable, initial selection state defaults to A1. The skeleton tests are smoke tests — they don't exercise rendering (that's Phase 3.2 cont.) but they pin the **contract that the class is constructable + every setter accepts its documented type + every method is callable**. The real value is COMPILE-TIME: if `Spreadsheet implements SpreadsheetClientApi` ever loses a method, the test file fails to type-check, surfacing the contract drift immediately. **Total slice volume**: 2 new TS source files (~580 lines), 2 test files (~310 lines, 26 tests), README update. **Lesson banked**: the skeleton-class-with-stubs pattern is exactly what's needed when porting a large API surface — the team picks ONE stub at a time to make real, and the compiler keeps the contract intact across many concurrent edits. This is the same pattern Vaadin's other modern components (Grid, ComboBox) used during their GWT→Lit migrations, but I learned about it from a different direction: the slice 117/118 architectural finding that "the Lit wrapper seam already exists; the rewrite swaps what's under it." The skeleton IS the seam-bottom; the wrapper is the seam-top.
- Slice 120 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.1 cont.: wire-protocol RPC types + TSV codec.** Third Phase 3.1 slice. Codifies the wire contract the Phase 3.6 swap depends on. **Surveyed the actual RPC surface** by grepping the existing Lit wrapper (`vaadin-spreadsheet.js`) for `this.api.X(...)` calls and `this.dispatchEvent(this.createEvent('...', e))` sites. **Findings**: 30+ property setters + 12 explicit RPC methods + 5 lifecycle methods = ~50-method client API surface; **39 distinct client→server event types** dispatched as `CustomEvent` on the host element. **Ported types** (slice 120): (1) `lit/types/rpc-server-to-client.ts` — `SpreadsheetClientApi` interface with all 50 methods grouped semantically (initial setup / 12 explicit RPCs / property mirrors / popup-button lifecycle). The 12 explicit RPC methods mirror `SpreadsheetClientRpc.java`'s typed-façade interface (clarified in slice 108). Property setters group by concern (sheet-level dimensions, sheet navigation, CSS styling, grouping, misc state) — the same grouping that slices 71-88 produced on the SERVER side via `SheetDimensions` / `SheetCssStyleMaps` / `SheetGrouping`. (2) `lit/types/rpc-client-to-server.ts` — `SpreadsheetEvent` discriminated union enumerating all 39 event types as a string-literal union, plus 17 strongly-typed per-event data interfaces for the most common cases (`OnSheetScrollData`, `CellSelectedData`, `CellRangeSelectedData`, `RowSelectedData`, `ColumnSelectedData`, `CellRangePaintedData`, `SelectionIncreasePaintedData`, `CellValueEditedData`, `SheetSelectedData`, `SheetRenamedData`, `LinkCellClickedData`, `RowsResizedData`, `ColumnResizedData`, `OnPasteData`, `GroupingCollapsedData`, `ContextMenuOpenData`, etc.). Plus a `SpreadsheetEventType` string-literal union of all 39 names (catches forgotten-event regressions at compile time when a new event is added). And an `asSpreadsheetEvent(event)` runtime narrower that handles the CustomEvent → SpreadsheetEvent conversion with type guards. **Why this matters**: the Phase 3.6 swap turns on the contract being honored — the new client must implement `SpreadsheetClientApi` exactly. Without these types, the rewrite would discover missing methods one by one at runtime. With them, the TypeScript compiler enforces completeness. **Ported algorithm**: `lit/algorithms/Tsv.ts` — clipboard tab-separated-values codec. `serializeToTsv(values)` byte-equivalent port of `CopyPasteHandlerImpl.getClipboardText` (the only direction the GWT client implemented — paste is delegated to the server). `parseTsv(text)` is the NEW inverse direction — the GWT client didn't have it because paste went straight to the server, but the new client may handle paste locally for trivial cases. Both functions are pure (no DOM, no state). Documented TSV's no-escaping convention (cells containing `\t` or `\n` are lossy in both directions — matches Excel's clipboard behavior). CRLF + classic-Mac CR line-ending normalization on the parse side for Windows-clipboard tolerance. **Tests**: 1 new test file `lit-tsv.test.ts` — 16 tests covering 1x1/1xN/Nx1/MxN grids, null cells, empty-string cells, no-trailing-newline contract, CRLF normalization, single-trailing-newline stripping, round-trip-with-null-normalization (nulls become empty strings on parse). **Total slice volume**: 3 new TS source files (~390 lines), 1 test file (~140 lines), 1 README update. **Lesson banked**: the RPC-type surface is the single most important "documentation as code" artifact for the Phase 3.2+ work. When the team starts implementing the new `Spreadsheet` class in Phase 3.2, they implement `SpreadsheetClientApi` — the compiler enforces that every method exists. When they fire client→server events, they construct a `SpreadsheetEvent` payload — the discriminated union enforces that the type literal matches one of the 39 known event types AND the data field matches that type's data shape. This is exactly the pattern Vaadin's other modern components (Grid, ComboBox) use, and exactly what slice 116's TIER3_PLAN identified as "the seam between the Lit wrapper and the new TS code."
- Slice 119 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.1 cont.: formula-ref extraction + cell-address parser.** Second slice of the Phase 3.1 pure-algorithm port work. **Investigation first**: surveyed remaining client-side pure helpers. Found that SequenceIncrement / SequenceValueExtractor / RangeSequenceDetector are **server-side only** (the GWT client doesn't reference them — `grep -rln "sequenceIncrement|sequenceValue|RangeSequence|autofill|dragfill|fillHandle" client/` returned zero hits). The autofill/drag-fill UI logic on the GWT client side sends events to the server, which then uses the sequence helpers to compute the increment server-side. So **no port needed** for those. Skip. **Formula-ref extraction lives in `FormulaBarWidget.java`** — the 16-color highlighting algorithm. Three private static helpers: `parseSingleCell` (string "A1" → CellCoord), `parseSingleCellRef` (parses "A1" or "A1:B3" or "Sheet2!A1" → MergedRegion or null), `parseCellReferences` (formula split → list of cell-ref tokens), and `isCellRef` (regex-test against the workbook's sheet names). The GWT-side `cachedRegex` 2-second-timer cache trick is unnecessary in modern JS — regex compilation is fast. **Ported subsystems** (slice 119): (1) `lit/algorithms/CellRef.ts` — `parseSingleCell(cellRef)` returns `{col, row}` with the same base-26 column-letter math as GWT (`A=1`, `Z=26`, `AA=27`, `ZZ=702`, `AAA=703`); `columnIndexToLetters(col)` inverts (1→"A", 27→"AA"); `cellCoordToAddress(coord)` formats; `parseCellRefRange(ref, activeSheetName)` handles sheet-qualified refs + ranges + single-cells, returning `null` when the ref is for a different sheet. (2) `lit/algorithms/FormulaRefs.ts` — `parseCellReferences(formula, sheetNames)` extracts cell-ref tokens in document order (duplicates preserved, ranges as single tokens not split on `:`); `isCellRef(token, sheetNames)` is the predicate. **Behavior-preservation note**: the `[A-z]` regex character class in the original Java is INTENTIONALLY wider than `[A-Za-z]` — it includes `[`, `\`, `]`, `^`, `_`, `` ` ``. Preserved verbatim in TS so the new client matches the GWT widget's token-extraction byte-for-byte. **Behavior-improvement flagged**: the GWT version didn't escape regex metacharacters in sheet names — a sheet named `"Q1.2026"` would have its `.` treated as "any char" in the cell-ref regex, so `"Q1X2026!A1"` would falsely match. The TS port escapes via a small `escapeRegex(name)` helper. Logged as "Open question" in the FormulaRefs.ts file's javadoc — should the new client preserve the GWT bug for byte-equivalence, or fix it? Fix seems obviously correct; flagging for review. **Tests**: 2 new test files — `lit-cell-ref.test.ts` (19 tests covering single-letter cols, multi-letter cols up to AAA703, multi-digit rows, lowercase tolerance, null/empty input, round-trip parse→format, range parsing, sheet-qualified parsing with active-sheet filter), `lit-formula-refs.test.ts` (18 tests covering plain refs, range refs, sheet-qualified refs, unknown-sheet rejection, non-cell-ref tokens, function-name + operator skipping, document-order + duplicate preservation, regex-metachar escape in sheet names). **Total slice volume**: 2 new TS source files (~290 lines), 2 test files (~280 lines), README update. **Lesson banked**: the "investigate before porting" step paid off — I would have wasted ~1 hour porting SequenceIncrement et al. if I hadn't grep'd for client-side usage first. Confirms the pattern from earlier slices: assume the plan might be wrong → check → execute the right thing.
- Slice 118 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.1 kickoff: TS/Lit module scaffolding + first subsystems ported.** Phase 3.0's team-driven work (screenshot capture, tolerance tuning) needs a running server + CI workers, so it can't progress in a session — but Phase 3.1's first half is parallelizable. **Setup**: created new `lit/` directory under the existing `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow/src/main/resources/META-INF/resources/frontend/vaadin-spreadsheet/` (alongside the existing Lit wrapper element `vaadin-spreadsheet.js`). Organized as `types/` (TS interfaces), `algorithms/` (pure-algorithm subsystems), `wire/` (JSON parsing helpers), plus a README explaining the structure. **Critical architectural insight surfaced**: the Lit wrapper element ALREADY exists and the Tier 3 rewrite swaps what's UNDER it, not the wrapper itself. The wrapper imports `Spreadsheet` from `./spreadsheet-export.js` (the 1.3 MB GWT blob); when Phase 3.6 ships, that import flips to point at the new bundled TS code. **Ported subsystems**: (1) **Subsystem E — MergedRegion + MergedRegionUtil** (~280 LOC Java → ~310 LOC TS). `MergedRegion.ts` provides `createMergedRegion()` and `createMergedRegionFromCorners(c1, r1, c2, r2)` factory functions (the Java class becomes a wire interface — no behavior, just shape) plus the `MergedRegionContainer` interface mirroring the Java lookup contract. `MergedRegionUtil.ts` ports `findIncreasingSelection` byte-equivalent — same 4-edge walk pattern (top/right/bottom/left), same `trouble` flag, same recursive tail, same inner clamps after each edge. Comments explicitly cite the Phase 3 spec invariants (`MergedRegionLookup`, `MergedRegionCascade`) so the cross-reference is durable. (2) **Wire-format parser** — the original Java `Parser.java` (185 LOC of generic JSON-to-Java-bean copying via JsInterop's `Object.assign` JSNI trick) collapses to ~180 LOC of clean TS because `JSON.parse` produces objects whose shape already matches our wire interfaces. No `copyJsToJava` reflection needed. 13 functions covering all the parse shapes Java had (`parseArrayOfCellData`, `parseArrayOfMergedRegion`, `parseArrayOfSpreadsheetActionDetails`, `parseArrayOfPopupButtonState`, `parsePopupButtonState`, `parseArrayOfGroupingData`, `parseMapStringOverlayInfo`, generic helpers, typed arrays `Int32Array`/`Float32Array`, `Map<number,*>`, `Set<*>`). Empty-input handling matches Java verbatim — `null`/`""`/`"null"` all map to empty arrays / null maps / empty sets. (3) **TS interfaces for all 6 wire DTOs** matching slice 106's `DtoWireFormatTest` snapshots exactly. `CellData` (10 fields, value-always-present per `@JsonInclude`), `MergedRegion` (5 fields), `OverlayInfo` (type as `'IMAGE'|'COMPONENT'` literal union mirroring the Java enum's `name()` wire format), `SpreadsheetActionDetails` (type as `number` preserving server's int over client's String — the wire-cross-type tolerance via Jackson + JSON.parse), `PopupButtonState` (server-side POJO shape only — the AbstractComponentState legacy fields are absent because NON_DEFAULT inclusion guarantees the server never emits them), `GroupingData` (5 fields). **Tests**: 2 test files in the IT module's `test/` directory using Web Test Runner + Chai (the vaadin-grid-flow convention). `lit-merged-region-util.test.ts` — 11 tests covering single-cell, exact-match shortcut, multi-cell-without-overlap, all 4 edge expansions individually, recursive expansion across two adjacent regions, and the createMergedRegion helper's "returns fresh object each call" contract. `lit-wire-parser.test.ts` — 17 tests covering empty-input handling for every shape + the all-fields wire-snapshots from `DtoWireFormatTest` (verifying byte-equivalent round-trip Java→JSON→TS) + cross-type preservation (int stays int) + the generic-shape helpers. **Build infrastructure**: added `web-test-runner.config.mjs` to the IT module (one-liner that imports the shared config), matching vaadin-grid-flow's pattern exactly. **What this enables**: the team-driven Phase 3.1 cont. work can now extend the same pattern — pick a pure-algorithm subsystem, port byte-equivalent, write Web-Test-Runner tests, commit. The hard subsystems (virtual scrolling A, GWT connector M) still need Phases 3.2 and 3.4 respectively. **Total slice volume**: 4 new TS source files, 2 test files, 1 config file, 1 README — ~1100 lines of TS + ~250 lines of tests + docs. **Lesson banked**: the GWT→TS port for pure-algorithm code is dramatically simpler than expected because (a) the Java side was already JSNI-bridge-heavy, (b) `JSON.parse` does what `copyJsToJava` did, and (c) TS's structural typing means wire DTOs become plain interfaces without runtime ceremony. The 30-50% LOC reduction we got on the Parser port is repeatable — every pure-algorithm Java helper that's currently JSNI-encrusted will likely shrink that much on the TS side. The hard subsystems (virtual scrolling, frozen panes) are the ones where the LOC stays roughly the same because the complexity isn't in the JSNI ceremony, it's in the algorithm.
- Slice 117 — **Tier 3 Phase 3.0 kickoff: feature inventory + fixture audit + infra proposal.** Started the actual execution of Tier 3. Phase 3.0 is the visual safety net before any code is rewritten. **Three deliverables** under new `refactor-artifacts/tier3-phase-3.0/` subdirectory + a README to navigate them: (1) `FEATURE_INVENTORY.md` — the parity bar; cross-references the 13 GWT subsystems (slice 116) + 77 IT test classes + 56 Phase 3 specs into ~50 distinct features, each with Phase-3-spec link + IT class + example fixture + acceptance criterion. Coverage: 48/50 features have IT coverage today (96%); 2 gaps identified (Sheet visibility F.4 + custom-editor fixtures K.1). (2) `FIXTURE_INVENTORY.md` — categorizes the **162 existing `.xlsx`/`.xls` fixtures** in `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-integration-tests/src/main/resources/{testsheets,test_sheets}/` into 16 themes (basic display / styling / conditional formatting / merged cells / frozen panes / cell shifting / grouping / multiple sheets / hyperlinks / named ranges / filter tables / images / charts (32 fixtures — biggest theme) / big files / edge cases / locale-rounding). Includes a recommended **golden-capture order** — top 10 fixtures (`Simple Invoice.xlsx`, `Tagetik 5.xlsx`, `Grouping.xlsx`, `100_000_rows.xlsx`, `merged_cells.xlsx`, `scrolled_frozen.xlsx`, `ConditionalFormatterSamples.xlsx`, `spreadsheet_styles.xlsx`, `Formulas.xlsx`, `data_filters.xlsx`) cover all 13 subsystems at least once, so the early captures get maximum parity-bar leverage. Identifies 6 fixture gaps (3 custom-editor fixtures, 1 Vaadin-component-overlay fixture, 1 mixed-visibility-sheets fixture, 1 perf-baseline fixture) — small additions during Phase 3.0. (3) `INFRA_PROPOSAL.md` — concrete tooling decisions: **TestBench's built-in `compareScreen` API** is the recommended diff tool (zero new tooling cost; already on the IT classpath at testbench-25.2.0-beta1; matches the pattern used by other Vaadin components). Considered + rejected: Playwright `toHaveScreenshot` (disruptive — two harnesses for one module; kept as a fallback if tolerance pain dominates), odiff (Rust CLI — integration cost exceeds speed benefit), pixelmatch + resemble.js (TestBench already does it). **Storage**: `src/test/resources/golden-screenshots/<fixture>/<viewport-key>.png` via Git LFS (~50 MB initially, 162 fixtures × 2 browsers × 1 dpr = 324 PNGs). **Tolerance**: 0.05 per-pixel diff default (TestBench convention), tunable per fixture via metadata JSON. **Browsers**: Chromium-headless + Firefox-headless for Phase 3.0; add Safari + `dpr=2` + mobile viewports in Phase 3.5. **Build integration**: new `visual-regression` Maven profile + `-Dscreenshot.refresh=true` workflow for golden regen. **CI**: per-PR job runs the profile; nightly noise-floor job catches CI-worker drift. **Critical architectural finding from the audit**: there's NO existing visual-regression infrastructure ANYWHERE in flow-components (verified via grep across the monorepo) — Vaadin's other components don't have screenshot tests yet, so Phase 3.0's infrastructure may end up being the reference for future component rewrites. **Phase 3.0 acceptance criteria** documented in INFRA_PROPOSAL § 7 (8 conditions). **6 open decisions for operator** documented in § 8 (TestBench-vs-Playwright, Git LFS approval, browser matrix cut, tolerance default, CI budget, goldens-regen owner). **Total Phase 3.0 scaffolding**: ~1100 lines across 4 docs. The team-driven part (actual golden capture, tolerance tuning, gap-fixture authoring) is now ready to start with all decisions framed. **Lesson banked**: the slice-117 audit produced two surprising findings that should sharpen any future "Phase 3.x kickoff" — (a) the fixture corpus was MUCH larger than estimated (162 vs the 30-50 the plan assumed), so the work is "systematize existing fixtures" not "build a corpus"; (b) there's zero visual-regression precedent in the broader flow-components monorepo, so Phase 3.0's tooling decision sets a baseline for the whole project. Both flip the framing from "build new" to "first-of-kind for a shared problem." The pattern is the same as every audit-driven slice this session: deep look reveals the actual landscape, then the plan adjusts.
- Slice 116 — **Tier 3 deep examination: GWT rewrite paths + OSS landscape + sharpened back-compat rule.** Started with the operator's question about whether a TS/Lit client rewrite breaks existing spreadsheets given the back-compat rule that said "old artifacts must render identically." The literal reading is "you can never rewrite a renderer" — wrong; the constraint is about **content + styling-intent preservation**, not pixel-perfect rendering across UI tech changes. **Skill update**: the `code-modernization` skill's back-compat section now splits into two levels — Level 1 (non-negotiable: file loads, data preserved bit-equivalently, styling intent honored, forward-write compatibility) and Level 2 (NOT promised: exact font rendering, 1px-vs-0.5px borders at certain devicePixelRatios, scrollbar appearance, selection-highlight emulation). Safety nets differ: Level 1 = byte/semantic equality tests + payload snapshots; Level 2 = golden-screenshot diffs with tolerance thresholds. **Tier 3 deep examination**: launched a parallel `Explore` agent to map the 105-file GWT client into subsystems. **Findings**: 13 major subsystems totaling ~22.6 K LOC; 2 of them at 4/5 difficulty (subsystem A virtual scrolling — frozen panes + DOM recycling + scroll sync; subsystem M GWT connector + state delegation — hardest piece). 10 specific Excel-quirk emulation areas (frozen panes, selection outline, virtual scrolling with dynamic heights, cell-overflow detection, formula-reference highlighting, etc.). **Critical architectural finding**: the `<vaadin-spreadsheet>` Lit wrapper element already exists (19 KB at `src/main/resources/META-INF/resources/frontend/vaadin-spreadsheet/vaadin-spreadsheet.js`); it delegates to the 1.3 MB GWT blob via `this.api`. The Tier 3 rewrite doesn't have to touch the integration surface — it swaps what's beneath the Lit wrapper, not the wrapper itself. **OSS-landscape evaluation**: 7 candidates evaluated (Univer, Handsontable, Luckysheet/Fortune-Sheet, x-spreadsheet, Glide Data Grid, AG Grid, Jspreadsheet/jExcel). All rejected for the same blocker — custom-editor / Vaadin-component-in-cell integration. Vaadin's unique value is putting `<vaadin-combo-box>` / `<vaadin-date-picker>` etc. into spreadsheet cells; OSS spreadsheet libraries are designed to be standalone, not framework-integrated. Adapter cost on top of the library's mismatched API exceeds the in-house cost. **Other options evaluated and rejected**: Option C (hybrid — Lit shell + OSS rendering core) introduces two-system integration complexity exceeding the saved work. Option D (canvas-based renderer) has prohibitive accessibility cost (parallel ARIA shadow DOM) + inconsistency with Vaadin's DOM-rendered ecosystem. Option E (j2cl / GWT 3 / Kotlin-JS) has toolchain risk + no Vaadin-Flow precedent. Option F (defer) accrues maintenance-debt interest (GWT 2.10 is in shrinking maintenance window). **Recommended path**: **Option A — in-house TS/Lit rewrite, phased over ~5-6 months (team of 2-3)**. Six phases: 3.0 safety net + golden-screenshot corpus (2-3 wk), 3.1 foundation + pure-algorithm subsystems + wire-protocol adapter (4 wk), 3.2 cell grid + scrolling — critical path (6 wk), 3.3 editing + formula bar (4 wk), 3.4 Vaadin integration — custom editors are highest-risk (4 wk), 3.5 polish + parity (3 wk), 3.6 rollout with feature flag + beta (3 wk). **Borrow ideas from OSS without taking the library**: Lit Virtualizer for vertical scrolling; native browser virtualization (`content-visibility: auto`, CSS containment); Glide Data Grid's row-virtualization patterns. Reduces subsystem A's LOC by 20-30% without third-party dependency. **Deliverable**: new `refactor-artifacts/TIER3_PLAN.md` (10 sections, ~500 lines) — current state recap, constraints, options analysis, recommendation, phased roadmap, risk register (10 risks), open questions for the operator, acceptance criteria. The modernization plan's Tier 3 section now defers to this document. **Lesson banked**: this is the **most thorough scoping piece this session** — the user explicitly asked "do not cut corners" and the deep look uncovered three load-bearing facts that change the framing: (1) the Lit wrapper seam already exists, (2) OSS libraries all share the same blocker (custom-editor integration), (3) the back-compat rule was misphrased and needed Level-1/Level-2 split. The pattern is the same as slices 99-115: assumptions → deep look → reality is different → plan reframed. Tier 3 is now a real project brief, not a hand-wavy mention.
- Slice 115 — **Modernization Tier 1 Step 4 follow-up: NullAway-integration cost audit + deferral.** The plan's Step 4 follow-up said "full `@NullMarked` rollout — best done after a NullAway config is added." Slice 115 mapped the actual cost. **Source-of-truth audit**: read upstream `vaadin/flow/flow-server/pom.xml` (the only flow-* module that currently uses NullAway). The integration config is ~50 lines of pom.xml per module: errorprone-plugin compiler-args (`-Xplugin:ErrorProne -XepDisableAllChecks -Xep:NullAway:ERROR -XepOpt:NullAway:JSpecifyMode=true -XepOpt:NullAway:OnlyNullMarked=true`), 12 `--add-opens` / `--add-exports` JDK module flags for javac internals access, `error_prone_core` 2.50.0 + `nullaway` 0.13.6 annotation-processor deps, and the `<fork>true</fork>` JVM-isolation flag. Build-time overhead per ErrorProne's documented baseline is 30-50% slower per compile. **Ecosystem-precedent finding**: zero flow-components siblings have adopted NullAway. `vaadin-grid-flow`, `vaadin-button-flow`, etc. — all use plain javadoc nullness. Adding NullAway ONLY to `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow` would diverge from 30+ siblings, exactly the "third pattern dilutes consistency" pitfall in the code-modernization skill. **Useful-coverage prerequisite**: `OnlyNullMarked=true` mode means unmarked packages don't get checked. Every `@NullMarked` package needs a full audit of its public-API contracts before marking — otherwise false positives flood the build. Slice 102's surgical `@Nullable` annotations gave the root package a head start (7 sites covered), but the other 11 subpackages (action / charts / charts/converter/* / client / command / framework / rpc / shared) would each need their own audit pass. **Recommendation banked into the plan**: defer until either (a) the broader flow-components project adopts NullAway at the parent-pom level, OR (b) a focused Spreadsheet-only effort accepts the 50-line cost + the audit discipline. The plan write-up captures the full rollout procedure for future-me — copy upstream's pom block, add `package-info.java` per subpackage in leaf-first dependency order, audit per-package, let compile errors drive the per-package to-do list. **No code changes in slice 115** — it's a scoping commit that closes the Step 4 follow-up question with concrete data. **Lesson banked**: this is the **third "audit yielded short ship list" outcome** this session (after slice 103's POI reflection audit: 2 of 4 sites convertible; slice 107's records audit: 1 of dozens convertible). The pattern is: "modernization plan author wrote ambitious item → deep look revealed the prerequisite cost is significant → cost is documented, decision is deferred." The plan and the journal jointly serve as the cost-data archive — future-me can re-evaluate without re-running the audit.
- Slice 114 — **Phase 3 spec batch 6: final 9 specs — Phase 3 backlog closed.** Wrote `ColumnAutofitter` (POI NPE-silent-skip + filter-button +16px padding + WeakHashMap cache), `ColumnSizeOperations` (width-0-is-hidden routing + un-hide-reloads-cell-data-but-re-hide-doesn't asymmetry), `RowSizeOperations` (twin of column ops with the `reloadSheetStyles(true, true)` asymmetry), `MergedRegionSynchronizer` (POI is source-of-truth, cache rebuilds to POI), `RuleBorderTracker` (4-way side asymmetry top/left spillover, right/bottom local + NONE-special-case grey override), `FreezePaneState` (4-field state-with-mirror; int[] equality blocks record migration — flagged in slice 107's record audit), `FontStyleComposer` (emit-only-if-different optimization with static `styleFontFamily` for baseline-capture reuse), `DefaultStyleBaseline` (one-shot-per-workbook capture with double-duty CSS-emission contract), `SheetOverlayInfoBuilder` (DONT_MOVE_AND_RESIZE absolute-pixel branch + EMU_PER_PIXEL-vs-EMU_PER_POINT axis distinction). **Phase 3 backlog: 0 specs remaining.** Total spec count: **17 → 56 (+39 across slices 109-114).** Six batches: complex orchestrators (3), predicates / dispatchers (4), popup-button + table families (7), sheet-* family (8), cell-cluster + custom-component (8), final cleanup (9). Plus the new `refactor-spec-write` skill landed in batch 3 documenting the format + workflow + pitfall list for future spec-writers.
- Slice 113 — **Phase 3 spec batch 5: cell-cluster + custom-component family (8 specs).** Wrote specs for two parallel loader/scanner pairs (`CellCommentLoader` / `CellCommentRangeScanner`, `HyperlinkLoader` / `HyperlinkRangeScanner`) and the custom-component cluster (`CustomComponentLoader` / `CustomComponentRangeScanner` / `CustomComponentRowSizer`), plus `CellFitCalculator`. Key invariants pinned: the comment loader's `topLeftLoaded` latch (frozen corner never scrolls out, so scan it once per lifetime); the hyperlink scanner's `XmlValueDisconnectedException` benign-skip; the custom-component loader's retention-reconciliation logic (keep components that are EITHER in scan OR still claimed by the editor map — preserves `StateNode` identity across scrolls); the row-sizer's defensive-copy + restore-from-POI-with-default-fallback pattern; the fit calculator's "no ratio → assume fit" first-paint default + the empirical 4px padding constant. The pane-walk pattern (4 quadrants for top-left frozen + top-right frozen + bottom-left frozen + main) is duplicated across the comment loader, hyperlink loader, and custom-component loader — flagged in 3 specs as a deferred ViewportPaneWalker extraction.
- Slice 112 — **Phase 3 spec batch 4: sheet-* family (8 specs).** Wrote specs for the entire Sheet* helper cluster: `SheetCssStyleMaps` (6-field state-with-mirror + the intentional exclusion of `cellStyleToCSSStyle` which stays on Spreadsheet for subclass compatibility), `SheetGrouping` (load-bearing row-vs-col asymmetry — row data is write-only with no server-side cache), `SheetLifecycleOperations` (create+delete with validation cascade + the always-reload-on-delete invariant covering cross-sheet formula references), `SheetVisibilityOperations` (the active-sheet-hide cascade + the counter-intuitive "hiding-the-last-sheet decrements-the-visible-index" rule), `SheetChangeDispatcher` (the scroll-stash-BEFORE-activate ordering invariant + the defensive `Math.abs` on initial state + the comment-pinned "rename intentionally doesn't fire a change event"), `SheetMetadataOperations` (rename with conditional visible-names mirror — only updates the mirror for non-hidden sheets), `SheetDimensions` (the 6-field cell-geometry state with live-reference-not-defensive-copy contract — same as PopupButtonRegistry / TableRegistry from batch 3), `SheetScrollMemory` (the defensive-copy-on-every-call requirement for both vertical AND horizontal arrays — slice 66's test pins reference preservation).
- Slice 111 — **Phase 3 spec batch 3: popup-button family + table-lifecycle (7 specs) + NEW refactor-spec-write SKILL.** Wrote 4 specs covering the popup-button family with explicit cross-references — `PopupButtonRegistry` (cell-to-button storage + absolute-ref convention + live-collection contract), `PopupButtonBinding` (virtual-child attach + JS-bridge dispatch + parent-guard for re-parented nodes + always-fire-JS-call invariant), `PopupButtonRefresher` (post-scroll re-attach decision + sheet-name filter gate + the 1-based-vs-0-based offset that's load-bearing for `isCellVisible`), `PopupButtonRowShifter` (tear-down-then-rebuild pattern + the `rowWasRemoved` half-open range + atomic `replaceAll`). And 3 specs covering the table-family: `TableRegistry` (the load-latch + `getAll()`-returns-live contract), `TableLoader` (latch-first short-circuit + active-sheet equals-not-reference filter), `TableLifecycle` (registry-remove-unconditional + active-sheet-gates-cleanup + the slice-100 pattern-matching idiom for the filter-table branch). **New skill landed**: `.claude/skills/refactor-spec-write/SKILL.md` — codifies the Phase 3 spec format used across slices 25, 28, 35, 41, 44, 46, 47, 50, 52, 70, 91, 92, 96 + this run's batches. Template: Purpose / Inputs / Outputs / Side effects / Invariants / Open questions. Pitfall list drawn from observed authoring mistakes (re-stating source, treating javadoc as ground truth, over-specifying implementation, forgetting cross-references). The new skill is dogfooded by slices 112-114.
- Slice 110 — **Phase 3 spec batch 2: predicates + sheet-lifecycle helpers (4 specs).** Wrote specs for `ActiveSheetSelector` (the visible-index ↔ POI-index translation + the 8-step activation cascade pinned in slice 75), `CellDeletionGuard` (3 deletion-shape predicates + "pass when nobody intercepts" semantics + the two short-circuit paths in `passesDeletionCheck`), `POIReflectionTools` (the slice-103 narrowing — 2 of 4 reflection bridges now use POI 5+ public API; the other 2 stay reflective with rationale), `DirectionalShiftOperator` (the load-bearing clear-order asymmetry between row variants (style-first) and column variants (value-first) + the null-source asymmetry).
- Slice 109 — **Phase 3 spec batch 1: complex orchestrators (3 specs).** Wrote the three highest-value-gap specs first: `RuleEvaluator` (the type-coherence matrix + the relative-reference delta handling + the slice-100 pattern-matching idioms across `matchesValue` / `matchesFormula`), `CellBorderStyleBuilder` (the 5-map transferred-border indirection — cell-style-index keyed, not cell-position keyed + the XSSF-theme `ApplyBorder == false` branch shared with `XSSFColorConverter`), `SheetOverlayManager` (the deferred-flush `reloadFromPoi` flag + visibility-transition tracking via wrapper state + the empty-storage lazy-load branch documented as a "kludge" for freeze interactions).
- Slice 108 — **Modernization Tier 2 Step 9c: clarify façade interfaces via javadoc (NOT rename — public API).** Scoping audit before executing the planned rename revealed that `SpreadsheetHandler`, `SpreadsheetServerRpc`, `SpreadsheetClientRpc`, and `GroupingHandler` are all `public interface` — i.e. PART of the public API. Plus `SpreadsheetHandlerImpl` is `public class` and is referenced by the public `Spreadsheet.setSpreadsheetHandler(SpreadsheetHandlerImpl spreadsheetHandler)` method — meaning any downstream consumer can subclass it to customize client-event handling. Renaming these types would be a breaking public-API change, exactly the same pitfall as slice 100's "seal Command" pivot and the code-modernization skill's "Sealing hierarchies you don't fully own" rule. **Pivot from rename to javadoc clarification**: same documentation outcome, zero API breakage. Added class-level javadoc to all 5 files (`SpreadsheetClientRpc`, `SpreadsheetServerRpc`, `SpreadsheetHandler`, `GroupingHandler`, `SpreadsheetHandlerImpl`) explaining what they actually are: typed façades around Vaadin Flow's modern Element API (`getElement().callJsFunction(...)`) + DomEvent (`@DomEvent("spreadsheet-event")` carrying `type` + `JsonNode` payload). Every javadoc says explicitly "NOT legacy Vaadin 8 RPC — see MODERNIZATION_PLAN.md Tier 2 Step 10" so a future reader (or me in 6 months) can stop hunting for the legacy machinery that doesn't exist. **Gates**: suite **1058 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged. Spotless re-flowed the multi-line javadocs (cosmetic, no semantic change). Checkstyle clean. **LOC delta**: +94 javadoc lines across 5 files. **Lesson banked**: this is the **second time this session** that "rename for clarity" pivoted to "clarify in javadoc" because the rename would have broken the public API. The first was slice 100 (Command-sealing → pattern matching). The pattern is: **when a name is misleading but in the public API, the cheap fix is documenting why the name is what it is, not changing it**. The code-modernization skill's "modern way tunnel" pitfall already covers this for pattern-choice cases; the same logic applies to naming choices.
- Slice 107 — **Modernization Tier 1 Step 7: records audit + selective migration.** Audit found ONE clean record candidate, not the dozens the plan's "audit per-class" framing implied. The plan suggested `CellValueChangeEvent` and `SheetChangeEvent` as candidates — both wrong: they extend `ComponentEvent`, and **records can't extend classes**. Same blocker for the other 5 inner event classes (`SpreadsheetEvent`, `FormulaValueChangeEvent`, `SelectionChangeEvent`, `ProtectedEditEvent`, `RowHeaderDoubleClickEvent`). **Audit inventory** — every candidate found, with its blocker: `ViewportBounds` blocked by `update(...)` mutator; `ViewportBufferConfig` blocked by setters with element-property side effects; `SeriesPoint` blocked by `item.xValue = cellValue` post-construction mutation in `AbstractSeriesReader` (lines 344, 348, 352); `BackgroundProperties` / `GradientProperties` / `AxisProperties` / `View3dData` / `TextProperties` / `TitleProperties` / `LegendProperties` all blocked by builder-style incremental field population (the chart-data reader pipeline sets fields one at a time as it parses XML); `BorderStyle` additionally blocked by anonymous-subclass usage in `ChartStylesReader.java:182` (records are implicitly final); `ColorParameters` in `ColorUtils.java` blocked by builder-style mutation in `createGradientProperties`; `RowData.CellData` and `RowData.CommentData` blocked by the `read(Cell)`/`writeTo(Cell)` setter pattern; the 101 Phase 1 extracted helpers are all orchestrators (not value objects) with collaborator references + side effects. **The one winner**: `ChartData.ColorProperties` (4 fields: red/green/blue/opacity). Verified no subclassing, no Mockito mocking, no post-construction mutation anywhere — the single read site at `ChartDataToVaadinConfigWriter.java:244-245` uses only the constructor and direct field reads. Converted to `public record ColorProperties(int red, int green, int blue, double opacity)` with a compact secondary constructor `ColorProperties(int[] rgbUnsignedWithLum, double opacity)` that delegates to the canonical constructor — preserves the existing call site `new ColorProperties(rgbArray, alpha)`. Updated the one reader to use accessor methods (`colorProp.red()` instead of `colorProp.red`). **Gates**: suite **1058 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged. Spotless + checkstyle clean. **LOC delta**: net **−1 line** (record shorthand saves 3 lines on the class body but accessor calls cost 2 lines on the reader). **Skill updated**: the `code-modernization` skill's "records-over-DTOs reflex" pitfall has been expanded with a full 6-pattern blocker checklist (extends-class, post-construction mutation, builder-style population, anonymous-subclass usage, mutators-with-side-effects, read-into-mutate pattern), drawing directly from this audit's findings. **Lesson banked**: the plan's framing of "Step 7 — records for SERVER-ONLY DTOs (selective)" was correct in principle but wrong in volume estimation. Almost no records-suitable classes exist in this codebase because the legacy patterns are pervasive: events extend framework classes, DTOs are built incrementally by readers, helpers carry collaborator references. The audit-with-blocker-inventory is the right deliverable; the migration itself is one file. This pattern — **deep audit yields short ship list** — is the same pattern as slice 103's POIReflectionTools work (2 of 4 reflection sites convertible) and slice 102's @Nullable surgery (8 of 200+ public methods documented). **The deep look's primary product is the explicit "won't migrate, here's why" list**; the actual migrations are secondary.
- Slice 106 — **Modernization Tier 2 Step 9 (sharpened): per-DTO wire-format characterization tests.** Sixth in-slice plan correction this session. Before executing, ran `diff` on each of the 6 server-vs-client DTO pairs to verify the scoping doc's premise that they were "mirror copies that need a generator". The diff showed the premise was wrong — every pair has intentional structural differences: `CellData` server uses Jackson `@JsonInclude`, client uses `@JsType`; `MergedRegion` client adds `@JsIgnore` on two methods; **`OverlayInfo` server uses a `Type` enum (IMAGE/COMPONENT), client uses String constants with the same values** — wire-compatible because Jackson serializes enum as `name()` which matches the client's String; **`SpreadsheetActionDetails` server has `int iconNodeId`, client has `String iconNodeId`** — wire-compatible because Jackson serializes int as a JSON number which the GWT client coerces; **`PopupButtonState` client extends `AbstractComponentState` (Vaadin-8-era base class!), server is a plain POJO** — wire-compatible because `Serializer`'s `NON_DEFAULT` inclusion ensures the server never emits the base-class fields the client would silently inherit. So a "generate client from server" pass would force POJO shape on both sides and break the cross-type tolerance the wire currently relies on. **Sharpened Step 9**: instead of a generator, write per-DTO Jackson wire-format characterization tests pinning the exact JSON bytes `Serializer.serialize(...)` produces. Any future field-rename, default-value change, or `@JsonInclude` edit on the server side fails the test at PR time — before silently breaking the GWT client. **New `DtoWireFormatTest`** with 8 tests covering all 6 DTOs plus two implicit-behavior pins: alphabetical-field-order (Jackson default) and NON_DEFAULT-compares-to-type-default-not-field-initializer-default (so `cellStyle = "cs0"` IS on the wire because `"cs0" != null`). Notable per-test pins: `cellData_freshInstance_emitsCellStyleAndNullValue` — pins that the `@JsonInclude` annotation on `CellData.value` with no argument defaults to `Include.ALWAYS`, overriding the global NON_DEFAULT for that field, so null `value` IS sent (load-bearing for the GWT client which expects the property to exist). `overlayInfo_freshInstance_emitsTypeIMAGE` — pins that the enum's IMAGE field-initializer default IS emitted because Jackson NON_DEFAULT compares to null for object types, not to the initializer. `popupButtonState_serializesOnlyServerSideFields` — pins that the server's POJO shape doesn't accidentally adopt the client's AbstractComponentState parent-class fields. **Two iterations of the test file**: first version had expected JSONs in declaration order and an empty-instance test expecting `{}`; both were wrong because (a) Jackson alphabetizes, and (b) `cellStyle`/`value` are always emitted. Watching the failing assertions report the *actual* output was the cleanest way to derive the correct snapshots — exactly the characterization-test workflow we use for refactors. **Gates**: suite **1050 → 1058** (+8 new), spotless re-flowed the test file (multi-line `assertEquals` collapsed), checkstyle clean. **LOC delta**: +194 new test file. **Lesson banked**: the modernization skill's "compare against the in-ecosystem reference" + "preserve observable contracts" steps caught the generator-misframing exactly as designed. The plan author wrote "9a annotation processor" assuming structural mirrors; the deep look revealed wire-format-compatible-distinct-types and pivoted to the right safety net (per-DTO snapshots). This is also the second slice this session to dogfood the skill — slice 101 was the first (reframed "add UIDs" to "drop suppressions" after the ecosystem check). The pattern is now reproducible: **assumed shape → diff/read → reality is different → execute the right thing, not the plan's first guess**.
- Slice 105 — **Modernization Tier 2 Step 11a: delete unused server-side `SpreadsheetState.java`.** Pure dead-code deletion. The class was defined in `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow/.../shared/SpreadsheetState.java` (66 public fields) but never instantiated anywhere on the server side (verified via two parallel `grep` passes across `src/main` AND `src/test` — zero matches besides its own class declaration line). It's a vestigial Vaadin 8 shared-state class that the server side has fully migrated past (now using `MirroredProperty<T>` + Element API for property-mirror traffic + `SpreadsheetClientRpc` façade for RPC traffic). The client-side mirror `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-client/.../shared/SpreadsheetState.java` stays — it's still actively consumed by `SpreadsheetConnector` + `SpreadsheetJsApi` on the GWT side, which Step 11b would address (deferred until a GWT-connector audit confirms whether the 66 fields are still actually consumed). **Gates**: suite **1050 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged (no callers were broken — confirmed because none existed). **LOC delta**: −X (deleted file, 66 fields × multi-line + javadoc; ballpark 200 lines). **Lesson banked**: "scoping investigation" slices (like slice 104) earn their keep by surfacing safe wins like this one. Without the investigation, the SpreadsheetState class would have stayed forever — every reader would assume it must be live because it has 66 fields and a real-looking interface. The deep look named it as dead code; the deletion is then trivial. Same pattern as POIReflectionTools's "swap reflection for now-public API" win in slice 103.
- Slice 104 — **Modernization Tier 2 scoping investigation.** The plan's previous Tier 2 framing said "the 7 wire-mirror DTOs are the central pain point" and listed Step 10 as "Vaadin 8 RPC → Vaadin Flow Element API". The scoping investigation showed both claims are off. **The actual wire topology** (Spreadsheet.java + rpc/ + frontend/vaadin-spreadsheet.js + client module): **server → client** is already on the modern Vaadin Flow API — 11 methods on `SpreadsheetClientRpc` are one-line `getElement().callJsFunction("name", args)` wrappers in `Spreadsheet.java` lines 679–755, plus 21 `MirroredProperty<T>` instances, plus 6 inline `getElement().setProperty(...)` calls with documented rationale (slices 94–96). **Client → server** is already on the modern DomEvent API — `@DomEvent("spreadsheet-event")` declared on `Spreadsheet.SpreadsheetEvent` (line 631), carries `type` + `data` via `@EventData`; the client-side `vaadin-spreadsheet.js` dispatches `CustomEvent('spreadsheet-event', {detail: {type, data}})` from 5 sites (lines 441, 445, 449, 453, 457); server-side dispatch goes `SpreadsheetEventListener` → `SpreadsheetHandlerImpl` → typed methods. The `SpreadsheetClientRpc` / `SpreadsheetServerRpc` / `SpreadsheetHandler` chain that the plan was treating as "legacy Vaadin 8 RPC" is actually just a Java-interface façade kept so the GWT-side connector and the server can share method names — **the actual wire is already Element API + DomEvent**. **Step 10 is therefore already done.** **The parallel-DTO count is 6, not 7** — `CellData` (14 fields), `MergedRegion` (6), `OverlayInfo` (9), `SpreadsheetActionDetails` (5), `PopupButtonState` (server `shared/`, client `client/`), `GroupingData` (server `shared/`, client `client/`). The previous count included the `SpreadsheetState` legacy shared-state class (66 fields), but **that class is server-side dead code** — defined in `shared/SpreadsheetState.java`, never instantiated anywhere on the server side (verified via grep) — while still **load-bearing on the client side** through `SpreadsheetConnector` + `SpreadsheetJsApi`. So `SpreadsheetState` is a half-dead artifact, not a wire DTO. **Serializer.java audit conclusion (Step 8)**: keep as-is. 30-line Jackson wrapper with two pieces of non-trivial behavior (`JsonInclude.Include.NON_DEFAULT` global config + try/catch error logging) that 28 server-side call sites depend on; inlining would *expand* duplication, not reduce it. **The revised Tier 2 step list**: 8 ✓ (audit done, no change), 9a (`@WireMirror` annotation processor — generates client-side `@JsType` copies from server-side originals, single source of truth, ~1 session) PLUS 9b (drop typed client mirrors entirely, switch GWT widget to `JsonObject.getString("field")` reads — ~6 sessions, deferred as Tier 3 prerequisite since it overlaps with the GWT-widget rewrite), 9c (rename/collapse the `*ClientRpc`/`*ServerRpc` façade interfaces — cosmetic, optional), 10 ✓ already done, 11a (delete unused server-side `SpreadsheetState.java` — safe deletion, no callers, ~0.5 session), 11b (drop client-side `SpreadsheetState` if redundant — needs GWT-connector audit first to confirm none of the 66 fields are still consumed). **Total open Tier 2 work after the scoping**: roughly **2 focused sessions for the safe slices** (9a + 11a), plus **1–2 more if 11b's audit shows redundancy**. 9b is correctly held back as Tier 3 preparation. **All Tier 2 changes keep the wire format byte-identical** — the `SpreadsheetHistoryManagerPayloadTest` snapshot is the acceptance gate. **No code changes in slice 104** — it's a scoping commit. The plan write-up was rewritten with the concrete topology + revised step list. **Lesson banked**: the plan author (earlier turn of mine) wrote Tier 2 in a hand-wavy way based on assumptions ("Vaadin 8 RPC is still in use", "7 DTOs", "the rollout is incomplete"). The deep look — reading `Serializer.java`, walking `Spreadsheet.java`'s 679–755 RPC implementation, opening the JS frontend file, counting actual server-side DTO files — produced concrete data that changed three of the four step framings. This is the same pattern as slices 99/100/101/102/103: **assumptions about distant code rot; investigate concretely before proposing**. The investigation step itself is the modernization, not the eventual edits.
- Slice 103 — **Modernization Tier 1 Step 6: POIReflectionTools audit against POI 5.5.1.** Step 5 was a duplicate of Step 2 (pattern matching for instanceof) — already executed in slice 100 as the pivot-from-sealing-Command; plan write-up updated to mark Step 5 as same-as-Step-2 with no separate execution. Step 6 was the real audit work: each of the 4 reflection bridges in `POIReflectionTools` re-evaluated against POI 5.5.1's current public API. **Two clean wins** (reflection dropped): (1) `getOrderedRuleList` priority comparator was reading `(CTCfRule) getFieldVal(rule, "_cfRule").getPriority()` — POI 5+ exposes `XSSFConditionalFormattingRule.getPriority()` directly, so the 6-line anonymous-class `Comparator<XSSFConditionalFormattingRule>` with manual `getFieldVal` calls + null-guard collapses to `rules.sort(Comparator.comparingInt(XSSFConditionalFormattingRule::getPriority).reversed())`. (2) `stopHere` was reading `(CTCfRule) getFieldVal(rule, "_cfRule").getStopIfTrue()` — POI 5+ exposes `XSSFConditionalFormattingRule.getStopIfTrue()` directly. The 5-line `if (rule instanceof X) { ... ctRule != null ... }` block collapses to `return rule instanceof XSSFConditionalFormattingRule xssfRule && xssfRule.getStopIfTrue();` (the pattern-matching idiom from slice 100 making the same bind+call in one line). **Two sites stay with reflection** (with rationale recorded in the class javadoc): `isBorderSet` needs `CTBorder.isSetLeft/Top/Right/Bottom/Diagonal/Horizontal/Vertical()` to distinguish "explicitly-set NONE" from "never declared" — POI's public `XSSFBorderFormatting.getBorderXxx()` returns a `BorderStyle` enum with NONE-by-default and collapses those two states, breaking the CF "explicitly-set NONE overrides previous rule" semantic the comment names. `hasStrikeThrough` needs "ANY entry in `CTFont.getStrikeList()` has `val=true`" — POI's public `XSSFFontFormatting.isStruckout()` inspects only the first entry, which is defensible for real Excel files but a behavior change vs the existing defensive scan. **The class javadoc was rewritten** to flip from "POI doesn't expose: rule priority, stopIfTrue, ..." (no longer accurate for the first two) to "POI's public BorderFormatting doesn't expose the isSet distinction; FontFormatting's isStruckout only checks the first strike entry" (the residual gaps). `getFieldVal` stays — still used by the two remaining reflection sites. **Unused imports dropped**: `java.util.Collections` (no more `Collections.sort`) and `CTCfRule` (no more `_cfRule` cast). **Tests stay green by construction**: the slice-79 `POIReflectionToolsTest` pins observable behavior (priority-descending order via the public sort; stopIfTrue false on a fresh rule) — both new code paths return the same values reflection used to extract, so the existing 16 tests pass unchanged. **Gates**: suite **1050 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged.
