Spotless + checkstyle clean. **LOC delta**: net **−24 lines** in `POIReflectionTools.java` (the priority comparator alone saves ~13 lines; `stopHere` saves ~5; the imports + class-javadoc rewrite balance). **Lesson banked**: the "future POI versions may add public APIs for these; this class is the seam to swap to them" comment in the original javadoc (slice 79's pin) **paid off exactly as designed**. The seam was named, and slice 103 swapped the inside of the seam without touching any caller. This pattern — "wrap library-version-specific code in a small named bridge class so future migrations are a single-file change" — is worth pinning as a sibling lesson to the `MirroredProperty` pattern: both name a mechanism so the rewrite later is one file, not a sweep.
- Slice 102 — **Modernization Tier 1 Step 4: surgical `@Nullable` sweep on Spreadsheet.java's javadoc-documented null sites.** Deep look before executing again revealed the plan's framing ("annotate the Spreadsheet public API") was too broad. **Constraint map**: (a) JSpecify 1.0 is already on the classpath transitively through Vaadin Flow (no new dependency needed — verified via `mvn dependency:tree`); (b) the upstream Vaadin Flow framework (`vaadin/flow`, ../flow on this disk) uses `@NullMarked` package-info + per-site `@Nullable` in **85 files** under `flow-server` — a clear ecosystem direction; (c) flow-components siblings (Grid, Button, TextField, Combo, Select, etc.) have **zero** JSpecify adoption — we'd be the first in the component monorepo; (d) **no NullAway / Checker Framework is configured** on this project, so the annotations are purely documentary (no compile-time enforcement) — equivalent ROI to upgrading the javadocs but with machine-readable type-position annotations. **Scope decision** (the deep-look output): annotate ONLY methods where the javadoc ALREADY documents the null contract — pure javadoc-to-type-annotation conversion. No `@NullMarked` package-info yet — that would be an unaudited contract claim for every other method/param/field I haven't read. No hunting for new nullable cases. **Sites annotated** (Spreadsheet.java only): the 6 `getCell` overloads return `@Nullable Cell` (the cell-may-not-exist-at-coordinates contract); the two `getCell(CellReference, ...)` overloads also gain `@Nullable CellReference` per their `cellReference == null ? null : ...` body; `getCell(String sheetName, ...)` gains `@Nullable String sheetName` per the javadoc "or current sheet if null" wording; `getCommentAuthorProvider()` returns `@Nullable CommentAuthorProvider`. **Import position lesson**: spotless enforces strict alphabetical order WITHIN groups but doesn't move imports between groups — `org.jspecify` belongs in the `org.*` block, after `org.apache.*` but before `org.slf4j.*` (alphabetically `j < s`); placed correctly on the first try after one corrective edit. **Gates**: suite **1050 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged. Spotless + checkstyle clean. **LOC delta**: net **+12 lines** (1 import, +1 alphabetical-order line in the import block, ~10 annotation insertions plus one method-signature line-break for the longer `getCell(@Nullable CellReference, Sheet)` overload). **Why I didn't add `@NullMarked` at the package level**: doing so would say "every public method/param/field in the spreadsheet package is non-null unless explicitly @Nullable" — which I cannot verify without auditing all ~50 helpers and ~200 public methods on Spreadsheet alone. Until someone runs that audit pass (or a NullAway config is added so inconsistencies become compile errors), the per-site `@Nullable` markers carry the documentary value safely; the broader rollout is correctly deferred. **Lesson banked**: this is the **fourth in-slice plan correction this session** (slices 99 dropped CellData-as-record after looking at wire contract, 100 dropped Command-sealing after looking at @since 1.0, 101 inverted Step 3 direction after the ecosystem reference, 102 narrowed Step 4 scope after weighing audit-effort × no-checker-configured ROI). The plan's "annotate the public API" was a hand-wavy ambition; the deep look turned it into a sharp, bounded slice that doesn't claim more than it has audited.
- Slice 101 — **Modernization Tier 1 Step 3: drop `@SuppressWarnings("serial")` sweep.** The plan as originally written said "replace 57 suppressions with explicit `serialVersionUID = 1L` declarations". Re-investigated before plowing in (per the code-modernization skill's "compare against the in-ecosystem reference" step) and found the plan was wrong-headed. Concrete data: spreadsheet has **96 `@SuppressWarnings("serial")` site-counts** across the whole component (83 in `src/main`, 1 in `src/test`, and the rest in IT modules out of slice scope), with **zero `serialVersionUID` declarations**. Modern Vaadin Flow components in the same repo (Grid, Button, TextField, Combo, Select, etc.) implement `Serializable` with **neither** — `vaadin-grid-flow` has 7 suppressions total, the typical modern component has 0/0. **And the compiler doesn't even raise the serial warning here** — `mvn compile` produced no output, meaning the annotations are silencing a warning nobody's emitting. The right Step 3 is therefore the **opposite direction** of what the plan author wrote: delete the 83 (+ 1 test-side) suppressions, don't add 83 UID declarations. Adding UIDs would be 83 lines of noise diverging from the ecosystem; deleting suppressions removes 83 lines of legacy cruft toward the ecosystem norm. **Sweep mechanics**: every site was on its own line (no inner-anonymous-class placement; verified via `grep -B1` for preceding `new SomeType()` returned 0 hits) so a single `perl -i -ne 'print unless /^\s*\@SuppressWarnings\("serial"\)\s*$/'` over the file list cleared all 84 occurrences in one pass. **Gates**: suite **1050 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged. Spotless re-formatted nothing. Checkstyle clean. **LOC delta**: net **−84 lines** across 84 files (each deletion is exactly one line). **Lesson banked**: this slice **dogfooded the code-modernization skill correctly**. The plan author (an earlier turn of mine) had written Step 3 with the conventional-wisdom framing — "Vaadin Flow session replication depends on serialVersionUID, replace suppressions with explicit IDs". Slice 101 caught this by following the skill's own "Step 5 — Compare against the in-ecosystem reference" instruction before executing: the modern Vaadin pattern in the same repo unambiguously doesn't bother with either annotation. The plan write-up has been amended explicitly with the rejected-original-framing for any future reader (so the "why didn't they add UIDs?" question is answered in the artifact). This is the third in-slice plan correction this session — slice 99 dropped CellData-as-record after looking at the wire contract; slice 100 dropped Command-sealing after looking at @since 1.0; slice 101 inverted Step 3's direction after looking at the ecosystem.
- Slice 100 — **Modernization Tier 1 Step 2: pattern matching for `instanceof`.** Second mechanical Tier 1 sweep under `refactor-artifacts/MODERNIZATION_PLAN.md`. Step 2 was originally "seal Command hierarchy" — abandoned at the start of the slice because `Command` is a `public` interface with `@since 1.0` documentation, heavily Mockito-mocked, and external code could implement it; sealing it would be a breaking API change, exactly the pitfall the new `code-modernization` skill names ("sealing hierarchies you don't fully own"). Pivoted to Step 5 (pattern matching) instead — strictly internal, observably equivalent. Swept ~40 cast-after-check sites across 13 files: `SpreadsheetFactory.java` (10 sites including SXSSFWorkbook in 4 lifecycle methods, the drawing/shape dispatch in `loadSheetOverlays`, the OOXML-anchor unwrap in `getDrawingAnchor`, the XSSFSheet/HSSFSheet pair in `getDrawingPatriarch`; also tightened `loadSheetTables` and `loadGrouping` to use the inverted-pattern early-return form `if (!(sheet instanceof XSSFSheet xssf)) return;` which eliminates the duplicated downcast — refactored `loadHSSFPicture`'s parameter type from `HSSFShape` to `HSSFPicture` since pattern-matching at the only callsite makes the narrower type free), `RuleEvaluator.java` (5 sites — the three eval-type checks in `matchesFormula`/`matchesValue` for ErrorEval/BoolEval/NumberEval/StringEval/BoolEval and the RefPtgBase guard in `getValueEvalFromFormula`), `Spreadsheet.java` (4 sites — the value-type dispatch in `createCell`, the HSSF/XSSF workbook protection unpack, the SpreadsheetFilterTable callback, and an additional inverted-form early-return for the grouping API check at line 4502), `CellValueManager.java` (2 sites — the XSSFCell quote-prefix style write and the parsedObject Date/Calendar/Number dispatch in `parseValueIntoNumericCell`; the two reflection-only HSSFCell/XSSFSheet guards left untouched since they don't cast the bound), `SpreadsheetUtil.java` (4 sites — the parsed-number Double/Number dispatch, the AreaEval/RefEval/RefListEval recursive walker in `getAllReferencedCells`, and the `styleHasQuotePrefix` XSSFCell unwrap), `CellBorderStyleBuilder.java` (3 sites — all three of the `getBorder*Style` helpers share the same XSSFCellStyle+ApplyBorder pattern, replaced via `replace_all` after merging into a single canonical form), `XSSFColorConverter.java` (1 site — same XSSFCellStyle+ApplyBorder pattern as the borders), `DefaultHyperlinkCellClickHandler.java` (2 sites — LazyRefEval and StringEval in `getStringHyperlinkValue`), `FontStyleComposer.java` (1 site — XSSFFont in `getFontFamilyCss`), `CellDataBuilder.java` (1 site — CustomDataFormatter color extraction), `ConditionalFormatter.java` (1 site — HSSFWorkbook color converter setup), `SpreadsheetStyleFactory.java` (1 site — same workbook-type dispatch), and `TableLifecycle.java` (1 site — SpreadsheetFilterTable filter clear). **Sites deliberately left unchanged** with rationale: (a) bare `instanceof` checks with no cast inside the branch (e.g. `SheetOverlayWrapper`'s four `XSSFClientAnchor` checks that call only base-`ClientAnchor` methods, `SheetOverlayVisibility`'s anchor-type guard, `ConditionalFormatter`'s HSSFSheetConditionalFormatting early-return, `POIReflectionTools`'s two reflection-guard sites where the cast is on `getFieldVal`'s return — not the instanceof target, `RuleEvaluator`'s XSSFConditionalFormattingRule early-return, `FormulaFormatter`'s three NumberToken/SeparatorToken checks that only call `token.toString()` on the supertype, and `SpreadsheetFactory`'s line-289 HSSFWorkbook filename-suffix check). Pattern matching binds a fresh variable — adding an unused one is just noise. (b) The two reflection-only branches in `CellValueManager.removeHyperlink` (XSSFSheet + HSSFSheet/HSSFCell) where `Field.get(sheet)` is reflection — no cast on `sheet` or `cell`. **Three branch-shape rewrites worth noting**: `RuleEvaluator.matchesFormula`'s nested if/else dropped to a flat sequence of `if-return` blocks after pattern matching killed the redundant cast — the nested form was load-bearing only for the cast-after-check pattern, not the logic. `Spreadsheet.expandOrCollapseRowsOrColumns` and `SpreadsheetFactory.loadGrouping`/`loadSheetTables` rewrote `instanceof HSSFSheet { return; } cast-to-XSSFSheet` as the inverted form `!(instanceof XSSFSheet xssf) { return; }` — same control flow, one fewer downcast site. `SpreadsheetFactory.loadHSSFPicture` had its parameter narrowed from `HSSFShape` to `HSSFPicture` since pattern-matching at the only callsite made the previously-needed widen-then-cast dance redundant. **Gates**: suite **1050 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged. Spotless re-formatted two files (RuleEvaluator + Spreadsheet) with no semantic change. Checkstyle clean. **LOC delta**: net **−24 lines** across the 13 files (each cast removal saves 1-2 lines; the inverted-form rewrites + delete-then-bind in SpreadsheetFactory save another ~6 lines; the `loadHSSFPicture` parameter narrowing saves the explicit cast). **Lesson banked (from the aborted Step 2 → pivoted Step 5)**: the code-modernization skill's pitfall about "sealing hierarchies you don't fully own" actually applies — when the originally-planned step would have broken a public API, the right move is to pivot to a later same-tier step that's safely internal, not to push the breaking change through. This is the second time the skill's pitfall list has caught a near-miss (the first was the records-over-DTOs reflex in slice 99's `CellData`).
- Slice 99 — **Modernization Tier 1 Step 1: switch expressions for cell-type dispatchers.** First slice executed under the new modernization plan (see `refactor-artifacts/MODERNIZATION_PLAN.md`). The plan came out of the **deep GWT investigation** that re-framed what "modernize this component" means: only the 105 files in `vaadin-spreadsheet-flow-client` are GWT-constrained (capped at Java 11 by `gwt-maven-plugin`); the entire server side — including every helper extracted across slices 1-98 — runs on Java 21. The earlier "what about modernization?" framing missed this distinction. Plan ranks moves into three tiers: server-side mechanical (this slice and several to follow), wire-protocol consolidation (depends on Serializer.java audit and DTO de-duplication), and TS/Lit client rewrite (frame as a separate project). This slice executes the safest Tier 1 step: migrate 5 cell-type `switch` statements to Java 14+ switch expressions in `CellNumericFormatter.getOriginalCellValue` (extracted two helper methods for NUMERIC and STRING arms to keep arm bodies single-expression), `CellNumericFormatter.getCachedFormulaCellValue` (combined the 4-way fall-through BLANK/FORMULA/_NONE/STRING into one arrow case; explicit `default -> null` preserves the pre-modernization null-on-unknown semantic that the old code relied on implicitly), `CellValueShifter.shift` (statement-form switch with arrow cases; comment documents that `default -> { }` no-op preserves the old `default: break`), `RuleEvaluator.hasCoherentType` (return-form), `CellValueCommand.getCellValue` (return-form, also dropped the redundant `else` after the early `return null`), and both `RowData` switches (read + writeTo, statement-form with grouped `case _NONE, BLANK ->` cases that replaced the fall-through pair). **Behavior-preservation notes**: (1) statement-form switches don't require exhaustiveness so omitting the default is identical to the previous `default: break`; (2) return-form switches with all enum constants listed are exhaustive by Java's compiler but adding a `default -> null` preserves the OLD null-on-future-unknown behavior in case POI adds a new CellType — the comment names this explicitly. (3) The duplicated `if (DateUtil.isCellDateFormatted(cell))` in `RowData.read`'s NUMERIC arm is preserved verbatim with a comment flagging it as not-in-scope; it's a separate bug. **Gates**: suite **1050 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged (no behavior change, no new tests needed). Spotless + checkstyle clean. **Lessons banked + skill landed**: new `.claude/skills/code-modernization/SKILL.md` encodes the general approach — map constraints before proposing changes; identify the modernization boundary (pure-internal vs crosses-a-contract vs build-target-constrained); tier moves by risk; preserve observable contracts; compare to in-ecosystem reference. Two artifacts together (plan + skill) so future sessions can pick up the modernization without retracing the constraint mapping.
- Slice 98 — **Multi-track: CellValueManager Phase 1 + two Phase 3 specs.** Two parallel tracks. **Track 1 (Phase 3 specs)**: `CellRangeNormalizer.md` (closes slice 89) and `RowShiftOrchestrator.md` (closes slice 97). Both pin the architectural details that the tests embed indirectly — the dual-overload arithmetic asymmetry on CellRangeNormalizer (different inner clamp math, same observable result), and the 12-step orchestration sequence on RowShiftOrchestrator including the `firstAffectedRow = startRow + n` arithmetic gotcha and the merged-region scan that's dead with modern POI but defensively preserved. **Track 2 (Phase 1 on CellValueManager)**: extract `CellDataBuilder` — the 148-LOC `createCellDataForCell` method + 11-LOC `handleIsDisplayZeroPreference` helper. Picked via Stage-1 enumeration agent that ranked it #1 of 5 candidates (very high cohesion + low entanglement). The helper takes a `CellValueManager` reference and reads through its package-private fields (`numericFormatter`, `fitCalculator`, `flushQueue`, `formulaFormatter`, `spreadsheet`) + public/protected methods (`getFormulaEvaluator`, `getConditionalFormattingEvaluator`, `doesValueFit`, `getCellWidth`, `getOriginalCellValue`). Four internal visibility tweaks (no API change): `numericFormatter`, `fitCalculator`, `flushQueue`, `formulaFormatter` widened `private` → package-private; `doesValueFit` widened `private` → package-private. `ZERO_AS_STRING` constant moved from CellValueManager (where it was only used by the now-deleted private helper) into the new CellDataBuilder. Public surface preserved: `protected CellData createCellDataForCell(Cell)` on CellValueManager remains as a thin delegator for subclass extension. **Characterization tests**: `CellDataBuilderTest` (12 tests) pinning the branch-dispatch shape rather than every formatting detail (formatting is delegated to `numericFormatter`, covered by `CellNumericFormatterTest`; full format fidelity is covered by ITs). Covered: basic CellData population (row/col 1-based + cellStyle + value); locked flag propagation; conditional-formatting branch (appends ` cf<N>` + marks flushQueue); the inverse no-CF branch; display-zero preference (3 sub-cases: zero+hide, zero+show, non-zero+hide); RuntimeException catch-all (returns `"#VALUE!"`); alignment branch (RIGHT appends ` r`, LEFT does not); invalid-formula marker (replaces value with `"#VALUE!"` AND strips leading `=`/`+` from formulaValue); empty-value-with-zero-style-index guard (cellData.value stays null). **Gates**: suite **1038 → 1050** (+12 new), spotless + checkstyle clean. **LOC**: `CellValueManager.java` shrunk from **1,175 → 1,014** (−161, **−13.7%**), the biggest single-slice drop on CellValueManager since slices 13a/13b. **Session aggregate after slice 98**: Spreadsheet.java 4,966 → 4,703 (already), CellValueManager.java 1,175 → 1,014 (this slice), total tests 963 → 1,050 (+87 characterization).
- Slice 97 — **Phase 1: extract `RowShiftOrchestrator`.** The biggest remaining single-slice win on Spreadsheet.java (~155-LOC `shiftRows(int, int, int, boolean, boolean)` body + ~20-LOC private `rowsMoved` helper + ~15-LOC private `getLastNonBlankRow` scan). All three move into one cohesive helper that orchestrates the full server-side bookkeeping that POI doesn't know about: cached row heights, hidden-row indexes, cell styles for shifted borders, evaluator caches (formula + conditional-formatting), value-manager client-cache row range, selection address, merged regions intersected by removed rows, popup buttons rooted in shifted rows, and the invalid-formula tracker. Public surface preserved: both public `shiftRows` overloads on `Spreadsheet` remain as thin delegators; the 1-arg overload still delegates to the 5-arg, the 5-arg now delegates straight to `rowShiftOrchestrator.shift`. Three internal visibility tweaks (no API change): `popupButtonRowShifter`, `invalidFormulaTracker` widened `private` → package-private (`sheetOverlayManager` was already package-private from earlier slices); `updateMergedRegions` widened `private` → package-private. **Characterization tests**: `RowShiftOrchestratorTest` (16 tests) pin: POI's `Sheet.shiftRows` is invoked AND both evaluator caches cleared; the `updateDeletedRowsInClientCache` row-range computation for both positive-N and negative-N branches (different formulas for `start`/`end`); the negative-N merged-region removal branch (mocked Sheet because real POI's `shiftRows` already removes invalidated regions before the SUT scan); `popupButtonRowShifter.shift` + `invalidFormulaTracker.shiftActiveSheetRows` are always dispatched; both branches of `sheetOverlayManager.hasOverlays` → `markReloadFromPoi`; the post-shift orchestration sequence (`updateMarkedCells` → `updateRowAndColumnRangeCellData(viewport)` → `updateMergedRegions`) via InOrder; all three selection-update sub-cases (selected-cell-inside-affected, selected-cell-outside-affected, selected-cell-null); both branches of `copyRowHeight` toggle (true grows the rowH array when `endRow + n + 1 > current length`; false leaves rowH untouched); `loadCustomBorderStylesToState` always fires. **Test-design lesson**: real-POI fixtures don't work for the merged-region-removal branch because POI 3.15+ already removes invalidated regions before the SUT's scan runs — the SUT branch is "POI-version-dependent dead code" with modern POI versions. To pin the branch authoritatively, the merged-region test specifically swaps to a mock `Sheet` (other tests in the file keep the real `XSSFWorkbook` fixture for the POI-side behavior verification). Two arithmetic gotchas worth pinning here: (a) `firstAffectedRow = startRow + n` for negative `n` (not `startRow`); (b) the `rowsMoved` scan range `(first+n, first]` uses `firstAffectedRow` as `first`, not the original `startRow`. Both bit me when writing tests; the comments in the test file document them. **Gates**: suite **1022 → 1038** (+16 new), spotless + checkstyle clean. **LOC**: `Spreadsheet.java` shrunk from **4,819 → 4,703** (−116, **−2.4%**). This is the **biggest single-slice drop this session** — the slice was deferred to a focused start exactly because of this size, and the safety net (1022 pre-existing tests) made it tractable without regression. After this slice, the substantive Phase-1 work on `Spreadsheet.java` is essentially complete: the remaining big-ish candidates from the enumeration agent (`GroupingOperations` ~135 LOC, `SheetReloadCoordinator` ~91 LOC, `CustomEditorAndComponentLoader` ~62 LOC) are all still high-entanglement and lower-LOC-per-test-effort than this one was.
- Slice 96 — **Two deferred items closed: `showCustomEditorOnFocus` migration + Phase 3 spec for `ClientReloadFlag`.** The boolean-MirroredProperty sweep finishes by migrating `showCustomEditorOnFocus`, which had been deferred from slice 94 because its getter (`isShowCustomEditorOnFocus`, public) reads from the element directly. Both the setter (writes via element.setProperty) AND the getter (reads via element.getProperty) are now in lockstep with the new `MirroredProperty<Boolean>` field — server-side cached value is the source of truth. Semantically equivalent because no other code path writes to the "showCustomEditorOnFocus" property; the migration adds nothing the client can observe but does give the server a queryable last-pushed value. Phase 3 spec for `ClientReloadFlag` (236 lines at `refactor-artifacts/specs/ClientReloadFlag.md`) closes the loop on slice 93: pins the load-bearing pattern paragraphs (coalescing, unconditional flag mutation, at-most-once-per-round flush, seed-on-flush coupling, long-to-double timestamp widening), names the `setReload(false)` no-op semantic that pre-extraction code carried and post-extraction architecture drops by construction, and lists three open human-review questions including whether the seed-on-flush coupling is intentional and whether `flush` should early-return on `pending == false`. **Gates**: suite **1022 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged. **LOC**: `Spreadsheet.java` **4,811 → 4,809** (−2). After this slice, the only inline `getElement().setProperty(...)` calls remaining on `Spreadsheet` are the two POI-canonical booleans (intentional — POI is source of truth), the `dirty` timestamp in `markAsDirty` (1-line; helper extraction would be over-engineering), the `id` get+set pair (read-through pattern), the protected `cellStyleToCSSStyle`, `appId` (one-shot at attach), and `setReportStyle` / `setStatusLabelValue` / a few others I haven't audited. The MirroredProperty rollout is now feature-complete for the simple cases. **Session takeaway across slices 89-96**: `Spreadsheet.java` shrunk from **4,966 → 4,809 LOC (−157, −3.2%)** across 8 slices; +59 characterization tests; 5 new helpers (CellRangeNormalizer, InvalidFormulaTracker, CellMarkUpdater, ClientReloadFlag) + 6 new MirroredProperty fields; 3 Phase 3 specs (InvalidFormulaTracker, CellMarkUpdater, ClientReloadFlag).
- Slice 95 — **Phase 4: MirroredProperty migration for the two non-boolean stragglers.** Followup to slice 94, picking up two callsites that weren't booleans: `invalidFormulaErrorMessage` (a public `String` setter — slice 28's journal entry claimed this was migrated but a re-grep showed the inline `getElement().setProperty(...)` was still in place; the migration was apparently dropped on the floor) and `setNamedRanges` (a package-private `List<String>` setter that already used `Serializer.serialize` — perfect fit for `MirroredProperty.serialized`). After this slice, the inventory of remaining inline `getElement().setProperty(...)` callsites in `Spreadsheet.java` is down to a documented short list: the two POI-canonical booleans (`displayGridlines`/`displayRowColHeadings`) where caching would compete with POI, `setShowCustomEditorOnFocus` where the getter reads through (deferred to its own slice), the `dirty` timestamp (one-line push, no flag, candidate for a `MirroredTimestamp` generalization later), the `id` getter+setter pair (read-through pattern), the protected `cellStyleToCSSStyle` (subclass surface), and `appId` (one-shot at attach). **Gates**: suite **1022 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged. **LOC**: `Spreadsheet.java` **4,815 → 4,811** (−4).
- Slice 94 — **Phase 4: continue MirroredProperty rollout for 4 push-only booleans.** Migrates `workbookProtected`, `hasActions`, `lockFormatColumns`, `lockFormatRows` from inline `getElement().setProperty(name, v)` to `MirroredProperty<Boolean>.bool(this, name, false)`. The pre-migration setters had no backing field — they were pure push-to-client write-throughs. Post-migration adds the cached field (the same architectural pattern the rollout slices 26-37 established) so the server now KNOWS what value it last pushed, which is a small but real improvement in queryability. Wire shape is byte-identical: `MirroredProperty.bool`'s mirror function is `(el, v) -> el.setProperty(name, v.booleanValue())` — same call as the inline setters. **Gates**: suite **1022 / 0 / 0 / 4** unchanged (no new tests needed — the existing MirroredProperty tests + the payload-snapshot test already cover the wire-format invariants). Spotless + checkstyle clean. **LOC**: `Spreadsheet.java` shrunk from **4,819 → 4,815** (−4 net; the 4 new field declarations add 12 lines, the 4 setter rewrites save 4 lines × ~4 lines each). The byte-format compatibility of this migration was the explicit point — **the payload-snapshot test once again serves as the Phase 4 safety net**. **Three booleans deliberately NOT migrated, with rationale documented here for the next reader**: `displayGridlines` / `displayRowColHeadings` — the canonical source is the POI Sheet, so a server-side cache would compete as source-of-truth and could go stale if POI is mutated externally. `showCustomEditorOnFocus` — its public getter `isShowCustomEditorOnFocus()` reads from the element directly; migrating it would change the getter's read path (still semantically equivalent, but a subtle behavior change worth scoping into its own slice). After this slice, the remaining inline `getElement().setProperty(...)` call-sites are: the two POI-canonical bools just named (intentional), the `dirty` timestamp in `markAsDirty`, the `id` get+set pair, the protected `cellStyleToCSSStyle`, `appId` (one-shot at attach), `namedRanges` (serialized — could migrate to MirroredProperty.serialized), and `invalidFormulaErrorMessage` (slice 28 was supposed to migrate this; needs investigation).
- Slice 93 — **Phase 4 architectural extract: `ClientReloadFlag`.** Names a previously-distributed pattern: the "deferred-flush boolean with client-side mirror". Pre-extraction, the pattern was spread across FIVE fragments on Spreadsheet: (1) `boolean reload` field at line 172, (2) private `setReload(boolean)` setter at lines 435-438 that pushes `System.currentTimeMillis()` to the element `reload` property only when the arg is true, (3) public `isRerenderPending()` read at line 1442, (4) an inline `Optional.ofNullable(UI.getCurrent()).ifPresent(ui -> ui.beforeClientResponse(this, e -> { if (reload) updateReloadState(); }))` block in `reloadActiveSheetData` at lines 3113-3119, (5) private `updateReloadState()` at lines 3329-3345 that combined the timestamp push + flag clear + `initialSheetSelection` defaulting from `sheetState.getSelectedCellsOnSheet(getActiveSheet())` or `"A1"`. Now: one **`ClientReloadFlag`** helper (~95 LOC) owns the boolean and three methods — `isPending()`, `markPending(Spreadsheet)` (combines the flag-set + UI-callback-registration), and package-private `flush(Spreadsheet)` (pushes timestamp, clears flag, seeds initial selection). On Spreadsheet: `isRerenderPending()` is a thin delegator; the 7-line inline block in `reloadActiveSheetData` collapses to `clientReloadFlag.markPending(this)`; `setReload` private setter and `updateReloadState` private method are DELETED outright (no other callers). One internal visibility tweak: `sheetState` field widened `private` → package-private (no API change). **Characterization tests**: `ClientReloadFlagTest` (10 tests) pins the always-set-flag-even-without-UI contract (critical — the `pending = true` line sits OUTSIDE the `Optional.ifPresent` block so headless contexts still mark properly), idempotent markPending, the flush sequence via InOrder (timestamp BEFORE seed read, so the timestamp emission happens regardless of selection state), the three flush-time selection-defaulting branches (existing-non-null is preserved, sheetState-known is used, sheetState-null defaults to "A1"), and the wire-format detail that Vaadin's `Element.setProperty(String, double)` widens `System.currentTimeMillis()` long → double. The `beforeClientResponse` callback-registration path itself is intentionally NOT exercised in the unit test (UI.getCurrent() is null in headless context); the integration covering it is the existing **`SpreadsheetHistoryManagerPayloadTest` golden snapshot** which passes unchanged — that's the architecturally meaningful safety net for this slice (the wire-format byte-shape is preserved). **Gates**: suite **1022 / 0 / 0 / 4** (+10 tests), spotless + checkstyle clean. **LOC**: `Spreadsheet.java` shrunk from **4,850 → 4,819** (−31, −0.6%). Flavor: this is **Phase 4 architectural refactor** — it doesn't just decompose, it *names* a pattern ("deferred flush with client mirror") that recurs in the codebase. Other "set X true, flush on next response" patterns (e.g. the `dirty` timestamp pushed via `markAsDirty`) could later adopt the same shape, with the same semantics. **Takeaway worth pinning**: the **payload-snapshot test was the load-bearing safety net** for this slice — characterization tests pin observable behavior of the helper, but the snapshot pins the wire-shape across the full end-to-end flow. For Phase 4 work (where the change is "rearrange the pieces, preserve the externals"), the snapshot is the test that ultimately matters. Future Phase 4 slices should always identify the equivalent snapshot before starting.
- Slice 90 — **`CellMarkUpdater` (~115 LOC) — cohesive cell-state update orchestrator.** Owns the five paths that all share the "a cell changed → push the change to the client" concern: `markCellAsUpdated(cell, cellStyleUpdated)`, `markCellAsDeleted(cell, cellStyleUpdated)`, `deleteCell(row, col)`, `updateMarkedCells()`, `refreshAllCellValues()`. Pure orchestrator — every method dispatches to existing helpers (valueManager, styler, selectionManager, conditionalFormatter, cellCommentLoader, formulaEvaluator, conditionalFormattingEvaluator) or back to Spreadsheet (refreshCells, reloadVisibleCellContents, updateRowAndColumnRangeCellData). Two visibility tweaks: `cellCommentLoader` field widened `private` → package-private, `updateRowAndColumnRangeCellData(...)` widened `private` → package-private. No API change (both were internal). **Characterization tests**: `CellMarkUpdaterTest` (11 tests) pins each branch of the two `mark*` overloads (cellStyleUpdated true/false), the cell-exists vs cell-null branch of `deleteCell` (plus an InOrder verification of the 4-call chain: styler → row.removeCell → cellDeleted → refreshCells), `deleteCell`'s active-sheet-index resolution (NOT a hardcoded 0), the 5-call orchestration shape in `updateMarkedCells` via InOrder, and both branches of `refreshAllCellValues` (loaded viewport reloads + reselects; firstColumn==-1 early-returns without reload). The clear-order InOrder test pins that evaluators are cleared BEFORE the value cache (which would otherwise re-trigger stale formula evaluation). **Gates**: suite **1012 / 0 / 0 / 4** (+11 tests), spotless + checkstyle clean. **LOC**: `Spreadsheet.java` shrunk from **4,892 → 4,850** (−42, −0.9%). Flavor: pure decomposition — the 5 methods carried real orchestration logic out of Spreadsheet. **Two takeaways**. **(1) Pure-orchestrator helpers test via Mockito `InOrder`.** When the SUT's logic is just "call X, then Y, then Z", the only contract worth pinning IS the order. Plain `verify()` would catch missing calls but not reorderings; `InOrder` catches both. The clear-order test for `refreshAllCellValues` is the best example: even though the 3 clears are independent (no return-value flow), the contract has them in evaluator-first / value-cache-last order because of formula-evaluation reanimation, and that's only observable via InOrder. **(2) Field-injection-via-reflection for package-private non-final fields on Spreadsheet mocks.** Mockito mocks don't run the constructor so package-private fields like `cellCommentLoader` are null on the mock. The `CellDeletionGuardTest` reflection pattern is now the third file to use it; worth adding a `@BeforeEach` helper or test fixture if a fourth case appears.
- Slice 89 — **Two low-entanglement extractions from `Spreadsheet.java`: `CellRangeNormalizer` + `InvalidFormulaTracker`.** Stage-1 enumeration via a parallel `Explore` agent ranked ten candidate clusters in the still-remaining Spreadsheet.java logic; picked the two safest. **`CellRangeNormalizer`** (~96 LOC, `(Spreadsheet)` constructor; pure utility — no state, only reads `getRows()` / `getCols()`): owns the two `createCorrectCellRangeAddress` overloads renamed to `fromAddressString(String)` / `fromCoordinates(int,int,int,int)`. Both methods reorder corners and clamp to the active sheet's extents, but with subtly different inner arithmetic (string clamps with `getRows() - 1` directly, int clamps with `getRows()` then subtracts one on emit) — both paths preserved verbatim and pinned by characterization tests. **`InvalidFormulaTracker`** (~91 LOC, `(Spreadsheet)` constructor; owns the previously inline `Map<Integer, HashSet<String>> invalidFormulas` field): exposes `mark` / `isMarked` / `removeMark` / `clearAll` plus the new `shiftActiveSheetRows(first, last, n)` which absorbs the 20-line inline block from `Spreadsheet.shiftRows` that re-keyed invalid-formula marks after a row shift. The two private helpers `rowWasRemoved` and `numberOfRowsAboveWasChanged` on Spreadsheet were only used by that inline block — both deleted as orphans. **API preserved**: the two `protected createCorrectCellRangeAddress(...)` methods stay on Spreadsheet as thin delegators (subclass-extension surface intact per Rule A); `markInvalidFormula` / `isMarkedAsInvalidFormula` / `removeInvalidFormulaMark` (package-private) stay as thin delegators. **Characterization tests**: `CellRangeNormalizerTest` (17 tests pinning ordered/reversed/single-cell/clamping/cross-overload paths plus the inclusive `>=` clamp boundary) and `InvalidFormulaTrackerTest` (21 tests pinning per-sheet isolation, the null-original guard, all three shift sub-paths drop/translate/keep, plus inclusive `<= first` and exclusive `first+n <` boundaries on the drop predicate). Self-audit per the 5-axis checklist: every test names a 1-line regression in the comment; no vacuous assertions. **Gates**: suite **1001 / 0 / 0 / 4** (+38 tests), spotless + checkstyle clean. **LOC**: `Spreadsheet.java` shrunk from **4,966 → 4,892** (−74 LOC, −1.5%). Flavor: **decomposition + encapsulation** — InvalidFormulaTracker is a pure decomposition (the shift logic carries real branching), CellRangeNormalizer is more encapsulation (a focused utility extracted from a 70-line span). Two takeaways worth pinning. **(1) The enumeration-via-Explore-agent shape is reusable.** With 4,966 LOC and ~260 methods left in Spreadsheet, hand-walking the file is no longer the right move; an Explore agent with the "classify methods as delegating-vs-owning-real-logic, then cluster" prompt produced a flat list of all 10 candidate clusters in one shot, ranked by value/entanglement. Future Phase-1 slices on Spreadsheet should start with the same enumeration pattern. **(2) `rowWasRemoved` arithmetic is subtle.** The predicate `n < 0 && first + n < row && row <= first` defines a half-open range `(first+n, first]` — inclusive at `first`, exclusive at `first+n`. The test pin includes BOTH boundaries (one test at `row == first` for inclusion, one at `row == first+n` for exclusion) so a future refactor that flipped either bound is caught. The `n < 0` guard is also pinned via a positive-n test that confirms drop NEVER fires regardless of row position.
- **Meta-audit on the fix-pass tests (2026-06-21).** Reran the `refactor-test-audit` skill across all 84 new + modified tests via six parallel `Explore` agents. Each agent's structured findings included a "name a 1-line regression that would break this test" prompt — intentionally hunting false positives. Result: **1 HIGH false-positive** (`CellValueShifterTest.shift_formulaCell_invalidFormulaTriggersCatchBranch_copiesOriginal` — test name promised catch-branch coverage but used "2+3" which has no cell references, so the matcher loop never executed and the catch was never reached); **2 MEDIUM gaps** (MergedRegionOperations cascade call-order not enforced; CommandReplayer empty-cells event verified with `any()` instead of captor); **4 LOW issues** (vacuous BLANK test, misleading locale comment, NPE-as-failure-mode in isGeneralCell, redundant write-only assertion). All 7 addressed: (1) the original catch-branch test was renamed to `shift_formulaCell_noCellReferences_passesThroughUnchanged` (honest about what it pins) AND a NEW test `shift_formulaCell_postShiftRefIsInvalid_catchBranchRestoresOriginal` was added that GENUINELY triggers the catch — by row-shifting "A1" up to produce "A0", which POI rejects in formula syntax, firing the catch's fallback that restores the source formula on dst. (2) MergedRegionOperations now uses `InOrder cascadeOrder = Mockito.inOrder(cascade)` + ordered verify. (3) CommandReplayer empty-cells now uses `ArgumentCaptor<ComponentEvent<?>>` and explicitly asserts `event.getChangedCells().isEmpty()`. (4) BLANK test now `verify(cell, never()).getCellFormula() / getStringCellValue() / getBooleanCellValue() / getNumericCellValue() / getErrorCellValue() / getDateCellValue()` to differentiate BLANK from a regression that fell through to another cell-type branch. (5) Locale comment corrected to acknowledge JVM-default coupling. (6) isGeneralCell now stubs `style.getDataFormatString()` so AND-order flip is observable via assertion (not NPE). (7) `overlayComponents_isWriteOnly` removed (redundant with `overlayComponents_addAndRemove_donotThrow`); the write-only commentary folded into the sibling's javadoc. **Gates after meta-audit**: 963 / 0 / 0 / 4 green; spotless + checkstyle clean. Findings + fixes appended to `refactor-artifacts/TEST_AUDIT_2026-06-21.md`. Lesson worth pinning: parallel multi-agent audits with the "name a 1-line regression" prompt catch test-name-lies-about-assertion smells that single-author review misses. The catch-branch finding in particular was a classic false positive — the test looked thorough, passed cleanly, but covered nothing real.
- **Test-audit fix-pass (2026-06-21) — Batches A/B/C.** Ran the new `refactor-test-audit` skill against all 58 existing test files for extracted helpers + 3 orphan checks. Six parallel `Explore` agents applied the 5-axis checklist (Coverage / Behavioral / Style / Assertion / Regression). Triaged into 49 clean / 11 weak / 1 documented-gap / 3 missing-test. **Batch A** added 3 new test files for previously test-less extracted helpers: `CommandStackTest` (22 tests, pins the LinkedList-shared-by-reference contract, overflow shape `removeFirst()+cursor-stays`, setMaxSize/clipAfter semantics), `CommandReplayerTest` (13 tests, pins the four-way `changeSelection` dispatch that `SpreadsheetHistoryManagerTest` explicitly punted on with a comment), `CellNumericFormatterTest` (31 tests, covers all 7 public methods + all 6 `CellType` branches in `getOriginalCellValue` + all 4 cached-result branches in `getCachedFormulaCellValue`). **Batch B** sharpened 4 existing tests: `CellValueShifterTest` +5 for FORMULA branches (row/col shift, $-absolute preservation, evaluator notify) + ERROR; `DirectionalShiftOperatorTest` +5 (sequence-increment dispatch tie via `MockedStatic<RangeSequenceDetector>` for all four directions + multi-row up); `SheetOverlayManagerTest` +1 (OverlayChangeListener callback re-triggers loadOrUpdate); `FontStyleComposerTest` +6 (all six FontFamily enum cases including NOT_APPLICABLE, with exact-string assertions to pin ordering). **Batch C** in-place sharpening of 7 weak findings: SequenceValueExtractor → assertArrayEquals form; MergedRegionOperations cascade order via ArgumentCaptor on coordinates; SheetOverlayStorage assertDoesNotThrow on the write-only loop; HyperlinkLoader verifyNoMoreInteractions instead of loose any()-match count; ColumnSizeOperations specific `SpreadsheetUtil.pixel2WidthUnits(88)` value; TableRegistry comment ties live-reference contract to the intentional performance choice; SheetGrouping comment makes the col-vs-row asymmetry explicit. **Quirk discovered during the pass:** `CellNumericFormatter.valueContainsOnlyNumbers` concatenates the locale decimal separator into a regex without `Pattern.quote(...)`. Under US locale the `.` is a regex meta-character meaning "any char", so "1,5"/"1X5"/"1.5" all match. Pinned as a characterization quirk in `valueContainsOnlyNumbers_commaUnderUSLocale_pinsRegexMetacharQuirk` with a comment naming the future fix (add `Pattern.quote(...)`) and an explicit "update this test when fixed" instruction. **One open question resolved:** the agent flagged `SpreadsheetHistoryManagerPayloadTest` as "minimal coverage" (2 tests); re-read during fix-pass — those two tests are golden-snapshot payload assertions comparing the full server-to-client element-property delta + pending JS invocations. Keeping as-is; "low count" was misleading. **Gates throughout:** spotless + checkstyle clean. Full unit suite: **963 / 0 / 0 / 4** (+84 tests on top of the prior 879). Audit doc landed at `refactor-artifacts/TEST_AUDIT_2026-06-21.md` with the per-helper findings + fix-pass execution log; the `refactor-test-audit` skill stays at `.claude/skills/refactor-test-audit/SKILL.md` for future audit passes. Phase 2 now ratchets to a higher bar: the user's "no refactor without coverage" memory principle is enforced by this audit pattern.
- Slice 73 — **Phase 2 tests for `ColumnAutofitter`.** 12 tests. Three behaviors pinned: (1) **POI NPE catch** — `Sheet.autoSizeColumn` is known to throw NPE on certain workbooks (`doThrow(new NullPointerException("poi"))` triggers the silent abort path; no colW update, no cache invalidation); (2) **filter-button padding** — columns hosting a `SpreadsheetFilterTable` popup button get `+16px` (14 button + 2 padding) added to the autofit width; non-filter tables and filter tables without a popup for the column get no padding; (3) **WeakHashMap refresh contract** — `refreshAutofittedColumnsInTable` walks the table's column range and re-autofits ONLY columns whose current width still matches the recorded autofit (user-manually-resized columns are skipped). Hit a new technique: **`Spreadsheet.sheetOverlayManager` is `final`** — Mockito mocks have the field but direct assignment from a test won't compile. Set via reflection in `@BeforeEach`: `Field f = Spreadsheet.class.getDeclaredField("sheetOverlayManager"); f.setAccessible(true); f.set(spreadsheet, mockOverlayManager);`. Worth adding to the Phase-2 lessons dossier — direct field access on a Spreadsheet mock works for package-private non-final fields, but `final` ones need reflection.
- Slice 72 — **Phase 2 tests for `ColumnSizeOperations`.** 16 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `XSSFSheet` + `ColumnHelper` + `CellValueManager`. Sibling of slice 71 but with tighter coupling: the operations maintain TWO synchronized states (`hiddenColumnIndexes` 1-based list + `colW` 0-based array), downcast to `XSSFSheet` for `getColumnHelper().cleanColumns()`, and call `CellValueManager.clearCacheForColumn` + `loadCellData` on column-width and un-hide. Pinned all the entanglements: width=0 routes through applyHidden; positive width un-hides the column (POI + the 1-based list) AND clears the cell cache + reloads the column's cell data; setHidden(true) on an already-hidden column doesn't add a duplicate to the 1-based list; setHidden(false) on a column that wasn't actually hidden still calls `setColumnHidden(false)` on POI BUT skips the cache invalidation branch (the inner applyHidden is partially no-op while the outer setHidden still fires reloadSheetStyles). Defensive copy on BOTH state collections (hidden list AND colW array) pinned via a mutation-of-originals test. The XSSF downcast is exercised directly (the setUp uses XSSFSheet, which is `final` — Mockito 5+ inline-mock-maker handles it transparently).
- Slice 71 — **Phase 2 tests for `RowSizeOperations`.** 13 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Sheet` + `Row`. Pinned the **height-zero-equals-hidden entanglement**: setHeight(0) routes through `applyHidden(true)` and NEVER calls setHeightInPoints; setHeight(positive) on a previously-hidden row clears `setZeroHeight(false)` + removes from `hiddenRowIndexes`. Pinned the **defensive-copy on hidden list** (new ArrayList → mutate → setHiddenRowIndexes; original list reference never mutated). Pinned the **1-based hidden-list convention** — `hiddenRowIndexes` stores `index+1`, not POI's 0-based index; a test confirms setting height on POI row 2 (1-based: 3) doesn't unhide an entry "2" (which corresponds to POI row 1). Pinned the **setHidden asymmetry**: `setHidden(true)` AND `setHidden(false)` both call `reloadSheetStyles(true, true)` — the second arg is hardcoded regardless of direction, matches the source intent "row visibility changed, recompute". `setDefaultHeightPoints` updates both the POI sheet AND `spreadsheet.setDefRowH` (mirror chain), but does NOT trigger reloadSheetStyles (default-height isn't a per-row change).
- Slice 70 — **Phase 3 spec for `SheetOverlayVisibility`.** Pairs with slice 53's 10 tests. Documents the **layered decision tree**: (1) `DONT_MOVE_AND_RESIZE` on XSSF anchor — always visible (issue #3261 workaround, x/y coords can't be matched against viewport); (2) hidden-range gate — non-type-2 anchors with all-columns-hidden OR all-rows-hidden → invisible; partial-hidden takes the normal path; type-2 anchors (col2==0 && row2==0) bypass the gate entirely; (3) **4-quadrant OR** — visible if any of (frozen-corner, frozen-rows, frozen-cols, main-viewport) returns true. Pinned the quadrant guards explicitly: Q1 needs both splits > 0; Q2 needs vertical > 0; Q3 needs horizontal > 0; Q4 unconditional. Pinned the POI `ClientAnchor.getCol1/2` is short / `getRow1/2` is int quirk as a non-obvious gotcha for future Phase 2 contributors. Three "Human review" questions: (1) is the type-2 hidden-range bypass intentional; (2) should negative `getLastFrozen*` be a precondition violation; (3) does the all-or-nothing hidden-range definition match the product intent. **12 specs total**: SequenceIncrement, ClientCellCache, ViewportBounds, ClientFlushQueue, ClientCacheFlusher, RangeSequenceDetector, MergedRegionLookup, MergedRegionCascade, MergedRegionOperations, SequenceValueExtractor, SheetOverlayStorage, SheetOverlayVisibility.
- Slice 69 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetDimensions`.** 11 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Element`. Six getter/setter pairs (defRowH/defColW/rowH/colW/hiddenColumnIndexes/hiddenRowIndexes). Pinned the **property-mirror semantic** on every setter: primitives go through `setProperty(String, double)` (float widens to double; int widens to double), arrays/lists go through `Serializer.serialize` returning a String → `setProperty(String, String)`. Initial state pinned (float=0.0, int=0, arrays/lists=null). Setters are independent (no cross-effects between fields). **The mirror is unconditional** — setting the same value twice fires the property write twice (no equality short-circuit) — load-bearing for callers who need to force a client refresh, but worth knowing if a caller wanted change-detection (they'd need it upstream).
- Slice 68 — **Phase 2 tests for `CellFitCalculator`.** 13 tests, mocked `Cell` + `CellStyle`. Pure-function calculator with one stateful piece (the per-style width-ratio map from the client). Pinned: null map → assume fit (initial state, before any client report); empty map → assume fit; style not in map → assume fit; inclusive boundary at `cellWidth - 4` (BigDecimal compareTo ≤ 0); **the 4px padding constant** (2px left + 2px right) explicitly via a column-width=13 vs 14 boundary test. `onWidthRatioUpdate` REPLACES wholesale (not putAll-merge) — load-bearing because partial-merge would leak stale ratios from prior client snapshots. `getRatioForStyleIndex` returns the ratio or null; the style-index lookup uses `cell.getCellStyle().getIndex()` (not arbitrary input), exercised by mid-test style switching.
- Slice 67 — **Phase 2 tests for `TableRegistry`.** 12 tests. Thin storage class with one latch (`tablesLoaded`). Pinned: HashSet semantics on add/remove/getAll; `getForSheet` filters by sheet identity via `equals` and returns a FRESH ArrayList (not a live reference) — confirmed by clearing the returned list and verifying the registry is untouched. `getAll` returns the LIVE HashSet (mutations through the returned reference DO affect the registry — different semantic from `getForSheet`). The `tablesLoaded` latch is orthogonal to the tables set: add/remove don't flip it, markLoaded* doesn't touch the set. Pinned the swap pair: `markLoadedForActiveSheet` flips true; `markNeedsReload` flips false.
- Slice 66 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetScrollMemory`.** 4 tests. Tiny helper that records per-sheet scroll positions. Pinned the **defensive-copy semantic** on both arrays (vertical + horizontal): each call reads the current arrays, `Arrays.copyOf`s them, updates the target index, writes back — never mutates the original references. Load-bearing because in-place mutation would skip the `MirroredProperty` client mirror. Other indices in the array are preserved across the update. **Suite 602 → 631 (+29 across slices 66, 67, 68).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cells for `SheetScrollMemory`, `TableRegistry`, `CellFitCalculator` all flipped to ✓.
- Slice 65 — **Phase 2 tests for `PopupButtonRowShifter`.** 9 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Registry` + `Binding` + `Sheet`. Pinned the row classification: (1) **rowWasRemoved** — when `n < 0` and `row` in `[first, first - n)`, the button is dropped (no setCellReference, no entry in rebuilt map); (2) **numberOfRowsAboveWasChanged** — when `row >= first AND row > last`, the button shifts by `n` (setCellReference fires with new row, new ref becomes the map key); (3) **unaffected** — neither condition true, the button stays in the rebuilt map with its original reference. Mixed-button test exercises all three classifications in one shift call. **Detach-first contract** pinned via `InOrder`: every `binding.detach` fires before `registry.replaceAll`. Empty-registry early-return pinned. n=0 edge case: a button at row past `last` still gets `setCellReference` called with the same row — no early-return for "no-op shift", just a redundant set call. Documents the contract — a refactor could add the early-return if perf matters.
- Slice 64 — **Phase 2 tests for `PopupButtonBinding`.** 9 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Element` (Vaadin Flow's DOM element) + `PopupButton`. Pinned attach: tracks in `attachedButtons`, calls `addPopupButton` via JS, appends as virtual child ONLY if not already a child of the spreadsheet (the parent check guards against double-appending — RPC still fires unconditionally because the GWT widget needs to know about the re-attach). Pinned detach: RPC fires + removeVirtualChild ONLY if the spreadsheet was the parent. Pinned `detachAll`: early-return when empty, iterates over a snapshot (safe from CME) and clears the set. Hit a Vaadin Flow API quirk — `Element.removeVirtualChild` is overloaded `(Element... elements)` AND `(Collection<Element> elements)`, so a bare `any()` matcher is ambiguous; required `any(Element.class)` to disambiguate. Also hit the "duplicate verify" Mockito gotcha: a bare `verify(mock).method(...)` means "exactly 1", so combining it with `verify(mock, times(2)).method(...)` is contradictory — fixed by keeping only the `times(N)` form.
- Slice 63 — **Phase 2 tests for `PopupButtonRefresher`.** 6 tests. Walks the registry and dispatches each button: skip if cell ref's sheet name doesn't match active sheet; attach if `isCellVisible(row+1, col+1)` returns true; detach otherwise. Pinned the +1 conversion from POI 0-based (`button.getColumn() / .getRow()`) to 1-based (`Spreadsheet.isCellVisible`). Mixed-buttons test exercises both attach AND detach in one refresh call.
- Slice 62 — **Phase 2 tests for `CustomComponentLoader`.** 14 tests covering three modes: (1) **`load()` with `factory == null`** — clears both client maps to null, unregisters every component, applies empty heights via the row sizer; pinned that the rowSizer call fires even with no components, and the unregister call is skipped when the set was empty; (2) **`load()` with factory** — 4-quadrant scan dispatch identical to other loaders + the load-bearing **reconciliation pass**: components from the prior scan that aren't in the new scan get unregistered AND removed from `componentIDtoCellKeysMap` UNLESS their node id is still in `cellKeysToEditorIdMap` (editor lifetime is keyed off the editor-map values, NOT the scan output — a subtle invariant that survives across loads); rowsWithComponents triggers `rowSizer.applyHeightsFor` only when non-empty; (3) **`unloadAll()`** — clears editor map + unregisters components, but does NOT touch `componentIDtoCellKeysMap` and does NOT call rowSizer (used pre-load when a new factory is wired). Pinned the asymmetry explicitly via `Mockito.clearInvocations` after a seed. Second use of `MockedStatic<Spreadsheet>` for the `getComponentNodeId` static. Used a stateful `doAnswer` to make the mocked scanner add `comp1` to `nextComponents` during seed-and-reconcile tests — clean way to inject state into a void method. **Suite 564 → 578 (+14).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `CustomComponentLoader × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. **The custom-component family is now fully Phase 2 covered** — RowSizer (8) + RangeScanner (13) + Loader (14) = 35 tests across the family.
- Slice 61 — **Phase 2 tests for `CustomComponentRangeScanner`.** 13 tests. **First use of `Mockito.mockStatic` in this Phase 2 suite** — required because the scanner calls the static `Spreadsheet.getComponentNodeId(Component)` to mint cache keys, and there's no other way to control its return for a mocked Component. Wrapped in a `MockedStatic<Spreadsheet>` field with `@AfterEach close()` to scope the static stub per-test. Pinned the per-cell decision tree: (1) **Component-vs-editor dispatch** — if `getCustomComponentForCell` returns non-null, register + add to `componentIDtoCellKeysMap`; else if cell is NOT locked, fall through to `getCustomEditorForCell`, register + add to `cellKeysToEditorIdMap` (different map); locked cells skip the editor lookup entirely. (2) **Component-wins precedence** — when both component and editor would return non-null, only the component path runs (editor lookup never fires). (3) **Existing-components dedup** — a component already in the `existingComponents` set is NOT re-registered, but still ends up in `newCustomComponents` because that set is the "what's visible now" snapshot. (4) **Merged-region skip-ahead** — same pattern as slice 59 / 45: cells inside a merge (not anchor) skip both lookups AND jump the loop counter to `region.col2 - 1`. (5) **Exception swallowing** — a factory exception is logged and the loop continues to the next cell. (6) **Null-row pass-through** — when the sheet has no row at r, the scanner passes a null `Cell` down to the factory rather than aborting; the factory decides how to handle it. **Suite 551 → 564 (+13).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `CustomComponentRangeScanner × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓.
- Slice 60 — **Phase 2 tests for `CustomComponentRowSizer`.** 8 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Sheet` + `Row`. Three behaviors pinned: (1) **Grow**: rows hosting components are bumped to `minimumRowHeightForComponents` if smaller; rows already taller are left alone; hidden rows are NOT grown even when in the components set (keeping them hidden matters more than showing the component); (2) **Restore**: rows that previously hosted a component but no longer do get reset — to 0 if hidden, to POI row's `getHeightInPoints()` if the row exists, or to the sheet's `defaultRowHeightInPoints()` if POI returns null for the row (load-bearing because component-hosting rows may have been deleted from POI without being deleted from the height array); (3) **Memory**: `currentRowsWithComponents` field stores the latest input from the previous call so the next call can compute the diff — a first-call invocation has no restore set yet because the memory is null. The first-call-then-empty-set test sequence pins this explicitly. Rows in BOTH the previous and current set stay grown (no flicker). **Suite 543 → 551 (+8).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `CustomComponentRowSizer × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓.
- Slice 59 — **Phase 2 tests for `CellCommentRangeScanner`.** 11 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Sheet` + `MergedRegionContainer` + `Comment` + `RichTextString`. Pinned: text + author + visible-list harvest (text into `cellComments`, author into `cellCommentAuthors`, visible-only into `visibleCellComments`); invalid-formula marker harvest (separate set from comments); zero-height-row skip; hidden-column skip; **merged-region skip-ahead**: a cell that's inside a merged region but NOT the anchor doesn't get its comment scanned AND the loop counter jumps to `region.col2 - 1` (so the next iteration starts at col2 + 1, skipping the entire merge in one step — useful when iterating a wide range overlapping a wide merge); merged-region anchor IS scanned normally; defensive new-collection writes (existing entries from prior scans survive). Hit a Java naming clash: a `private static <T> T any()` test helper shadowed Mockito's `any(Class)` form, causing compile errors. Renamed away and used static-imported `any()` directly. **Suite 532 → 543 (+11).**
- Slice 58 — **Phase 2 tests for `CellCommentLoader`.** 9 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `CellCommentRangeScanner`. Mirrors `HyperlinkLoader`'s 4-quadrant scan dispatch but adds two extras: (1) **early-return** when `getFirstColumn() == -1` (spreadsheet not loaded yet, method will be re-called); (2) the **`topLeftLoaded` latch** — area 1 (frozen × frozen corner) is scanned at most ONCE per spreadsheet lifecycle because frozen panes don't scroll, so a re-scan would be wasted work. `resetTopLeftLoaded()` arms the latch again (used when a structural change like unfreeze+refreeze invalidates the cached state). Also pinned that all FOUR state maps (`cellComments`, `cellCommentAuthors`, `visibleCellComments`, `invalidFormulaCells`) are reset to fresh empty collections BEFORE any scan, in that order. **Suite 523 → 532 (+9).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cells for the cell-comment family flipped to ✓. **The cell-comment family is now fully Phase 2 covered.**
- Slice 57 — **Phase 2 tests for `HyperlinkRangeScanner`.** 11 tests, real `XSSFWorkbook` fixture + mocked `Spreadsheet` + `HyperlinkCellClickHandler` + mocked `XSSFHyperlink` / `Hyperlink`. Pinned: per-cell decision tree — XSSF link → tooltip text fallback-to-address; non-XSSF (HSSF/plain) → always address (no tooltip text path); HYPERLINK formula cell + registered handler → handler's target; HYPERLINK formula with null handler → silently skipped; null row / null cell guards; in-place mutation of the tooltips map (read from `getHyperlinksTooltips`, mutate, write back — load-bearing for the cross-range preservation invariant). **Found a Mockito + XSSFCell quirk**: `XSSFCell.setHyperlink(mock(XSSFHyperlink.class))` works for the FIRST cell in the sheet but fails to actually attach the mock on subsequent cells (or to cells in a freshly created row). Verified by a failing boundary test that expected to see a tooltip on a second hyperlink-mocked cell — the link was silently dropped. Pivoted the boundary test to use HYPERLINK formula cells instead (which don't require setHyperlink and exercise the same range-bounds logic). Worth knowing for future Phase 2 tests in this family.
- Slice 56 — **Phase 2 tests for `HyperlinkLoader`.** 6 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `HyperlinkRangeScanner`. Thin orchestrator — pinned: tooltips map reset BEFORE any scan call (full-rebuild contract, old tooltips dropped first); 4-quadrant scan dispatch identical to slice 53's overlay-visibility dispatch (both splits > 0 → area1 only fires; vertical only → skip area1+3; horizontal only → skip area1+2; area4 always fires); the `times(N)` upper-bound assertions catch any extra/missed scan calls. **Suite 506 → 523 (+17).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cells for `HyperlinkLoader` and `HyperlinkRangeScanner` flipped to ✓. **The hyperlink family is now fully Phase 2 covered** — both helpers tested.
- Slice 55 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetOverlayManager`.** 14 tests, ALL collaborators mocked (`Spreadsheet`, `SheetOverlayStorage`, `SheetOverlayVisibility`, `SheetOverlayInfoBuilder`). The orchestrator's complexity comes from the decision tree — pinned all branches: visible overlay → buildInfo + setVisible(true) + setOverlays(merged map); invisible-and-was-visible → setVisible(false) + setResource(null) + setOverlays(map-with-removed); invisible-and-never-was-visible → silently skipped (no setVisible call at all, the "was visible" check is on the overlay's own state); overlay with component → registerCustomComponent + storage.addComponent; overlay with resource handler → setResource only if resources map doesn't already contain the id (idempotent); overlay with null id → maps update skipped, no listener attached, but setVisible still fires; freshHashMap copy contract pinned via argThat (in-place mutation would skip the `MirroredProperty.serialized` mirror). Bypassed the static `SpreadsheetFactory.loadSheetOverlays` call by stubbing `storage.isEmpty()` to false in every test — the "empty → load from factory" branch is intentionally NOT exercised (would require static-method mocking, which is heavyweight). markReloadFromPoi test exercises the flag's observable effect (next loadOrUpdate clears the storage); the subsequent static factory call ran against a mock spreadsheet without throwing, which is a useful incidental signal that the factory is defensive enough to handle a mock. **Suite 492 → 506 (+14).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `SheetOverlayManager × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. **The slice-4 overlay family is now fully Phase 2 covered** — `SheetOverlayStorage` (12), `SheetOverlayVisibility` (10), `SheetOverlayInfoBuilder` (9), `SheetOverlayManager` (14) = 45 tests across the family.
- Slice 54 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetOverlayInfoBuilder`.** 9 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Sheet` + `ClientAnchor` + `XSSFClientAnchor` + the XML-bean shape types (`CTPoint2D`, `CTPositiveSize2D`). Pinned the two-branch contract: (1) `DONT_MOVE_AND_RESIZE` on XSSF anchor → absolute EMU coords, col=1 row=1, dx uses `EMU_PER_PIXEL` while dy/height use `EMU_PER_POINT` (load-bearing asymmetry matched to client-side rendering expectation); (2) regular cell-anchored path → +1 conversion to 1-based, skip-leading-hidden-columns/rows loop (col loop advances while `isColumnHidden(col)`, same for rows), conditional dx/dy assignment (dx only set if leading column WASN'T shifted by the hidden-skip; defaults to 0 if it was). Type field propagates from `overlay.getType()`. Hit a second POI/Mockito surprise: `SheetOverlayWrapper.getWidth` takes `(Sheet, int[] colW, int defaultColumnWidthPX)` — third arg is `int`, not `float`. My initial `anyFloat()` matcher hit a compile error before tests even ran. Caught in one cycle. **Suite 483 → 492 (+9).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `SheetOverlayInfoBuilder × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. **The slice-4 overlay family now has 3-of-4 members with Phase 2 coverage** — only `SheetOverlayManager` (the lifecycle orchestrator) remains.
- Slice 53 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetOverlayVisibility`.** 10 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `ClientAnchor` + `XSSFClientAnchor` + `SheetOverlayWrapper`. Hit a POI API surprise: `ClientAnchor.getCol1()` / `getCol2()` return `short` (not `int`!) while `getRow1()` / `getRow2()` return `int`. Caught by a Mockito stub compile error, fixed by casting the col stubs to `(short)`. Worth flagging — POI's `ClientAnchor` design has an int/short asymmetry that's easy to miss. Tests pin the visibility decision tree: (1) `DONT_MOVE_AND_RESIZE` on `XSSFClientAnchor` returns true unconditionally — workaround for [issue #3261](https://github.com/vaadin/flow-components/issues/3261), the impl can't tell whether x/y coords are in viewport; (2) the same anchor type on a non-XSSF anchor takes the normal path (special-case is XSSF-specific); (3) hidden-range gate is **all-or-nothing** — partial hidden columns/rows take the normal path; (4) type-2 anchors (col2==0 && row2==0) bypass the hidden-range gate entirely; (5) 4-quadrant freeze-pane visibility: with both splits > 0, all 4 areas are checked; any single area returning true makes the overlay visible (OR short-circuit). Used `lenient()` for the freeze-pane / viewport stubs because not every test exercises every method (strict stubbing would flag unused ones). **Suite 473 → 483 (+10).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `SheetOverlayVisibility × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. The slice-4 overlay family now has 2-of-4 members with Phase 2 coverage.
- Slice 52 — **Phase 3 spec for `SheetOverlayStorage`.** Pairs with slice 51's tests. Documents the two-collections-different-lifecycles design (lazy-init `overlays` vs eagerly-init `overlayComponents`), the destructive `initialize()` semantic (replaces with fresh empty set on reload), the live-reference return from `all()` (no defensive copy — callers can mutate the backing set, footgun pinned). The **dead-state observation** from slice 51 (`overlayComponents` is write-only with no readers) is escalated in the spec's "Non-obvious quirks" + "Human review" sections with a hypothesis: the set probably tracked component detachment on a prior Spreadsheet version, and the detach logic moved elsewhere while the field stayed orphaned. Three "Human review" questions: (1) confirm `overlayComponents` is genuinely dead (or document the consumer); (2) decide whether `add` should lazy-init for friendlier semantics; (3) decide whether `all()` should return an unmodifiable view to prevent the footgun. **11 specs total**: SequenceIncrement, ClientCellCache, ViewportBounds, ClientFlushQueue, ClientCacheFlusher, RangeSequenceDetector, MergedRegionLookup, MergedRegionCascade, MergedRegionOperations, SequenceValueExtractor, SheetOverlayStorage. The slice-4 overlay family now has 1-of-4 members with both Phase 2 + Phase 3 coverage.
- Slice 51 — **Phase 2 tests for `SheetOverlayStorage`.** 12 tests, mocked `SheetOverlayWrapper` + `Component` as opaque tokens. Two parallel collections in one class: `overlays` (lazy-init via `initialize()`, NPE pre-init for `add` / `clearOverlays` / `all`), `overlayComponents` (non-null from construction, works pre-init). Pinned both lifecycles and their asymmetry — load-bearing because Spreadsheet's workbook-load path is what triggers `initialize()`, so pre-initialize adds are a usage error that the storage explicitly NPEs on (no lazy-init to mask the bug). **Found and pinned a dead-state observation**: `overlayComponents` has only `addComponent` / `removeComponent` — no getter, no clear method. Grep across `src/main` confirms nothing reads the set back; it's purely written by `SheetOverlayManager` (line 102 attach, line 140 detach). The set is genuinely write-only from outside the class. Pinned in `overlayComponents_isWriteOnly` as a future-cleanup marker: either add a getter to expose the tracking, or remove the field entirely. The test itself just exercises the write API since the set isn't observable. **Suite 461 → 473 (+12).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `SheetOverlayStorage × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. (This is the first member of the slice-4 overlay family with Phase 2 coverage — the other three — `SheetOverlayVisibility`, `SheetOverlayInfoBuilder`, `SheetOverlayManager` — are still IT-only.)
- Slice 50 — **Phase 3 spec for `SequenceValueExtractor`.** Closes the slice-12 trio's documentation alongside `SequenceIncrement.md` (slice 25) and `RangeSequenceDetector.md` (slice 41). Captures: the **first-mismatch stop condition** (numeric variant stops at null cell OR non-NUMERIC type; string variant stops at null OR non-STRING; column variants additionally stop at null row); the **trailing-null fill** that keeps the returned array length at exactly `range + 1` regardless of when iteration stopped (load-bearing for downstream `SequenceIncrement.compute`, which scans linearly and exits on null); the **left-justified indexing** (`result[0]` corresponds to the leading position, not absolute column); the FORMULA-bypass quirk consistent with `RangeSequenceDetector` (a formula cell with cached numeric value stops the numeric variant because `getCellType()` returns FORMULA not NUMERIC); the silent-stop-no-distinction trade-off (the caller can't tell from the array whether iteration ended at null-cell vs wrong-type). Three "Human review" questions: (1) should `c1 > c2` be a precondition or tolerated (currently silent `NegativeArraySizeException`); (2) should the four near-identical methods be unified via templated generics; (3) should column variants take a precomputed row slice to be uniform with row variants. **10 specs total**: SequenceIncrement, ClientCellCache, ViewportBounds, ClientFlushQueue, ClientCacheFlusher, RangeSequenceDetector, MergedRegionLookup, MergedRegionCascade, MergedRegionOperations, SequenceValueExtractor. The slice-12 trio is now **fully tested AND fully specced**.
- Slice 49 — **Phase 2 tests for `PopupButtonRegistry`.** 10 tests, mocked `PopupButton` instances as opaque storage tokens. Thin storage class — most tests pin HashMap delegation (get/put/remove/values/clear/isEmpty), but the load-bearing `replaceAll` swap-semantic gets dedicated coverage (3 tests): replaceAll swaps the underlying map reference rather than merging, replaceAll with an empty map empties the registry, and the subtle trade-off that replaceAll takes a *reference* not a copy — so the caller must not retain the passed map (a mutation through the original reference still affects the registry's storage, pinned explicitly in the last test). The reference-not-copy semantic is mid-stream worth a domain-expert look: the `PopupButtonRowShifter` build-and-swap idiom usually discards the input map immediately, so the trade-off is fine in practice, but a future caller that reuses the input could create subtle bugs. **Suite 451 → 461 (+10).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `PopupButtonRegistry × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. (Note: the popup family still needs Phase 2 tests for `PopupButtonBinding`, `PopupButtonRowShifter`, `PopupButtonRefresher` — but the registry is the foundation, and the shifter's correctness depends on this registry's swap semantic being pinned.)
- Slice 48 — **Phase 2 tests for `MergedRegionSynchronizer`.** 7 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Sheet` + `MergedRegionOperations`. Pinned: the empty-sheet short-circuit (`setMergedRegions(null)`, NOT an empty ArrayList — load-bearing because null vs empty is observable on the `MirroredProperty.serialized` mirror); the per-region 1-based coordinate translation (POI's 0-based +1 → cache's 1-based); monotonic id mint via direct `mergedRegionCounter++` (matches the contract pinned in slice 45); the "POI is the source of truth" invariant — the synchronizer NEVER calls `spreadsheet.getMergedRegions()`, only writes the rebuilt list (this is the load-bearing distinction between bulk-rebuild and per-region add/remove); and that the happy path NEVER routes through `operations.addRegionToPoiAndCache` (which would re-fire the merge cascade for every existing region). The `addRegionToPoiAndCache` fallback path (intended for "POI reports a region not in the cache") is **flagged as apparently-unreachable dead code** in the test comments — the IOOB catch's position around the field-access block can't trigger for a well-formed POI `CellRangeAddress`, and the catch's stated intent (compare against cache) is mismatched against the actual code (which ignores the cache). Worth flagging for a follow-up cleanup or comment fix. Same for the trailing `while (poiRegionCount < rebuilt.size())` trim loop — rebuilt grows by 1 per iteration, so the condition is never true. Both pinned as legacy-defensive code rather than tested. **Suite 444 → 451 (+7).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `MergedRegionSynchronizer × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. **The merged-region module is now fully Phase 2 covered** — `MergedRegionLookup` (12), `MergedRegionCascade` (11), `MergedRegionOperations` (8), `MergedRegionSynchronizer` (7) = 38 tests.
- Slice 47 — **Phase 3 spec for `MergedRegionOperations`.** Closes the merged-region trio's Phase 3 coverage. Pins: validation-before-POI-write (OVERLAP/ENCLOSES throw before any mutation); the defensive new-ArrayList copy on every cache write (load-bearing because the cache is a `MirroredProperty.serialized` field and in-place mutations would skip the client mirror); monotonically increasing `mergedRegionCounter` via direct field access (same logical merge after unmerge/re-merge gets a new id on the client); the load-bearing index-aliasing assumption in `removeRegion` ("POI index == cache index" — implicit, not enforced); the asymmetric event-payload semantics (`selectionManager` callbacks receive the POI `CellRangeAddress`, the cascade receives the DTO `MergedRegion`); and the inside-region discard partial-state risk (a discard at index 0 happens immediately; if a later OVERLAP check throws, the discard is NOT rolled back). Three "Human review" questions: (1) is the POI/cache index-aliasing assumption documented anywhere; (2) should partial-state from inside-region discards be rolled back on exception; (3) is monotonically increasing `mergedRegionCounter` intentional or accidental. **9 specs total**: `SequenceIncrement`, `ClientCellCache`, `ViewportBounds`, `ClientFlushQueue`, `ClientCacheFlusher`, `RangeSequenceDetector`, `MergedRegionLookup`, `MergedRegionCascade`, `MergedRegionOperations`. The merged-region trio is now **fully tested AND fully specced** — Phase 2 + Phase 3 both green for all three members.
- Slice 46 — **Phase 3 spec for `MergedRegionCascade`.** Pairs with slice 43's tests. Pins: the wider-by-one cascade area (a 2x2 merge walks 3x3 cells because the row/column just past the bottom-right needs its borders re-rendered); the asymmetry between `afterMerge` (only marks inside-non-anchor cells for removal — anchor stays + outer +1 cells stay) and `afterUnmerge` (every existing cell in the cascade area gets `markCellForUpdate`); the `updateCustomBorders=false` per-cell + bulk `loadCustomBorderStylesToState` at the end (avoids quadratic style work); the null-row vs null-cell asymmetry in `afterUnmerge` (null row skips entirely → no `clearCellStyle`; null cell inside an existing row triggers `clearCellStyle(r, c)` by coordinate). The asymmetry is **flagged as a "Human review" question** — it's plausibly a bug (a null row escapes the clear-by-coordinate path that a null cell would trigger), but pinned as-is because the IT tests are stable. Two more Human-review questions added: (2) should `markCellForUpdate` fire for the +1 cells whose values haven't changed; (3) should the cascade survive a thrown exception mid-loop. Saved to `/refactor-artifacts/specs/MergedRegionCascade.md`. **8 specs total**: SequenceIncrement, ClientCellCache, ViewportBounds, ClientFlushQueue, ClientCacheFlusher, RangeSequenceDetector, MergedRegionLookup, MergedRegionCascade. The merged-region trio now has Phase 2 + Phase 3 coverage for 2 of 3 (lookup + cascade); `MergedRegionOperations` is the obvious next spec to close it.
- Slice 45 — **Phase 2 tests for `MergedRegionOperations`.** 8 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` + `Sheet` + `MergedRegionCascade` (`final`, but Mockito 5+ inline-mock-maker on the `-javaagent` surefire flag handles final classes transparently) + `CellSelectionManager`. Stateful `AtomicReference<ArrayList<MergedRegion>>` carries the cached region list across the `getMergedRegions` / `setMergedRegions` round-trip — that pattern reproduces the parent's stateful getter/setter pair without instantiating a real `Spreadsheet`. Pinned: happy path (POI mutated, region built with 1-based coords + mint id 0, cascade fired, selection manager notified, `updateMarkedCells` called exactly once); the **defensive new-ArrayList copy** semantic — the operation builds a fresh list rather than mutating the prior reference in place (load-bearing because the cache is a `MirroredProperty.serialized` field, and in-place mutations would skip the client mirror); sequential id mint via direct field access on `mergedRegionCounter` (post-increment is observable from outside, even on a mock — fields default to 0 and Mockito doesn't intercept direct field access on a CGLib subclass); OVERLAP rejection (`IllegalArgumentException` with "overlaps" in message, no POI mutation); ENCLOSES rejection (different message, also no POI mutation); INSIDE-region discard (silently removes the existing region first, then adds the new one, both cascades fire); `removeRegion` ordering pinned via `InOrder`: `sheet.removeMergedRegion` → `cascade.afterUnmerge` → `spreadsheet.updateMarkedCells` → `selectionManager.mergedRegionRemoved`. Pinned the **load-bearing assumption** that the integer index passed to `removeRegion` aligns the POI list and the cache list — the operation doesn't verify alignment, so a misaligned cache would remove the wrong region from one side. Worth flagging for review. **Suite 436 → 444 (+8).** Spotless + checkstyle clean.
