Coverage matrix cell `MergedRegionOperations × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. **The merged-region trio is now fully tested** — `MergedRegionLookup` (slice 42, 12), `MergedRegionCascade` (slice 43, 11), `MergedRegionOperations` (slice 45, 8).
- Slice 44 — **Phase 3 spec for `MergedRegionLookup`.** Pairs with slice 42's tests. First spec for a block that implements an existing client-side interface (`MergedRegionUtil.MergedRegionContainer`) — the contract is part of an already-public API surface rather than a new internal one. Captures: live-view-no-caching semantic (the supplier is consulted every call; mutations to the underlying list are visible on the next query); first-match-wins ordering for both query methods; null-supplier-result silence (returns null cleanly without NPE); and the load-bearing distinction between exact-anchor match (`getMergedRegionStartingFrom`) vs containment match (`getMergedRegion`). Pinned the supplier-indirection-enables-non-Mockito-test lesson explicitly — the slice 42 test file uses a one-line lambda + `AtomicReference::set` for mid-test overrides rather than Mockito. **7 specs total**: `SequenceIncrement`, `ClientCellCache`, `ViewportBounds`, `ClientFlushQueue`, `ClientCacheFlusher`, `RangeSequenceDetector`, `MergedRegionLookup`. The merged-region trio now has Phase 2 for two members + Phase 3 for one; closing it (Phase 2 for `MergedRegionOperations`, Phase 3 for the cascade + operations) is the obvious next move.
- Slice 43 — **Phase 2 tests for `MergedRegionCascade`.** 11 tests, mocked `Spreadsheet` / `SpreadsheetStyleFactory` / `CellValueManager` + real `XSSFWorkbook` for the iteration. Pinned the wider-by-one cascade area for both methods (a 2x2 merge at (1,1)-(2,2) iterates 3x3 = 9 cells, not 4 — because the cells just past the bottom-right corner need their borders re-rendered). Pinned the markCellForRemove count for `afterMerge`: 3 calls for a 2x2 merge (inside-but-not-anchor cells), 0 calls for a 1x1 merge (anchor IS the only inside cell). Pinned the dual-mode branch in `afterUnmerge`: existing cells get `cellStyleUpdated` + `markCellForUpdate`; MISSING cells get `clearCellStyle(row, col)` BY COORDINATE — the cascade explicitly clears cached style for cells that never had a POI object. Pinned the null-row vs null-cell asymmetry: a null row aborts the whole row (no `clearCellStyle` for any column in that row), whereas a null cell INSIDE an existing row triggers `clearCellStyle`. The asymmetry is non-obvious — could plausibly be a bug, but the IT tests are stable, so pinning it as-is. Found and corrected the (row, col) parameter order on `clearCellStyle` mid-run; one failing test cycle taught the precise call-order semantic before the rest of the suite locked in. Also pinned the per-cell `updateCustomBorders=false` invariant — both cascades use the cheap form per-cell and call `loadCustomBorderStylesToState` exactly once at the end (avoids quadratic style refresh). **Suite 425 → 436 (+11).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `MergedRegionCascade × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓.
- Slice 42 — **Phase 2 tests for `MergedRegionLookup`.** 12 tests, **no mocking framework needed** — the lookup takes a `Supplier<List<MergedRegion>>`, so injection is a one-liner lambda and overrides are a one-liner reassignment. That's a useful general lesson: when a refactor introduces a supplier/factory indirection at the seam, downstream tests stop needing Mockito. Pinned both methods: `getMergedRegionStartingFrom` (exact-corner match — pinned by an inner-cell-returns-null counter-test so the distinction from the other method is load-bearing), `getMergedRegion` (containment with inclusive ≤ on all four bounds — pinned by both the top-left, the bottom-right, and a "just outside" cluster test covering col2+1, row2+1, col1-1, row1-1). First-match-wins iteration order pinned for both methods (degenerate but legal state). Null-supplier and live-mutation tests pin the "no caching" semantic — the lookup re-reads the supplier each call, so a backing-list `.add` after construction is visible on the next query. **Suite 413 → 425 (+12).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `MergedRegionLookup × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓.
- Slice 41 — **Phase 3 spec for `RangeSequenceDetector`.** Closes the slice-12 trio: `SequenceIncrement` (pure math), `SequenceValueExtractor` (POI read), `RangeSequenceDetector` (dispatch glue). Spec covers the 89-LOC dispatcher's two static methods (`getRowIncrement`, `getColumnIncrement`) — the four null-return shortcuts (missing row, missing leading cell, non-STRING/NUMERIC type, non-progression from `SequenceIncrement.compute`); the leading-cell-type-only dispatch (trailing cells don't affect the type pick); the 1-based-indexing convention enforced at the dispatcher level, not in the extractor. Pinned the FORMULA-bypass quirk explicitly — a formula cell with a cached numeric result returns `null` from the detector, matching Excel's drag-fill semantics where formulas drag their text not their cached value. The 13 existing tests in `RangeSequenceDetectorTest` cover every invariant. Saved to `/refactor-artifacts/specs/RangeSequenceDetector.md`. **6 specs total now**: `SequenceIncrement`, `ClientCellCache`, `ViewportBounds`, `ClientFlushQueue`, `ClientCacheFlusher`, `RangeSequenceDetector`. The slice-12 trio is now fully specced.
- Slice 40 — **Phase 2 tests for `ClientCacheFlusher`.** 6 tests using real `XSSFWorkbook` for the sheet + real `ClientCellCache` and `ClientFlushQueue` (lightweight enough to assert post-conditions directly) + mocked `Spreadsheet` / `CellValueManager` / `ConditionalFormatter` / `SpreadsheetClientRpc` for the outgoing side effects. Pinned the six cases the slice-38 spec explicitly called out: empty sheet (no RPC, no event), one marked plain cell (one `cellsUpdated` + `refreshCellStyles` pair, cache marked, queue cleared), formula cell with stale cache (eager re-flush even without explicit mark), conditional formatting on non-marked cell (in-band CF marking lifts the cell into the batch within the same iteration), changed-formula-cells (ordering pinned via `InOrder` — `fireFormulaValueChangeEvent` runs BEFORE `cellsUpdated` per the spec's ordering invariant), and the null-`createCellDataForCell` fallback (formula cell gets a synthesized minimal `CellData` with col/row/cellStyle). Used `ArgumentCaptor<ArrayList>` for the fallback batch inspection (with `@SuppressWarnings({"unchecked", "rawtypes"})` because the captor on `ArrayList<CellData>` requires the raw type). **Suite 407 → 413 (+6).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `ClientCacheFlusher × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓. The slice-13b trio (`ClientCellCache`, `ClientFlushQueue`, `ClientCacheFlusher`) is now fully specced AND fully tested — Phase 2 + Phase 3 both green for that group.
- Slice 34 — **`MirroredNullableMap<K, V>` sibling abstraction + migration of `cellKeysToEditorIdMap`.** Designed and built the second sibling abstraction in the same family as `MirroredProperty<T>` — but as a **separate class** rather than a `MirroredProperty` variant. The null-coalescing semantic differs in two ways that broke the `MirroredProperty.BiConsumer<Element, T>` strategy abstraction: (1) the field has a non-null default (empty HashMap) instead of a typed initial value, (2) `set(null)` MUTATES state (calls `.clear()` on the existing reference) rather than replacing it. Trying to shoehorn this into the existing class would obscure both differences. Wrote `MirroredNullableMap.java` (~80 LOC) + `MirroredNullableMapTest.java` (8 tests pinning the surprising `set(null)` semantic: clears in place + preserves caller's reference + still pushes serialized null to client). Then migrated **`cellKeysToEditorIdMap`** — field declaration changed from `private HashMap<String, String> ... = new HashMap<>()` to `private final MirroredNullableMap<String, String> ... = new MirroredNullableMap<>(this, "cellKeysToEditorIdMap")`. Setter body collapsed from 7 lines to 1. Getter `cellKeysToEditorIdMap` → `cellKeysToEditorIdMap.get()`. One direct in-place call at line 1678 (`!cellKeysToEditorIdMap.isEmpty()` inside an `if`) updated to `.get().isEmpty()`. **Suite 384 → 392 (+8 from MirroredNullableMapTest).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Design note: created as a SEPARATE class on principle — when the abstraction differs in two semantic dimensions from its sibling, forcing it into the same class via lambdas/strategies obscures more than it shares. Worth capturing this design heuristic in the slice-plan skill's abstraction-flavor section.
- Slice 35 — **Phase 3 spec for `ClientFlushQueue`.** Companion spec to `ClientCellCache.md` from slice 29. Both helpers are slice-13b siblings used in tandem by `ClientCacheFlusher`. The spec captures the most surprising semantic of `ClientFlushQueue` explicitly: **`clear()` does NOT touch `changedFormulaCells`** — the field has its own drain-and-replace lifecycle via `drainChangedFormulaCells()`. The three queues have two lifecycles (marked/removed paired, changedFormula independent), pinned in `clear_drainsMarkedAndRemoved_butLeavesChangedFormulaCells`. Saved to `/refactor-artifacts/specs/ClientFlushQueue.md`. Now 4 specs total: `SequenceIncrement`, `ClientCellCache`, `ViewportBounds`, `ClientFlushQueue`. Cross-references between paired specs noted as TODO for the next pass (CompanionBlocks section).
- Slice 36 — **Phase 2 tests for `DefaultStyleBaseline`.** 6 tests with mocked `Spreadsheet` (returning a real XSSFWorkbook) + real POI `CellStyle` / `Font` fixtures. Pinned: fresh-baseline defaults (null Object, 0 short), `setDefaultTextAlign` simple storage, `captureDefaultStyle(defaultStyle, sb)` dual-action behavior (captures font into baseline fields AND emits CSS into StringBuilder), no-op on non-default styles (sb untouched, baseline untouched), bold + italic flag detection. Hit a POI API surprise: `CellStyle.getFont(Workbook)` doesn't exist — use `workbook.getFontAt(cellStyle.getFontIndexAsInt())` instead. Caught and fixed on first compile. **Suite 392 → 398 (+6).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `DefaultStyleBaseline × unit-fix + char` flipped to ✓.
- Slice 31 — **MirroredProperty `serialized` rollout — 3 more fields migrated.** Used the existing `MirroredProperty.serialized` factory (no new variant needed) for `sheetNames` (String[]), `overlays` (HashMap<String, OverlayInfo>), `mergedRegions` (ArrayList<MergedRegion>). All Pattern B (Has-cache, serialized, no null-coalescing). Originally planned 4 fields; `namedRanges` turned out to be Pattern D (write-only, no field) per slice 28's pattern-discovery learning. **Hit one snag mid-migration:** `overlays.clear()` at line 1673 was a direct in-place mutation on the field. After migrating, `overlays` is now a `MirroredProperty<HashMap>` wrapper with no `clear()` method — caller needed updating to `overlays.get().clear()`, plus the null-check above changed from `overlays != null` to `overlays.get() != null` (the wrapper is never null; the inner map can be). Fix took one edit, compile caught it immediately. **Total migrated after slice 31: 9 fields** (workbookChangeToggle, sheetProtected, rows, cols, infoLabelValue, sheetIndex, sheetNames, overlays, mergedRegions). **Suite 375 → 375 (no new tests; behavior preserved).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. New learning worth capturing: when migrating a field that's referenced by direct in-place mutation (not via setter), the callers need updating to `field.get().<mutation>()`. Worth adding to the refactor-slice-plan skill's grep-before-compile step as a specific check for `.<method>()` calls on the field name.
- Slice 32 — **Phase 3 spec for `ViewportBounds`.** Third natural-language spec. Smallest block to date (70 LOC source, 10 tests). Demonstrates the template works for trivial state-holder blocks where most sections are short. Notable: Error modes section is "None — the block cannot throw" (legitimate; the class has no inputs that fail). Pinned the `update(-1,-1,-1,-1)` sentinel as the "viewport not yet known" contract with the explicit caller (`setSpreadsheetComponentFactory`'s `getFirstRow() != -1` check). Saved to `/refactor-artifacts/specs/ViewportBounds.md`. Three Phase-3 specs now: `SequenceIncrement` (pure logic), `ClientCellCache` (state holder + prune operations), `ViewportBounds` (trivial state holder). Template works across the spectrum.
- Slice 33 — **Phase 2 tests for `FreezePaneState`.** 9 tests with Mockito + mocked `Element`. Pinned: defaults (splits=0, scroll arrays=null), construction does NOT eagerly mirror, both axes independent for both split positions and scroll arrays, getters return the SAME reference (no defensive copy — pinned via `assertSame`), null scroll-position arrays get serialized and pushed (Serializer.serialize handles null, producing a JSON null), setting one axis doesn't touch the other's property. Hit two snags on first run: (1) used `elemental.json.JsonValue` import that doesn't exist in this Vaadin Flow version — `Serializer.serialize` returns `String`, not `JsonValue`; (2) Mockito matcher-mixing issue with `setProperty(literal, anyXxx())` — fixed by wrapping literals in `eq(...)`. Both fixed on second attempt. **Suite 375 → 384 (+9).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `FreezePaneState × unit + char` flipped to ✓; row now has 6/7 ✓. Phase-2 process insight: **`Serializer.serialize` returns String, not JsonValue.** This means `setProperty(name, Serializer.serialize(x))` routes through the `setProperty(String, String)` overload, not `setProperty(String, JsonValue)` as one might expect from "serialized". The `MirroredProperty.serialized` factory's lambda matches this: `el.setProperty(name, Serializer.serialize(v))` — the `v` becomes `String` via serialize, then String overload dispatch. Worth capturing in the spec for `MirroredProperty` if/when one is written.
- Slice 28 — **MirroredProperty rollout cont. — `sheetIndex` migrated (+ scope discovery for `id` and `invalidFormulaErrorMessage`).** Originally planned to migrate three fields. Investigation revealed that `id` (line 148) and `invalidFormulaErrorMessage` (line 4675) are both Pattern D — write-only mirrors with NO server-side cache (just `getElement().setProperty(name, value)`, no `this.X = value` assignment). They don't fit MirroredProperty because there's no cache to maintain. The `id` getter goes through `getElement().getProperty(...)` so the client is the single source of truth; same for `invalidFormulaErrorMessage`. **Only `sheetIndex` migrated** — straightforward int field, getter at line 265, setter at line 429. Total migrated fields after slice 28: 6 (workbookChangeToggle, sheetProtected, rows, cols, infoLabelValue, sheetIndex). **Skill insight worth capturing**: when surveying MirroredProperty candidates, distinguish Pattern A/B (has field, cache to maintain) from Pattern D (no field, write-only). Only A/B fits; D doesn't have anything to abstract. Should update the `refactor-slice-plan` skill's abstraction-flavor section to call this out.
- Slice 29 — **Phase 3 spec for `ClientCellCache`.** Second natural-language spec. Validates the template works for state-holding blocks (not just pure-logic blocks like `SequenceIncrement`). Spec sections: Purpose, Inputs (per-method), Outputs (per-method), Side effects (none outside own state), Invariants (5 explicit invariants, all map to passing tests), Edge cases (key in both sets, empty range, prune of column 3 vs 30 prefix discipline, empty cache), Error modes (null key → NPE from HashSet, Integer.MIN/MAX OK, malformed keys persist), Non-obvious quirks (dropSent short-circuit, pruneRowsRange uses loop index not key-derived row, no interaction with ClientFlushQueue), Source links, Contract status (every invariant maps to a passing test in ClientCellCacheTest.java's 13 tests), What the spec doesn't cover (performance, thread safety, serializable edge cases), Human review (3 specific questions for the reviewer). Saved to `/refactor-artifacts/specs/ClientCellCache.md`. The template proved its worth a second time — the 11-section skeleton captured everything a future architectural-refactor operator would need to know. State-holding blocks need a slightly different Invariants section (state-machine-style "after X, Y holds") vs pure-logic Invariants (mathematical correctness); both fit the template.
- Slice 30 — **Phase 2 unit tests for `ViewportBufferConfig`.** 7 tests with Mockito + mocked `Element`. Pinned: both axes default to 200, construction does NOT eagerly mirror (matches `MirroredProperty<T>` contract — pinned as a precondition for any future migration), each setter updates cache + pushes via `setProperty(name, int)`, axes are independent (setting row buffer doesn't push column property), no equality-check deduplication on repeat sets (matches the original setX behavior). Hit Mockito's `InvalidUseOfMatchers` on first run when mixing literal-string with `anyInt()` — fixed by wrapping the literal in `eq(...)`. **Suite 368 → 375 (+7).** Spotless + checkstyle clean. Coverage matrix cell `ViewportBufferConfig × unit + char` flipped from blank to ✓. **Process note**: writing tests for ViewportBufferConfig naturally surfaces the contract that any MirroredProperty migration of this helper must preserve — the spec-via-tests pattern starts paying dividends when one Phase-2 helper's tests become acceptance criteria for a future Phase-1 (abstraction) slice on the same helper.
- Slice 27 — **MirroredProperty rollout — 4 more fields migrated.** Continuing the slice-26 PoC. Migrated: `sheetProtected` (boolean, simple set/get), `rows` (int, multiple read sites including the public `getRows()`), `cols` (int, with internal `getCols()` and a self-comparison `if (getCols() != cols)` site that becomes `if (getCols() != cols)` unchanged — the parameter named `cols` shadows the field but Java's name resolution favors the parameter), `infoLabelValue` (String, private getter + setter pair). Per-migration mechanics: change `private boolean X` → `private final MirroredProperty<Boolean> X = MirroredProperty.bool(this, "X", initial)`, change each `return X` → `return X.get()`, change each `setX(value) { this.X = value; getElement().setProperty(...) }` body → `this.X.set(value)`. Each migration is ~3-5 lines edited per field. **Suite 368 → 368** (no new tests; the migrations preserve behavior). Spotless + checkstyle clean throughout. **Total migrated fields after slice 27: 5** (workbookChangeToggle + sheetProtected + rows + cols + infoLabelValue). Remaining simple-primitive candidates in Spreadsheet (per grep of `getElement().setProperty` call sites): ~13 (id, reload-timestamp, dirty-timestamp, sheetIndex, workbookProtected (no-field variant), hasActions (no-field variant), displayGridlines (no-field), displayRowColHeadings (no-field), showCustomEditorOnFocus (no-field), invalidFormulaErrorMessage, appId, ...). The "no-field" variants are write-only mirrors that don't need MirroredProperty — they can stay as direct setProperty calls because there's no cache to maintain. The serialized-mirror group (~12 fields like cellComments, hyperlinksTooltips, overlays, mergedRegions, namedRanges) still needs a null-coalescing variant of MirroredProperty before migration. Future slice 28 candidates: id, sheetIndex, invalidFormulaErrorMessage. The MirroredProperty abstraction is proving itself at scale — each migration is mechanical, low-risk, and incrementally collapses ~3 lines of boilerplate.
- Slice 26 — **`MirroredProperty<T>` abstraction PoC — built + tested + first migration landed.** Last turn's "deferred" verdict reconsidered. Designed a single-class abstraction with a `BiConsumer<Element, T>` strategy and four factory methods (`bool`, `integer`, `string`, `serialized`) covering the four common dispatch patterns in `Element.setProperty`. Key design decision: **construction does NOT eagerly sync the initial value** — the original setX() pattern only mirrored on explicit set, and the Flow framework pushes initial state through its own mechanism; mirroring at construction would shift initial-render timing. Documented in the constructor with a comment + pinned in `MirroredPropertyTest.construction_doesNotEagerlySync`. Wrote `MirroredProperty.java` (~118 LOC) + `MirroredPropertyTest.java` (7 tests covering: no-eager-sync, get-returns-initial, set-updates-and-pushes for each factory type, null-value handling for string mirror, idempotent-pushes-on-same-value confirming no equality-check de-duplication). Then migrated **one field** as PoC proof: `workbookChangeToggle` in `Spreadsheet.java` from `private boolean` + manual setX to `private final MirroredProperty<Boolean>` + delegating set/get. Three lines changed in Spreadsheet; the setter body collapsed from 2 lines to 1. **Suite 351 → 368 (+17 across slices 24+26), all green on first run.** Spotless + checkstyle clean. The migration validates the abstraction works under real conditions; the remaining ~17 boolean/primitive setters in Spreadsheet are now candidates for follow-up slices applying the same pattern. Note: the 12 Group-B (Serialized) setters with null-coalescing semantics (e.g. `setCellKeysToEditorIdMap` which `clears()` instead of replacing on null) need a richer abstraction or stay manual — `MirroredProperty.serialized` doesn't handle the null-clear case. Future slice: design an extension or split factory for null-coalescing serialized mirrors.
- Slice 22 — **Phase 2 slice (unit-fix tier on `RangeSequenceDetector`, unit tier on `ViewportBounds`).** Two helpers: `RangeSequenceDetectorTest.java` (13 tests, XSSFWorkbook fixture; pins numeric/string sequence detection, missing-row early-out, type-gate that rejects FORMULA/BOOLEAN leading cells, negative increments, 1-based indexing alignment with POI 0-based), `ViewportBoundsTest.java` (10 tests, pure data structure; pins primitive-int defaults, atomic update, per-axis `hasChanged` predicates, the `update(-1,-1,-1,-1)` sentinel for "viewport not yet known"). **Suite 328 → 351 tests** (+23, all green on first run). Spotless + checkstyle clean. Two more rows of the Phase 2 coverage matrix flip to `✓`: `RangeSequenceDetector × unit-fix`, `ViewportBounds × unit`.
- **Slice 23 — `MirroredProperty<T>` abstraction — DEFERRED.** Initial attempt to design the abstraction surfaced real subtleties: `Element.setProperty` has different overloads for primitives (`int`/`boolean`/`double` resolving via widening) vs serialized objects (`JsonValue`), so a single `MirroredProperty<T>` API can't cleanly handle both groups without either (a) a sub-typed hierarchy (`MirroredFlag` / `MirroredNumeric` / `MirroredArray`) or (b) a factory-method pattern that hides the dispatch behind a `BiConsumer<Element, T>` strategy. 30 setProperty call-sites still in `Spreadsheet.java` (most others moved to slice 14/15 helpers): roughly 18 primitive setters + 12 serialized setters. Real abstraction opportunity (the pattern is genuinely repeated > 20 times), but each call-site has small per-field quirks (default values, null-coalescing setters, paired field/element-property naming asymmetries) that need design care. Not a half-day slice. Documented in task #55 as deferred; would benefit from a focused RFC-style design pass before implementation. Per the pipeline's Phase-1 abstraction-flavor warning (§10 Rule C): "Abstraction slices are riskier than the other two because the new generic helper becomes a vocabulary the rest of the codebase has to learn." Right call to not rush it.
- **`refactor-history` skill dogfood (slice 1 helper).** Ran the skill manually against `SpreadsheetHistoryManager.java` to validate the tooling and the prompt structure. Discovery: **`gh` CLI is not installed in this environment**, so the GitHub-API tier of the skill is unavailable. The git-only tier works fine. Mined output: 8 distinct authors over the file's lifetime, 9 substantive non-housekeeping commits (the rest are license-header / formatting noise), original PR is "Undo Redo #35" from 2014-06-17 (legacy `vaadin/spreadsheet` repo) by Teppo Kurki. Subsequent active development in 2015 (CellValueChangeEvent set semantics, Focusable refactor, scroll-on-shift fix), then a long gap, then a "communication working" commit in 2021 from the Flow port. **Skill updated** with an explicit "Setup check (start here)" section that names three tiers (best=git+gh, ok=git-only, limited=shallow-clone) and asks the operator to state which tier they're on at the top of the output. The skill degrades gracefully from gh-rich to git-only without surprises.
- Slice 21 — **First Phase-2 slice (unit tier on `ClientCellCache` + `ClientFlushQueue`).** Two new test files: `ClientCellCacheTest.java` (13 tests) and `ClientFlushQueueTest.java` (9 tests). Both helpers are pure-logic data structures from slice 13b; tests are `unit` tier (no POI fixture needed). Pinned behaviors include: try-plain-first dropSent semantics with the documented quirk that a key in both sets only drops from plain; `pruneRowsRange` returns `List<CellData>` with each removal carrying the loop's row index + the column extracted from the key; `pruneColumn` distinguishes `col3` from `col30` via the `"col<N> r"` prefix discipline; `clear()` on `ClientFlushQueue` drains the two flush queues but **not** `changedFormulaCells` — that field gets drained-and-replaced via `drainChangedFormulaCells()` so callers can iterate the snapshot without concurrent-modification risk. **Suite 306 → 328 tests** (+22, all green on first run). Spotless + checkstyle clean. Both helpers' rows in the Phase 2 coverage matrix flip from blank to `✓` for the `unit` column. New `char` annotations in the test javadocs explicitly say "Pin the original idiom: ..." — every characterization point is documented inline. Demonstrates the Phase 2 slice flavor proposed in the pipeline V2 rewrite (§12). Next slice candidate (slice 22): `unit-fix` tests for one of `CellValueShifter` / `RuleEvaluator` / `CellBorderStyleBuilder` — POI-bound but pure logic, fits the dominant Phase-2 backlog tier.
- **PIPELINE V2 — Phases 1-5 + cross-cutting concerns.** Ugur asked: "rethink the pipeline considering all these (repo-history mining, behavior pinning across test tiers, natural-language specs, architectural refactor with public API preserved, method-level decomposition, human checkpoint policy)." Restructured the pipeline doc to explicitly call out five phases. Phase 1 (structural decomposition, §0-§11) is what we've been doing across slices 1-18. Phases 2-5 were added as §12-§15 outlines with enough concrete content to act on. §16 introduces multi-repo history mining as a cross-cutting concern, backed by a new `refactor-history` skill. §17 articulates the human-checkpoint policy: the pipeline minimizes human input, gating humans to decisions only the LLM can guess wrong (domain meaning, intentional-vs-bug, architectural taste, scope), automating everything that has an oracle answer (compile, lint, tests, payload diff). Eight explicit checkpoints named; "two emails" rule for checkpoint cost. The journal entries for slices 1-19 are all retroactively Phase-1 slices. Future phases get their own slice flavors. Created `refactor-history` skill at `.claude/skills/refactor-history/SKILL.md` — self-contained per the existing skill conventions, multi-repo aware (Spreadsheet's history lives in both `vaadin/flow-components` and the legacy `vaadin/spreadsheet`), filters aggressively (skip dependabot, skip auto-generated PR descriptions, prioritize > 100-word human writing). Six new skills now: `refactor-slice-plan`, `refactor-concerns`, `refactor-characterize`, `refactor-verify`, `refactor-review`, `refactor-history`. All self-contained; none reads the pipeline doc at runtime. Pipeline V2 changes the journal's framing — future slice entries should state which Phase + flavor they belong to (e.g. "Phase 2 slice — characterization tier for ClientCellCache").
- Slice 17 — **First slice on `ConditionalFormatter` (883 LOC, untouched until now).** Three new package-private helpers: **`POIReflectionTools`** (~212 LOC, all-static utility class — `getFieldVal`, `getOrderedRuleList`, `stopHere`, `isBorderSet`, `hasStrikeThrough` — bridges into POI's hidden XML state where the POI public API doesn't expose what conditional-formatting needs). **`RuleBorderTracker`** (~176 LOC, `(ColorConverter)` constructor; owns the two `topBorders` / `leftBorders` maps that track which CF rules emit spillover borders to adjacent cells, plus the `addBorderFormatting` body — extracts border CSS from a rule and registers any spillover styles. Takes `colorConverter` by constructor injection because that field stays `protected` on the parent — Rule A trap, well-documented in the javadoc). **`RuleEvaluator`** (~289 LOC, `(Spreadsheet)` constructor; owns the full rule-evaluation pipeline `matches` → `matchesValue` / `matchesFormula` → `getValueEvalFromFormula` + the typing predicates `hasCoherentType` / `isCoherentTypeFormula`. Pure logic; no state mutation on cell or workbook). Removed from `ConditionalFormatter`: 2 fields (`topBorders`, `leftBorders`), 11 methods (the 5 reflection helpers, `addBorderFormatting`, the 5 evaluation methods + 2 type-checker helpers). All `protected` methods (`matches`, `matchesFormula`, `matchesValue`) preserved as thin delegators per Rule A — subclass override surface stays intact. The `colorConverter` field stays on the parent (protected) with `RuleBorderTracker` taking it by constructor — pattern established in slice 15 for protected fields. **Suite 306/0/0/4 green**, spotless + checkstyle clean. `ConditionalFormatter.java` shrunk from **883 → 349 LOC (−534, −60.5%)** — biggest LOC-percentage win since slice 12. Honest flavor: **decomposition** — `RuleEvaluator` carries ~280 LOC of real type-comparison and formula-evaluation logic; `RuleBorderTracker` carries ~90 LOC of XML-aware border-CSS assembly; `POIReflectionTools` carries reflective-access logic used across all three. The remaining `ConditionalFormatter` is the orchestrator `createConditionalFormatterRules` + `runCellMatcher` + delegators. Two small process notes: (1) After deleting orphan methods with `awk`, a leftover javadoc block remained (the old `getOrderedRuleList`'s comment) with a dangling `*/`. Compile error caught it instantly. Lesson: when using `awk` to delete by line range, the *javadoc* preceding the deleted method usually ends up orphaned — always check the boundary before/after the deletion range. (2) The new `RuleEvaluator` referenced `WorkbookEvaluatorUtil` which lives in a different package (`org.apache.poi.ss.formula`); the import had to be added. Lesson: when extracting a method that uses a fully-qualified type alias, check imports in the new file even if the method body looks identical.
- Slice 16 — **First slice on `SpreadsheetStyleFactory` (1,107 LOC, untouched until now).** Three new package-private helpers: **`DefaultStyleBaseline`** (~98 LOC, `(Spreadsheet)` constructor; owns `defaultFont` / `defaultFontFamily` / `defaultFontHeightInPoints` / `defaultTextAlign` — the four cached fields captured from workbook style 0; methods: `captureDefaultStyle(CellStyle, sb)` which both writes the field set AND appends the default font's CSS to the supplied StringBuilder, plus simple getters/setters. The captureAndEmit pattern is preserved verbatim from the original `defaultFontStyle` — both effects in one call). **`FontStyleComposer`** (~135 LOC, `(Spreadsheet, DefaultStyleBaseline)` constructor; methods: `appendFontStyle(sb, cellStyle)` which emits CSS for any font property that differs from the baseline, plus the `static styleFontFamily(Font)` utility that's called from both this composer and `DefaultStyleBaseline.captureDefaultStyle` — static so it works before any composer instance exists during baseline capture). **`CellBorderStyleBuilder`** (~607 LOC, `(Spreadsheet, ColorConverter)` constructor; owns all five border maps `shiftedBorderTopStyles` / `shiftedBorderLeftStyles` / `mergedCellBorders` plus the two temp accumulators; exposes the bulk of the border-CSS logic via `appendBorderStyles(sb, cellStyle)` + `processCustomStyling(cell)` + `flattenAccumulators()` + `removeCellFromShiftedStyles(out, selector)` + `removeCellFromInternalShiftedMaps(selector)` + `stripLeadingSelectorsFromShiftedMaps()` + `buildMergedCellBorderCss(selector, rules)` + `appendShiftedStylesToOutput(out)` + `resetAll()` / `resetAccumulators()`. Internal private helpers: `insertMapEntryIfNeeded`, `updateStyleMap`, `getBorderRightStyle`, `getBorderBottomStyle`). Removed from `SpreadsheetStyleFactory`: all 5 border-map fields, all 4 default-style fields, 10 private methods (`buildMergedCellBorderCSS`, `doCellCustomStyling`, `insertMapEntryIfNeeded`, `updateStyleMap`, `defaultFontStyle`, `fontStyle`, `styleFontFamily`, `getBorderRightStyle`, `getBorderBottomStyle`, `borderStyles`). The four large public methods (`clearCellStyle`, `cellStyleUpdated`, `loadCustomBorderStylesToState`, `reloadActiveSheetCellStyles`) became orchestrators that delegate to the builder. `addCellStyleCSS` got rewired to call `fontStyleComposer.appendFontStyle` + `borderStyleBuilder.appendBorderStyles` + `defaultStyleBaseline.getDefaultTextAlign()`. `createHyperlinkCellStyle` (public) now reads through `defaultStyleBaseline.getDefaultFont()/getDefaultFontHeightInPoints()`. **Suite 306/0/0/4 green**, spotless + checkstyle clean. `SpreadsheetStyleFactory.java` shrunk from **1,107 → 495 LOC (−612, −55.3%)** — the biggest LOC win since slice 12. Honest flavor: **decomposition slice** in the truest sense. The 4 large public methods that used to be 60-100 lines each are now 10-25 lines each, leaving the builder to handle the cache-touching work. Two slice-16 learnings worth pinning: **(1) Cross-class package-private static-constant access** is fine. The new helper needed `SpreadsheetStyleFactory.BORDER` (a package-private static `Map`); accessed via the class-name-qualified reference. Worth noting because some readers worry that "package-private" means "no qualified access" — it doesn't, it just means "only same-package callers." **(2) Constructor-order constraint when helpers depend on each other or on parent-init side effects.** `borderStyleBuilder` needed the `colorConverter` field, which is set by `setupColorMap()` in the constructor. The three helper fields had to be initialized AFTER `setupColorMap()` runs — which means they can't be `final` if they're declared as fields with constructor-body assignment. Dropped `final` from the three helper fields. Worth noting in the pipeline so future operators don't fight the compiler. **(3) When the agent recommends N helpers but state-sharing argues for fewer, trust the read.** The Explore agent's report ranked cluster A (ShiftedBorderStyleBuilder) and cluster B (MergedCellBorderManager) as separate. But `doCellCustomStyling` writes to both clusters' state in one pass, and splitting it would force one helper to call into the other for half the work — an unclean dependency. I merged A + B into a single `CellBorderStyleBuilder` (607 LOC, larger than the typical helper but genuinely cohesive). Future agents should always be evaluated by what the *code* says, not just what the agent says.
- Slice 15 — **Sheet-state encapsulation: dimensions + grouping + CSS-style maps on `Spreadsheet.java`.** Three new package-private helpers: **`SheetDimensions`** (~102 LOC, `(Spreadsheet)` constructor; owns `defRowH` / `defColW` / `rowH[]` / `colW[]` / `hiddenColumnIndexes` / `hiddenRowIndexes` — the six fields describing per-sheet row / column geometry). **`SheetGrouping`** (~106 LOC; owns `colGroupingData` / `colGroupingMax` / `rowGroupingMax` / `colGroupingInversed` / `rowGroupingInversed` — five fields for outline / group state on each axis. Preserves the asymmetric `setRowGroupingData` which pushes directly to the element without caching — a quirk from the original code). **`SheetCssStyleMaps`** (~122 LOC; owns `rowIndexToStyleIndex` / `columnIndexToStyleIndex` / `lockedColumnIndexes` / `lockedRowIndexes` / `shiftedCellBorderStyles` / `conditionalFormattingStyles` — six client-facing CSS / style cache mirrors). 17 fields moved out, all getter / setter pairs on `Spreadsheet` became thin delegators. **Rule A footnote:** `cellStyleToCSSStyle` was `protected` on `Spreadsheet` (the only one of the 18 dimension / grouping / style fields that wasn't private), so it stays on the parent for downstream subclass compatibility — `SheetCssStyleMaps` documents the exclusion explicitly. **Suite 306/0/0/4 green**, spotless + checkstyle clean. `Spreadsheet.java` shrunk from **5,028 → 4,983 LOC (−45 LOC, −0.9%)**. **Same flavor as slice 14: structurally encapsulation, not decomposition.** Every moved setter was the uniform `this.X = X; getElement().setProperty(name, Serializer.serialize(X))` pattern. The 17 fields had no behavior beyond mirroring; the helpers grow by exactly what the parent loses. Cohesion is the win: Spreadsheet now exposes "dimension state" / "grouping state" / "CSS style state" as three named concerns rather than 17 independent fields. Future pipeline note: the `field = x; element.setProperty(...)` pattern is so uniform across ~30 setters on Spreadsheet that a single `MirroredProperty<T>` abstraction would eliminate the boilerplate everywhere — but that's an *abstraction*, not a refactor (would centralize a cross-cutting concern that's currently distributed), so saved for a separate "state mirroring" pass if the user wants to pursue it.
- Slice 13b — **Three helpers from `CellValueManager` for client-side cache decomposition.** Three new package-private helpers totaling ~360 LOC: **`ClientCellCache`** (~140 LOC; owns the two `sentCells` / `sentFormulaCells` sets that track what the client has cached; exposes `containsNormal` / `containsFormula` / `markNormalSent` / `markFormulaSent` / `dropSent` / `clear` for basic state plus the two prune operations `pruneRowsRange(int, int)` that returns `List<CellData>` removals and `pruneColumn(int)` for column-clear events; the prune operations match the `SpreadsheetUtil` cell-key format conventions). **`ClientFlushQueue`** (~97 LOC; owns the two pending-changes sets `markedCells` and `removedCells` plus the formula-change collector `changedFormulaCells`; methods `markForUpdate` / `isMarkedForUpdate` / `enqueueRemoval` / `addChangedFormulaCell` / `drainChangedFormulaCells` / `hasChangedFormulaCells` / `getRemovedCells` / `clear`). **`ClientCacheFlusher`** (~123 LOC, `(Spreadsheet, CellValueManager, ClientCellCache, ClientFlushQueue)` constructor; owns the previously 65-LOC `updateMarkedCellValues` body — iterates the sheet, builds the cell-data batch from formula cells and marked cells, fires the formula-change event if applicable, appends queued removals, pushes the batch via the RPC proxy, clears the queues). Removed from `CellValueManager`: 4 fields (`sentCells`, `sentFormulaCells`, `removedCells`, `markedCells`), 1 field (`changedFormulaCells`), the bodies of `updateMarkedCellValues` / `updateDeletedRowsInClientCache` / `clearCacheForColumn` (all 3 became thin delegators). Widened `fireFormulaValueChangeEvent` from `private` to package-private so the flusher can call it. **Suite 306/0/0/4 green** on first compile run — no false starts thanks to the grep-before-compile step from slice 13a's lesson. **CellValueManager.java** shrunk from **1,297 → 1,175 LOC (−122 LOC, −9.4%)** in this slice. **Cumulative slice 13** (13a + 13b on CellValueManager): **1,476 → 1,175 LOC (−301, −20.4%)** across 6 new helpers totaling ~727 LOC. Honest flavor note: this is a *decomposition* slice — the flusher carries real state-machine logic (formula re-evaluation gating, conditional-formatting marking, RPC drain) that previously sat inline in CellValueManager. The cache + queue helpers are thinner (mostly state holders), but the flusher's 65 LOC of logic represents genuine method-body extraction.
- Slice 14 — **First slice on `Spreadsheet.java` since slice 11, the "viewport encapsulation" set.** Three new package-private helpers: **`ViewportBounds`** (~70 LOC, no-arg constructor; owns the four 1-based indices `firstRow` / `lastRow` / `firstColumn` / `lastColumn` representing the cell range currently displayed; exposes `getFirstRow` / `getLastRow` / `getFirstColumn` / `getLastColumn` getters plus the change-detection predicate `hasChanged(int, int, int, int)` and the bulk-updater `update(int, int, int, int)`). **`ViewportBufferConfig`** (~52 LOC, `(Spreadsheet)` constructor; owns `rowBufferSize` and `columnBufferSize`, defaults 200/200; setters mirror the value into the corresponding `Element` property so the client gets the change). **`FreezePaneState`** (~80 LOC, `(Spreadsheet)` constructor; owns `verticalSplitPosition` / `horizontalSplitPosition` and the two per-pane scroll-position arrays; all four setters mirror to the `Element` property, the int[] setters go through `Serializer.serialize`). Removed from `Spreadsheet`: the 10 fields listed above. Public/protected API preserved as thin delegators: `getFirstRow` / `getLastRow` / `getFirstColumn` / `getLastColumn`, `getRowBufferSize` / `setRowBufferSize` / `getColBufferSize` / `setColBufferSize`, `getLastFrozenRow` / `getLastFrozenColumn` (via private `getVerticalSplitPosition` / `getHorizontalSplitPosition` delegators), package-private `setVerticalScrollPositions` / `setHorizontalScrollPositions` / `setVerticalSplitPosition` / `setHorizontalSplitPosition` / `getVerticalScrollPositions` / `getHorizontalScrollPositions`, plus the protected `onSheetScroll` (now built on `viewportBounds.hasChanged` + `viewportBounds.update`). Updated four indirect call sites that read the moved fields by name: a `firstColumn == -1` check, the `updateRowAndColumnRangeCellData(firstRow, firstColumn, lastRow, lastColumn)` calls in `reloadVisibleCellContents` and the shift-areas path, the `firstColumn = lastColumn = firstRow = lastRow = -1` viewport-reset in `reloadActiveSheetData`, and the four-pane visibility check in `isCellVisible`. **Suite 306/0/0/4 green**, spotless + checkstyle clean. `Spreadsheet.java` shrunk from **5,049 → 5,028 LOC (−21 LOC, −0.4%)**. **Honest learning: this is an *encapsulation* slice, not a *decomposition* slice.** The moved code was 10 thin setter wrappers (each `field = x; element.setProperty(name, x)` patterns) — there wasn't multi-step logic to redistribute. The LOC barely moves because the helpers grow by exactly what the parent loses (the new delegators occupy nearly the same line count as the originals). The win is **cohesion**: Spreadsheet has lost 10 independent state concerns; fields that always change together now live together. Added a new "Two flavors of slice — decomposition vs encapsulation" subsection to the pipeline (Rule #10) and to the `refactor-slice-plan` skill (Rule C) so future slice plans state which flavor they're doing, and so reviewers know which payoff to expect.
- Slice 13a — **Three helpers from `CellValueManager` (numeric formatting + column fit + deletion-handler guard).** First slice on `CellValueManager.java` (1,476 LOC, second-biggest unrefactored god class). Three new package-private helpers: **`CellNumericFormatter`** (~173 LOC, instance with no constructor args; owns the `formatter` POI `DataFormatter`, `originalValueDecimalFormat`, `localeDecimalSymbols`, and `cellValueFormatter` — the four fields that always reset together on `updateLocale`; exposes the formula-bar `getOriginalCellValue`, the formula-result oracle `getCachedFormulaCellValue`, and the two numeric predicates `valueContainsOnlyNumbers` and `isGeneralCell`). **`CellFitCalculator`** (~80 LOC, instance with no constructor args; owns the `cellStyleWidthRatioMap`, exposes `onWidthRatioUpdate`, `doesValueFit(Cell, String, int cellWidthPixels)`, and `getRatioForStyleIndex` — the last one feeds the scientific-notation branch of `createCellDataForCell` without leaking the map). **`CellDeletionGuard`** (~114 LOC, instance with `(CellValueManager)` constructor; holds three `passes*DeletionCheck` predicates plus the `isInRange` helper that decides whether a custom `CellDeletionHandler` claims responsibility for a delete-cells action). Note on the back-reference: `CellDeletionGuard` takes the parent rather than a 5-param constructor (`Spreadsheet`, `CellNumericFormatter`, `getFormulaEvaluator()`, `getConditionalFormattingEvaluator()`, `getCustomCellDeletionHandler()` — these all forward to the handler and would have to change in lockstep on every signature evolution). The parent-reference is fine here precisely because each method genuinely needs *all* those collaborators, and the slice is decomposition (Rule B) — the helper carries the deletion-decision logic out of the parent, not just the wiring. Removed from `CellValueManager`: the four fields, the `EXCEL_FORMULA_BAR_DECIMAL_FORMAT` constant (now lives in `CellNumericFormatter`), six private methods (`isGenerallCell`, `valueContainsOnlyNumbers`, `doesValueFit`, `selectedIsInRange`, both `passesDeletionCheck` overloads, `passesRangeDeletionCheck`), plus the `cellStyleWidthRatioMap` field. All public/protected API preserved as thin delegators (`getDataFormatter`, `setDataFormatter`, `getOriginalValueDecimalFormat`, `updateLocale`, `getOriginalCellValue`, `onCellStyleWidthRatioUpdate`, `getCellWidth`). **Suite 306/0/0/4 green**, spotless + checkstyle clean. `CellValueManager.java` shrunk from **1,476 → 1,297 LOC (−179, −12.1%)**. Two learnings worth flagging: (1) near-miss behavior change in the fit calculator — I initially wrote `cellStyleWidthRatioMap.putAll(ratios)` instead of `cellStyleWidthRatioMap = ratios` (replace vs merge); caught by re-reading the original on-the-fly. Added a new "Reassignment vs in-place mutation" check to `refactor-review` skill. (2) Missed grep — two stray `formatter.formatCellValue(...)` call sites in `CellValueManager` survived the field removal; first compile flagged them, but a pre-compile grep would have found them faster. Added a "grep before you compile" step to `refactor-slice-plan` skill.
- **Slice 12 cumulative result on `CellSelectionShifter.java`:** **890 → 243 LOC (−647 LOC, −72.7%)** across slices 12a/b/c. Five new package-private helpers totaling ~773 LOC of focused, single-purpose code (SequenceValueExtractor, SequenceIncrement, CellValueShifter, RangeSequenceDetector, DirectionalShiftOperator). What remains in `CellSelectionShifter` is exactly its public surface: the two paint methods (`onSelectionIncreasePainted`, `onSelectionDecreasePainted`), the protected `shiftCellValue` delegator (preserved for subclass override surface per rule #8), the private `fireCellValueChangeEvent` event-firing helper, and two one-line manager-accessor helpers. **29 new characterization tests** (15 `SequenceIncrement` + 14 `SequenceValueExtractor`) pin the pure-logic helpers. Suite 306/0/0/4 throughout, spotless + checkstyle clean throughout. **Cumulative god-class progress including slice 12:** `Spreadsheet.java` 6,224 → 5,049 LOC + `CellSelectionShifter.java` 890 → 243 LOC; total 1,822 LOC moved out into 39 focused helpers across slices 1-12.
- Slice 2 — **Cell Merge & Split decomposition.** Implemented the multi-class extraction pattern that rule #10 calls for. Four new package-private classes (~353 LOC of focused, single-purpose code), each describable in one sentence: **`MergedRegionLookup`** (read queries — implements the frozen `MergedRegionContainer` interface), **`MergedRegionCascade`** (style + cache-marker side effects on cells in/around a region), **`MergedRegionOperations`** (add/remove one region with validation + POI write + cascade orchestration), **`MergedRegionSynchronizer`** (bulk rebuild from POI state). The inline anonymous `MergedRegionContainer` instance in `Spreadsheet.java` (lines 1075–1115) was lifted into the new `MergedRegionLookup` file. The private helpers `createMergedRegionIntoSheet` and `deleteMergedRegion` were deleted from `Spreadsheet`; their logic now lives in `MergedRegionOperations`. The public `addMergedRegion(CellRangeAddress)`, `removeMergedRegion(int)`, and the private `updateMergedRegions()` became thin delegators. **No new public/protected surface on Spreadsheet:** the only visibility change was widening `getMergedRegions()` from `private` to package-private (no public API change — package-only). **Net LOC change:** `Spreadsheet.java` went from **6,224 → 6,081** (143 LOC removed from the god class). **Gates:** unit suite **277/0/0/4** (same 20 characterization + 2 payload tests, still green), spotless clean, checkstyle clean.
- Ugur pushed back on the scope: "refactoring `SpreadsheetHistoryManager` is not enough — we need to do the same to `Spreadsheet` and similar god classes." Reasonable: HistoryManager was 247 LOC; `Spreadsheet` is **6,224 LOC** and is the actual god class. The earlier methodology framed Spreadsheet as the target's host and the managers as the targets, which made Spreadsheet itself implicitly exempt from decomposition. Pipeline §1, §3 Stage 1, and §5 rewritten so the orchestrator is a first-class refactor target alongside the managers. Slice candidates now drawn from BOTH Spreadsheet AND each god-class-shaped manager (`CellValueManager` 1476, `SpreadsheetStyleFactory` 1107, `CellSelectionShifter` 890, `ConditionalFormatter` 883, `CellSelectionManager` 843, plus Spreadsheet itself). Next move: Stage-1 enumeration of Spreadsheet's distinct concerns to populate the slice-2 candidate list.
- Slice-1 attempt #2 — **actual decomposition of `SpreadsheetHistoryManager`'s logic into three smaller pieces.** The 247-LOC monolith conflates three concerns: (i) a bounded LinkedList with a cursor for undo/redo navigation, (ii) the side-effect application when a command is replayed (sheet swap, command.execute, selection change, value-change event firing), and (iii) the public-API surface. Decomposed into three classes: **`CommandStack`** (new, package-private, 130 LOC) — pure data structure that owns the command list + cursor + max-size; no Spreadsheet dependency, trivially unit-testable. **`CommandReplayer`** (new, package-private, 114 LOC) — takes a `Spreadsheet`, applies the side effects of replaying a command (sheet swap, selection change, value-change event); uses same-package access to `Spreadsheet`'s `fireEvent` (`protected`) and the `initialSheetSelection` field (`protected`). **`SpreadsheetHistoryManager`** (216 LOC, mostly Javadoc + delegation) — keeps every existing `public`/`protected` member (no API change) and becomes a thin coordinator. Protected fields (`historyIndex`, `commands`, `spreadsheet`) preserved as a read-only view of the stack's internal state for any subclasses; the `commands` LinkedList is shared by reference with the stack, and `historyIndex` is rewritten in lockstep after every stack mutation. Protected hook methods (`makeSureCorrectSheetActive`, `changeSelection`, `discardAllAfter`) preserved as thin delegators so subclass overrides still take effect (`applyCommand` calls through `this.makeSureCorrectSheetActive` rather than `replayer.makeSureCorrectSheetActive` for exactly this reason). **No new public or protected API anywhere; no changes to Spreadsheet's API surface; the `SpreadsheetHistoryHost` interface from attempt #1 is gone.** The 20 characterization tests + 2 payload tests still apply unchanged and pass against both attempt-#2 and the pre-decomposition tree (verified via `git stash` differential). **Final gates:** unit suite **277 / 0 / 0 / 4**, spotless clean, checkstyle clean. Slice 1 is now actually done.
